# AP-03 — Stomach Meridian (ST) — Foot Yangming (足阳明胃经)

> **Status:** Phase 2 — COMPLETE. 45/45 points. Review done 2026-08-16.
> Phase 1: ST36, ST25 (Tier A). Batch 1: ST1–ST4. Batch 2: ST5–ST8. Batch 3: ST9–ST12. Batch 4: ST13–ST18. Batch 5: ST19–ST24. Batch 6: ST26–ST30. Batch 7: ST31–ST35. Batch 8: ST37–ST39. Batch 9: ST40–ST42. Batch 10: ST43–ST45.
>
> **Meridian overview:** The Stomach channel (Foot Yangming) is the longest
> Yang meridian with 45 points, running from the infraorbital region (ST1)
> down the face, chest, abdomen, and anterior leg to the second toe (ST45).
> Paired with Spleen (Foot Taiyin). Peak time in Chinese clock: 07:00-09:00.
>
> **Total points:** 45 standard | Complete: 45/45

---

## ST36 — Túc Tam Lý (足三里, Zú Sān Lǐ)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis)**
The most extensively studied acupoint in the world. Molecular mechanism
identified (PROKR2, Liu 2021 Nature). Benchmark entry for the entire Atlas.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 3 cun below ST35 (Dubi), one
finger-breadth (approximately 1 cun) lateral to the anterior crest
of the tibia.

Anatomical landmarks: on the tibialis anterior muscle, between it and
the tendon of extensor digitorum longus. At the level of the tibial
tuberosity's inferior border.

Needle depth range: 25-38 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique
(adjusted for patient build).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Deep peroneal nerve (L4, L5) — motor branch to tibialis anterior
  (Schreiber, Chae & Chiang 2026: ST36 associated with motor branch
  along deep peroneal nerve in cadaveric dissection + electrostimulation)
- Lateral sural cutaneous nerve (superficial sensation)
- Spinal segment: L4-L5

Fascial plane: intermuscular connective tissue plane between tibialis
anterior and extensor digitorum longus. >80% acupoints at fascial
convergence planes (Langevin & Yandow 2002).
→ Cross-ref: A03 S3.4

Vascular: anterior tibial artery and vein (lateral to point, in
the anterior compartment).
→ Cross-ref: A04

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue
- Tibialis anterior muscle (primary)
- Interosseous membrane (deepest safe layer)

PROKR2+ neurons: abundant in hindlimb deep fascia and periosteum.
16.7% of lumbar DRG neurons express PROKR2 vs 6.1% thoracic
(Liu et al. 2021, Nature). These neurons innervate deep connective
tissue at this location.
→ Cross-ref: A02 S3.5

Receptor density: enriched local chemical milieu at acupoint
(adenosine, ATP, opioid receptors — Ma SX histological studies).
→ Cross-ref: A06 S1

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: **He-Sea (合穴)**, Earth element (Yang channel rule).
He-Sea points are where channel Qi converges deeply — used for
Fu organ disorders (Nan Jing). Earth on a Yang Earth channel =
horary point (Earth-of-Earth).
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Yuan-Source: No (ST42 Chongyang is Yuan-Source of Stomach)

Luo-Connecting: No (ST40 Fenglong is Luo of Stomach)

Xi-Cleft: No (ST34 Liangqiu is Xi-Cleft of Stomach)

Back-Shu: No (BL21 Weishu is Back-Shu of Stomach)

Front-Mu: No (CV12 Zhongwan is Front-Mu of Stomach)

Eight Confluent: No

Hui-Meeting: No

Special designations:
- **Command Point of the Abdomen** (四总穴: "Belly — Zusanli holds")
  One of the Four Command Points (Si Zong Xue) for body regions.
- **Ma Dan-yang Heavenly Star Point** (马丹阳天星十二穴)
  One of 12 points selected by Ma Dan-yang (Song Dynasty) as
  most clinically important.
- **Lower He-Sea of Stomach** (下合穴)
  Lower He-Sea points are where Fu organ Qi concentrates at the
  lower limbs (Lingshu Ch. 4). ST36 serves as both the regular
  He-Sea AND Lower He-Sea for Stomach — same point, dual function.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST36 Zusanli — He-Sea, Command Point of Abdomen,
Heavenly Star, Lower He-Sea of Stomach. Arguably the single most
important point in all of TCM. Referenced in every major classical text
from Neijing onward.

**2. Trigger point:** Tibialis anterior trigger point 1 (TrP1).
Referred pain pattern: anterolateral leg, dorsum of foot, great toe
(Travell & Simons). Dorsher 2008: anatomic correspondence confirmed
within the 93.3% overall overlap dataset. The trigger point and
acupoint share the same motor endplate zone in tibialis anterior.
→ Cross-ref: B11 S3.3

**3. Ashi correspondence:** Common tender point at anterolateral
upper leg in patients with GI dysfunction or knee pain. Palpation
frequently reveals taut band in tibialis anterior at this level.

**4. Marma point:** Janu Marma (knee region, 107-point Ayurvedic
system, Frawley). Approximate correspondence — Janu is a broader
knee zone, ST36 is inferior to it. Possible correspondence within
2-3 cm depending on Marma delineation used.

**5. Head zone:** **DIVERGENCE.** ST36 is at L4-L5 dermatome. Stomach
viscerotome is T6-T9. There is NO segmental convergence between ST36's
spinal level and the stomach's autonomic innervation. This is the
defining non-segmental gap.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S8 Q1

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line (Myers 2001). Tibialis
anterior is a key station on this myofascial meridian. Dorsher 2009:
Superficial Front Line substantially overlaps Stomach meridian pathway.
→ Cross-ref: A09 S1.3

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** NOT directly predicted by Kim 2017
model for GI pathology. Neurogenic spots for GI disease would appear at
T6-T9 dermatome (abdominal skin), not at L4-L5 (leg). This confirms
ST36's GI effects operate through a non-segmental supraspinal pathway,
not through segmental convergence.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S4.3, C04 S3.2.3

**8. Cupping point:** Not a standard cupping point. Limb location is
not typical for cupping application (primarily back/shoulder in both
TCM and Sunnah traditions).

**Convergence score: 5/8**
(TCM, Trigger Point, Ashi, Marma approximate, Anatomy Trains)

The Head zone DIVERGENCE is not a weakness — it is a critically
important datapoint revealing that ST36's mechanism is non-segmental.

### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing Lingshu (Ch. 4, Ben Shu): listed as Lower He-Sea of Stomach,
  primary point for treating Stomach Fu disorders. "When disease is in
  the Stomach, select Zusanli."
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing (Huangfu Mi, ~260 CE): cold in the intestines with
  distention and fullness, frequent belching, aversion to the smell of
  food, insufficiency of stomach Qi, borborygmus, abdominal pain,
  diarrhea of untransformed food.
- Nanjing (Ch. 68): He-Sea points treat conditions of reversed Qi
  and diarrhea — ST36 as He-Sea of Stomach is primary for these.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng (Yang Jizhou, 1601): comprehensive — GI disorders,
  general weakness, immune function, mental disorders, lower limb
  conditions. "If one wants to remain well, keep Zusanli perpetually
  warm" (常灸足三里) — longevity moxibustion tradition.

Nguồn hiện đại:
- Deadman (Manual of Acupuncture): gastric pain, vomiting, abdominal
  distention, indigestion, borborygmus, diarrhea, constipation,
  dysentery, dizziness, mental disorders, hemiplegia, beriberi, knee
  pain, breast abscess, edema, urinary difficulty.
- WHO 2008: recognized for gastric conditions, knee pain, general
  tonification.

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- Most-cited single point across ALL classical texts
- "Four seas" concept (Lingshu Ch. 33): ST36 = He point of the Sea of
  Nourishment (水谷之海)
- Tonification tradition: daily moxa at ST36 for health maintenance is
  the most widely practiced preventive acupuncture technique in East Asian
  medical history
- Combined most frequently with: CV12 (Front-Mu of Stomach), SP6
  (three Yin crossing), LI4 (Command Point of face/Yang pairing),
  BL21 (Back-Shu of Stomach)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Systemic anti-inflammatory effect**
Confidence: 🟢 Confirmed

Liu et al. 2021 (Nature): low-intensity EA (0.5 mA) at ST36 →
PROKR2+ sensory neurons in deep fascia → vagal afferents → brainstem
NTS/DMVN → adrenal catecholamine release → anti-inflammatory.
TNF-α reduced ~50%, IL-6 reduced ~50% in endotoxemia model.
PROKR2 ablation completely abolished effect.

Lai et al. 2020 (Evidence-Based Complementary and Alternative Medicine):
systematic review of 54 studies — acupuncture at ST36 for experimental
sepsis. Reduces oxidative stress, inflammation, microcirculatory
disturbance. Maintains immune balance across multiple organ systems.

Heliyon 2024 (comprehensive landscape review): 10 years of PubMed
literature. ST36 stimulation has therapeutic effects across respiratory,
neurological, digestive, endocrine, and immune system diseases. Most
prominent function: regulating inflammatory state via MAPK signaling
pathway, involving macrophages, T-lymphocytes, mast cells, neuroglial
cells.

Sham comparison: verum EA at ST36 > sham in anti-inflammatory outcomes.
Location matters — abdominal stimulation (ST25) drives DIFFERENT
(sympathetic) pathway.
→ Cross-ref: A02 S3.5, A05

**2. Gastrointestinal motility**
Confidence: 🟢 Confirmed

Mechanism: EA at ST36 → NTS/DMVN brainstem relay → vagal efferent →
gastric motility increase. NMDA receptor-dependent synaptic activity in
DMVN mediates the effect (PMC3478787). During EA, vagus nerve discharge
frequency > sympathetic discharge frequency = net vagal-dominant
response.

Clinical: accelerated gastric emptying in functional dyspepsia and
diabetic gastroparesis. EA at ST36 improved delayed gastric emptying
in STZ-induced diabetic rats and rescued interstitial cells of Cajal
(ICC) networks (PMC3877115).

2026 data mining study (Frontiers in Medicine): ST36 + CV12 identified
as core acupoint combination for functional dyspepsia — validated
from data mining through experimental confirmation.

Sham: verum > no treatment for GI motility effects.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6 (somatovisceral reflexes)

**3. Chronic pain (analgesic)**
Confidence: 🟢 Confirmed

Vickers et al. 2012/2018 (JAMA, Journal of Pain): ATC individual
patient data meta-analysis, 39 RCTs, 20,827 patients. Acupuncture >
sham (effect size 0.15-0.23 SD) and > no treatment (0.44-0.63 SD) for
chronic pain (p<.001). ST36 is among the most commonly included points
in chronic pain protocols.

Goldman et al. 2010 (Nature Neuroscience): acupuncture needling at ST36
produces 24-fold increase in local adenosine concentration. Analgesic
effect mediated by adenosine A1 receptor. Absent in A1 receptor
knockout mice. Inhibiting adenosine degradation enzymes potentiates
both adenosine increase and analgesia.

Han Ji-Sheng EA frequency-opioid mapping: 2 Hz EA → enkephalin and
β-endorphin release; 100 Hz → dynorphin release. Mechanism blocked
by naloxone = opioid-mediated analgesia confirmed.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S2.4, A06 S3

**4. Brain activation patterns**
Confidence: 🟡 Plausible

2022 systematic review/meta-analysis (Frontiers in Neurology): 16
task-based fMRI studies, 401 healthy subjects. Acupuncture at ST36
consistently activates: right inferior frontal gyrus (opercular),
left superior temporal gyrus, right median cingulate/paracingulate
gyri. Pattern partially overlaps with interoceptive processing networks.

2023 coordinate-based meta-analysis (Frontiers in Neuroscience):
confirmed consistent brain response pattern to ST36 across studies.

2025 (Frontiers in Neuroscience): ST36 vs GB34 (anatomically adjacent,
overlapping L4-S2 dermatomes, DIFFERENT meridians). Result: distinct
brain networks. ST36 → visual network, correlated with noradrenaline/
acetylcholine receptor maps. GB34 → subcortical/basal ganglia,
correlated with dopamine/serotonin. = Some specificity BEYOND simple
somatotopy.
→ Cross-ref: A09 S3.4

**5. Immunomodulation**
Confidence: 🟡 Plausible

Heliyon 2024: MAPK pathway as prominent regulatory mechanism.
Affects macrophages, T-lymphocytes, mast cells, neuroglial cells
in target organ tissues. Multi-system, multi-target regulatory
mechanism established in animal models.

Cholinergic anti-inflammatory pathway: EA at ST36 activates vagal
efferents → acetylcholine release → α7nAChR on macrophages →
NF-κB inhibition → reduced pro-inflammatory cytokines.
→ Cross-ref: A02 S4.3, A05 S3.2

Limited human RCT data for immune-specific outcomes. Animal evidence
strong but translation to clinical immunotherapy unconfirmed.

**6. General tonification / longevity**
Confidence: 🔴 Unvalidated

Traditional claim: daily moxibustion at ST36 prevents disease and
extends life. No controlled studies for preventive health or longevity
in humans. Some animal studies suggest repeated stimulation enhances
immune parameters, but these do not validate the longevity claim as
stated in classical texts.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

Disease-state sensitization: GI pathology may increase sensitivity at
ST36 region, but neurogenic spots for GI disease would appear at T6-T9
dermatome (abdominal skin), NOT at L4-L5 (Kim 2017 model). ST36's GI
effects do not operate through the neurogenic inflammation pathway but
through the PROKR2-vagal supraspinal pathway.

The non-segmental nature of ST36 means it does NOT follow the dynamic
acupoint model described for segmentally-linked points. ST36 may still
show local tissue changes (trigger point activation in tibialis anterior
under leg use/strain), but these would relate to its musculoskeletal
function, not its GI function.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST36:**

A01/A02 (Neural — PRIMARY):
- Deep peroneal nerve (L4-L5) → dorsal root ganglion → spinal cord
- PROKR2+ sensory neurons in deep fascia/periosteum (Liu 2021 Nature):
  LOW-intensity EA → PROKR2 → vagal afferent → NTS/DMVN brainstem →
  adrenal catecholamine release → systemic anti-inflammatory
- HIGH-intensity EA → spinal sympathetic pathway (PROKR2-independent,
  Liu 2020 Neuron) = different mechanism at same point
- Adenosine mechanism (Goldman 2010): needle manipulation → 24× adenosine
  increase → A1 receptor → local analgesia

A03 (Fascia):
- Located at intermuscular fascial plane (tibialis anterior / extensor
  digitorum longus). Needle rotation → collagen winding → mechanical
  stretch → fibroblast activation → ATP release → adenosine → A1
  receptor → analgesia (Langevin needle-grasp chain).

A05 (Immune):
- Local mast cell degranulation at needling site
- Systemic: cholinergic anti-inflammatory pathway via vagal activation

A06 (Chemical):
- Local adenosine (Goldman 2010: 24-fold increase)
- EA frequency-dependent opioid release (Han): 2 Hz → enkephalin/
  β-endorphin; 100 Hz → dynorphin
- Enriched receptor milieu at acupoint (Ma SX)

A07 (Segmental):
- L4-L5 segment — relevant for LOCAL effects (knee pain, lower leg)
- NOT relevant for GI effects (stomach viscerotome = T6-T9)
- The non-segmental gap is RESOLVED by PROKR2-vagal pathway (A02 S3.5)

A08 (Muscle):
- Tibialis anterior motor endplate zone → trigger point mechanism
- Local twitch response obtainable with deep needling

**WHY this location produces GI effects (the central question):**

ST36 is at L4-L5 dermatome. Stomach is at T6-T9 viscerotome.
Segmental convergence does NOT explain the gastric effect.

Resolution (Liu 2021):
1. PROKR2+ sensory neurons are abundant in hindlimb deep fascia
   (16.7% lumbar DRG) but sparse in thoracic fascia (6.1%)
2. Low-intensity stimulation activates PROKR2+ neurons selectively
3. These neurons project via DRG → spinal cord → brainstem NTS/DMVN
4. DMVN → vagal efferent → gastric smooth muscle → motility increase
5. = Supraspinal pathway with brainstem relay, molecularly defined
6. Ablation of PROKR2 neurons eliminates the entire pathway

This is the STRONGEST explanation for any non-segmental acupoint
effect in the entire BSA framework. It does not require meridian
channels or Qi flow — it requires specific neurons in specific
anatomical locations projecting to specific brainstem nuclei.

**Gap:** Traditional indications extend far beyond GI and anti-
inflammatory effects. PROKR2-vagal pathway explains the most-studied
effects but not all classical claims (mental disorders, edema, breast
abscess, etc.). Multiple parallel mechanisms likely operate at this
single point. Systematic mapping of ALL pathways activated at ST36
remains incomplete.

⚡ Framework note: ST36 non-segmental mechanism (PROKR2→vagal-adrenal)
resolves the primary gap identified in A07 S8 Q1 and A09 S4.3 for this
specific point. The resolution is POINT-SPECIFIC — other non-segmental
acupoint effects (e.g., LI4→face, PC6→nausea) require their own
pathway identification. A09 S4.3 should note that the 30-40% non-
segmental gap is partially closed.

⚡ Framework note: ST36 vs GB34 brain activation data (2025, Frontiers
in Neuroscience) extends A09 S3.4 fMRI analysis. Adjacent points on
different meridians show distinct brain networks correlated with
different neurotransmitter receptor maps — suggesting specificity
BEYOND somatotopy, potentially at the neurochemical level. This is
new evidence not captured in A09's current fMRI assessment.

⚡ Framework note: Goldman 2010 adenosine mechanism operates at a
DIFFERENT level than PROKR2 pathway. PROKR2 = specific neurons for
specific autonomic effects (anti-inflammatory, GI). Adenosine = local
tissue-level analgesia from needle manipulation itself. Both operate
simultaneously at ST36. This supports the multi-system convergence
zone model (A09 S2.2) — multiple independent mechanisms at one site.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

**Châm (B01):**
Needle depth: 25-38 mm perpendicular. Adjusted for patient build
(thinner patients: 20-25 mm; muscular: up to 40 mm).
De Qi characteristics: typically strong — aching (suan), heaviness
(zhong), distension (zhang) radiating distally along anterior leg.
Needle grasp commonly felt by practitioner.
Schreiber et al. 2026: target = motor branch of deep peroneal nerve
to tibialis anterior. Cadaveric dissection + electrostimulation
confirmed neuroanatomical target.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S2.2, S2.3

**EA (B01):**
Han frequency-opioid mapping:
- 2 Hz → met-enkephalin + β-endorphin (μ/δ opioid receptors)
- 100 Hz → dynorphin (κ opioid receptor)
- 2/100 Hz alternating → all three opioids (broad analgesia)
PROKR2 activation: LOW-intensity (0.5 mA) critical. High-intensity
(3.0 mA) switches from vagal to sympathetic pathway at same point.
Dose-response: intensity determines WHICH pathway, not just magnitude.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S2.4

**Bấm huyệt (B02):**
Pressure at ST36 for GI symptoms — commonly used in self-care
protocols (nausea, bloating, fatigue).
PROKR2 depth question: UNRESOLVED. PROKR2+ neurons are in DEEP
fascia/periosteum (~20-25 mm below skin). Surface thumb pressure
reaches ~10-15 mm. Whether sufficient force reaches PROKR2 layer
is unknown. Acupressure effects at ST36 may operate through
different mechanisms (superficial nerve endings, fascial stretch)
rather than PROKR2 activation.
→ Cross-ref: B02 S3.1, C04 S3.2.1

**Cứu (B08):**
Traditionally favored technique at ST36 — "daily moxa for longevity."
Method: indirect moxa (moxa stick 2-3 cm above skin) or direct
(small cone on ginger/garlic slice). Thermal stimulation → TRPV1
activation at acupoint (higher TRPV1/nNOS density at acupoints,
Ma SX). Same vagal-GI endpoint as EA but via thermal afferent entry.
Heat-sensitive moxibustion (Chen Rixin): some points show differential
thermal reactivity — ST36 data available but not definitive.
→ Cross-ref: B08 S2.1, S5.1

**Dry needling (B11):**
Target: tibialis anterior TrP1. Taut band at motor endplate zone.
Local twitch response (LTR) expected and sought. Technique overlaps
with acupuncture at same location — distinction is theoretical
framework and clinical reasoning, not anatomy.
→ Cross-ref: B11 S3.3

**So sánh:**
EA most studied, strongest evidence base for systemic effects
(anti-inflammatory, GI motility). Manual acupuncture most widely
used clinically. Moxibustion traditionally preferred for
tonification/prevention. No head-to-head comparison adequately
powered to determine which technique is most effective at ST36
for specific indications.

### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: None — lower leg location, no thoracic cavity
proximity.

Vascular risk: Anterior tibial artery and vein are lateral and deep
to the standard point location. Avoid deep medial insertion directed
toward the interosseous membrane. Superficial veins may be visible —
standard precaution.

Pregnancy: No contraindication. ST36 is NOT among the traditionally
contraindicated points during pregnancy (unlike LI4, SP6, BL67,
sacral points).

Special restriction: None. One of the safest points for all
techniques (needling, moxa, pressure, EA).

Caution: In very thin patients, deep perpendicular needling may reach
interosseous membrane → posterior tibial compartment. Maintain
awareness of depth. Standard clinical competence sufficient.

---

## ST25 — Thiên Xu (天枢, Tiān Shū)

**Evidence tier: A (Deep Analysis)**
Front-Mu point of Large Intestine. Critical for the PROKR2 intensity-
dependent pathway comparison with ST36 (Liu 2020 Neuron).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 2 cun lateral to the center of
the umbilicus, on the rectus abdominis muscle.

Anatomical landmarks: at the level of the navel, lateral to the
linea alba, on the belly of the rectus abdominis. Bilateral point.

Needle depth range: 15-25 mm perpendicular (caution: peritoneal
cavity beneath rectus abdominis in thin patients).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Anterior cutaneous branch of the 10th intercostal nerve (T10)
- Spinal segment: T10

Fascial plane: anterior abdominal wall fascia. Multiple layers —
anterior rectus sheath → rectus abdominis → posterior rectus sheath
→ transversalis fascia → peritoneum.
→ Cross-ref: A03

Vascular: branches of the superior and inferior epigastric arteries
and veins. Superficial: subcutaneous venous network.

Muscle layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous fat (variable thickness)
- Anterior rectus sheath
- Rectus abdominis muscle
- Posterior rectus sheath (above arcuate line)
- Transversalis fascia
- Peritoneum (deepest — NOT to be penetrated)

Spinal segment: T10 — within the large intestine's viscerotome
(T10-L1). = SEGMENTAL correspondence (unlike ST36 which is non-
segmental for GI effects).
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: No (Five Shu points are distal to elbow/knee only)

Yuan-Source: No

Luo-Connecting: No

Xi-Cleft: No

Back-Shu: No (BL25 Dachangshu is Back-Shu of Large Intestine)

**Front-Mu: YES — Front-Mu point of the Large Intestine (大肠募穴)**
Located on the Stomach channel but serves as alarm/collection point
for Large Intestine. Front-Mu points are where organ Qi concentrates
on the anterior body surface. Used for acute conditions and excess
patterns of the organ.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S3.1

Eight Confluent: No

Hui-Meeting: No

Special: No additional designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST25 Tianshu — Front-Mu of Large Intestine.
"Celestial Pivot" (Tian = heaven, Shu = pivot) — named for its
position at the navel level, the "pivot" between upper and lower
body. Primary point for all large intestine disorders.

**2. Trigger point:** Rectus abdominis trigger points. Abdominal wall
trigger points commonly found in the periumbilical region (Travell &
Simons). Referred pain to same abdominal quadrant. Dorsher: trigger
point correspondence confirmed.
→ Cross-ref: B11 S3.3

**3. Ashi correspondence:** Peri-umbilical tenderness is a common
clinical finding in IBS, constipation, and other GI disorders.
Palpation frequently reveals tension in rectus abdominis at this level.

**4. Marma point:** Nabhi Marma (navel region, 107-point Ayurvedic
system). Direct correspondence — both systems identify the
periumbilical region as diagnostically and therapeutically significant
for GI function.

**5. Head zone:** T10 dermatome = within large intestine viscerotome
(T10-L1). CONVERGENCE. ST25 is segmentally linked to the large
intestine. Beissner 2011: Front-Mu points correspond to Head's
maximum points.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S4.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line (Myers). Rectus abdominis
is a major station on this line. ST25 is within the abdominal portion
of the Superficial Front Line.

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** Would be predicted by Kim 2017
model for large intestine pathology. T10-L1 dermatome matches. Colitis
rats showed neurogenic spots at T10-L1 in Kim 2017 experiment —
consistent with ST25 location.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S4.3, C04 S3.2.3

**8. Cupping point:** Abdominal cupping at the periumbilical region is
used in some TCM protocols, though less common than back cupping.

**Convergence score: 7/8**
(TCM, Trigger Point, Ashi, Marma, Head Zone, Anatomy Trains,
Neurogenic Spot)

Among the highest convergence scores possible. Only cupping is marginal.
The segmental correspondence (T10) is direct and strong — contrasting
with ST36's non-segmental nature.

### Chỉ định truyền thống (DATA — ghi nhận, không endorse as mechanism)

Nguồn cổ:
- Neijing: named as key point for regulating the intestines and
  the transition between upper and lower body
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: abdominal distention, borborygmus, diarrhea,
  constipation, pain around the navel, dysentery
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: all intestinal disorders, menstrual irregularity,
  edema

Nguồn hiện đại:
- Deadman: abdominal pain, abdominal distention, borborygmus,
  diarrhea, dysentery, constipation, intestinal abscess, irregular
  menstruation, edema
- WHO 2008: recognized for intestinal conditions

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng nổi bật:
- Primary point for ALL large intestine disorders
- Commonly combined with: BL25 (Back-Shu of Large Intestine, Shu-Mu
  combination), ST36 (He-Sea for GI support), SP15 (adjacent point)
- In modern protocols: IBS, chronic constipation, ulcerative colitis

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**1. Intensity-dependent pathway segregation**
Confidence: 🟢 Confirmed

Liu et al. 2020 (Neuron): HIGH-intensity EA (3.0 mA) at ST25 →
spinal sympathetic pathway → catecholamine release via DIFFERENT
mechanism than ST36. This is PROKR2-independent. The abdominal
location (T10) drives sympathetic spinal reflex, NOT the PROKR2→vagal
supraspinal pathway seen at ST36.

Key distinction: SAME needle technique, DIFFERENT body location =
DIFFERENT autonomic pathway activated. ST36 (limb) → vagal.
ST25 (abdomen) → sympathetic. = Location determines pathway.
→ Cross-ref: A02 S3.5

**2. IBS / functional GI disorders**
Confidence: 🟡 Plausible

Multiple RCTs include ST25 in protocols for IBS, functional
constipation, and functional dyspepsia. ST25 is among the most
commonly selected points for GI research protocols. However, most
studies use multi-point protocols — isolating ST25's individual
contribution is difficult.

Segmental mechanism: T10 intercostal nerve → spinal cord T10 →
somatovisceral reflex → large intestine. Direct segmental
correspondence supports the traditional indication.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S1.6

**3. Constipation**
Confidence: 🟡 Plausible

Several RCTs show EA at ST25 (often bilateral, combined with other
points) improves bowel frequency and stool consistency in chronic
constipation. Mechanism: local somatovisceral reflex via T10 segment
+ possible direct abdominal wall stimulation affecting gut motility.

Sham comparison: evidence mixed for ST25-specific superiority vs
sham at nearby non-acupoints. Suggests a significant segmental
component (any T10 stimulation may produce some effect) with
possible additional benefit from the specific multi-system
convergence at ST25.

**4. Colonic motility (animal)**
Confidence: 🟡 Plausible

Animal studies demonstrate EA at ST25 modulates colonic motility.
Disease-state-dependent effects (Liu 2020 Neuron): anti-inflammatory
pathway activated differs by disease state, not just by intensity.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

Disease-state sensitization: neurogenic inflammatory spots at T10-L1
predicted by Kim 2017 for large intestine pathology. Colitis rat model
confirmed spots at T10-L1 = consistent with ST25 location. The dynamic
acupoint model APPLIES to ST25 (segmental point) in a way it does NOT
apply to ST36 (non-segmental point).

This creates an important contrast: ST25 follows the standard
segmental-convergence + neurogenic-inflammation model. ST36 operates
through a completely different (PROKR2-supraspinal) pathway. Both are
on the same meridian, both treat GI disorders, but through fundamentally
different mechanisms.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST25:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- T10 intercostal nerve → spinal cord → segmental somatovisceral
  reflex → large intestine modulation
- HIGH-intensity EA → spinal sympathetic pathway (Liu 2020 Neuron).
  This is PROKR2-independent — abdominal fascia has lower PROKR2
  density (6.1% thoracic DRG)
- Abdominal stimulation → segmental sympathetic reflex → can INHIBIT
  GI motility (Sato 1997) — opposite direction from ST36's vagal-
  mediated GI FACILITATION

A03 (Fascia):
- Multiple fascial layers at anterior abdominal wall provide
  mechanotransduction substrate

A05 (Immune):
- Local periumbilical immune tissue interaction possible
- Proximity to gut-associated lymphoid tissue (GALT)

A07 (Segmental — PRIMARY for ST25):
- T10 dermatome = T10 viscerotome for large intestine
- Direct segmental convergence at dorsal horn WDR neurons
- Beissner 2011: Front-Mu points = Head zone maximum points

**WHY this location produces GI effects:**
Unlike ST36, the answer for ST25 is straightforward segmental
convergence: T10 skin stimulation → T10 spinal cord → T10 autonomic
outflow → large intestine. No non-segmental gap to resolve.

**Contrast with ST36:**
```
ST25 (abdomen, T10):  segmental → sympathetic → GI inhibition
ST36 (leg, L4-L5):    non-segmental → PROKR2 → vagal → GI facilitation

Same meridian. Same organ target. Opposite neural pathways.
Opposite autonomic effects. Location determines everything.
```
This contrast is one of the strongest demonstrations that location
determines mechanism in acupuncture — validating C04's central thesis.

⚡ Framework note: ST25 vs ST36 contrast within the SAME meridian
demonstrates that traditional meridian organization (both are
"Stomach channel points") does NOT predict mechanism. Two points on
the same meridian use completely different neural pathways (segmental
vs supraspinal) to reach the same target organ, with potentially
opposite effects. This strengthens A09 S3.1's fundamental asymmetry:
points are real and mechanistically distinct; the connecting meridian
pathway does not explain WHY they work.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích + dose-response

**Châm (B01):**
Needle depth: 15-25 mm perpendicular. CRITICAL depth awareness —
peritoneal cavity lies deep to posterior rectus sheath. Obese patients
tolerate deeper needling; thin patients require shallower depth.
De Qi: distension and fullness around umbilicus, sometimes radiating
laterally.

**EA (B01):**
Bilateral ST25 commonly used as EA pair (positive-negative electrodes
on left and right ST25). Liu 2020 Neuron: high-intensity (3.0 mA)
for sympathetic pathway activation. Frequency parameters follow Han
mapping.
Dose-response: intensity determines pathway — low → weak/no effect;
high → sympathetic activation. Reverse pattern from ST36 (where LOW
intensity activates PROKR2-vagal). This dose-response reversal is
location-dependent.

**Bấm huyệt (B02):**
Periumbilical pressure commonly used for bloating, constipation.
Shallow target (rectus abdominis) = more accessible to surface
pressure than ST36's deep fascia target.

**Cứu (B08):**
Moxa at ST25 for chronic diarrhea, cold-pattern intestinal disorders
(traditional). Indirect moxa or moxa box over bilateral ST25 + CV8
(navel, moxa-only point).

### An toàn

Pneumothorax risk: None — abdominal location, no thoracic cavity.

**Peritoneal puncture risk:** Primary safety concern. Deep perpendicular
needling can penetrate posterior rectus sheath → transversalis fascia →
peritoneum → peritoneal cavity. Risk especially in:
- Thin patients with minimal subcutaneous fat
- Patients with abdominal surgery (adhesions alter anatomy)
- Pregnant patients (CONTRAINDICATED for deep needling)

Max safe depth: individualized. Experienced practitioners assess tissue
layers during insertion. Standard guideline: needle perpendicular to
the depth of the posterior rectus sheath, no further.

Pregnancy: **CAUTION** — deep needling contraindicated during pregnancy
(proximity to uterus, peritoneal cavity). Superficial needling or
acupressure may be used with caution in early pregnancy; avoided in
late pregnancy.

Vascular risk: Superior/inferior epigastric vessels — usually not
problematic at standard depth. Superficial veins visible in some
patients.

---

## ST1 — Thừa Khấp (承泣, Chéng Qì)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Contains Tears" (承 = receive/contain, 泣 = tears/weeping)
— on the infraorbital margin, directly below the pupil, between
the eyeball and the lower orbital rim. The name references the
point's location where tears collect and its classical use for
lacrimation disorders. ST1 is the **STARTING POINT (起穴)** of
the Stomach channel — receiving the Yangming baton from LI20
(Yingxiang) at the contralateral nostril. The LI→ST transition
is the ONLY contralateral same-name crossing in the 12-channel
cycle: LI20 on the right side connects to ST1 on the LEFT (and
vice versa), via a midline crossing at the philtrum/GV26 area
(Ling Shu: 左之右，右之左).

ST1 is a **MEETING POINT of three vessels**: Foot Yangming (ST),
Ren Mai (CV), and Yang Qiao Mai (阳跷脉). Yang Qiao Mai
connects BL1 (medial canthus) → ST1 (infraorbital) → ST4
(mouth corner) — tracing a periorbital-to-oral arc along the
Yangming face. This is the first of TWO Yang Qiao Mai–ST
meetings in the first 4 points (ST4 is the second).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the face, directly below
the pupil, on the infraorbital margin, between the eyeball and
the inferior orbital ridge.

Anatomical landmarks: with the patient gazing forward, the point
is directly below the center of the pupil, at the lower border
of the orbital rim (infraorbital margin). The depression between
the eyeball above and the bony rim below is palpable.

Spatial relationships:
- LI20 (Yingxiang) is inferior and medial — at the nasolabial
  sulcus beside the nostril. The preceding channel's terminal
  point (contralateral).
- ST2 (Sibai) is directly below — at the infraorbital foramen,
  ~1 cm inferior.
- GB1 (Tongziliao) is at the lateral orbital rim — ST1 and GB1
  represent INFERIOR vs LATERAL orbital approaches.
- BL1 (Jingming, future) is at the medial canthus — three Yang
  same-name transitions arrive at three different orbital
  positions.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm subcutaneous, directed inferiorly along
the orbital floor or laterally along the infraorbital margin.
NEVER insert deeply toward the orbit. Push the eyeball gently
superiorly and insert along the inferior orbital rim.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Infraorbital nerve (CN V2):** terminal branches ascending
  from the infraorbital foramen (~1 cm below, at ST2). These
  branches supply sensory innervation to the lower eyelid,
  lateral nose, and upper lip. At ST1, these are ascending
  terminal branches reaching the orbital margin.
- **Inferior palpebral branches (CN V2):** specifically supply
  the lower eyelid and infraorbital skin.
- **Zygomatic and buccal branches of facial nerve (CN VII):**
  motor to orbicularis oculi (inferior orbital fibers) — the
  muscle encircling the eye. Dysfunction → incomplete eye
  closure in facial paralysis.
- Spinal segments: N/A — cranial nerve territory (CN V2, CN VII)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular:
- Infraorbital artery (branch of maxillary artery, exits with
  the nerve at the infraorbital foramen)
- Inferior palpebral arteries (branches of ophthalmic artery)
- Angular artery (terminal facial artery) ascending nearby
→ Cross-ref: A04

Tissue layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin (thin, periorbital — bruises easily)
- Orbicularis oculi (palpebral and orbital portions)
- Orbital septum (barrier between preseptal and postseptal)
- Orbital fat (deep — WITHIN the orbit, NOT to be penetrated)

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft, Back-Shu,
Front-Mu, Eight Confluent, or Hui-Meeting designation.

**Channel starting point (起穴):** ST1 is the first point on
the Stomach channel. It receives qi flow from **LI20
(Yingxiang)** at the contralateral nostril — the **Hand Yangming
(LI) → Foot Yangming (ST)** same-name transition. This
completes the documentation of the Yangming same-name transition
from BOTH sides (sender: LI20 in AP-02 Obs. 29, receiver: ST1
here).

With ST1 documented, ALL THREE Yang same-name face transitions
are now recorded from both sender and receiver:

| Same-name Pair | Sender (止穴) | Receiver (起穴) | Location | Crossing |
|---------------|-------------|----------------|----------|----------|
| Yangming | LI20 (AP-02) | **ST1 (here)** | **Nose → infraorbital** | **Contralateral** |
| Taiyang | SI19 (AP-06) | BL1 (future) | Ear → medial eye | Ipsilateral |
| Shaoyang | TE23 (AP-10) | GB1 (AP-11) | Lateral brow → lateral eye | Ipsilateral |

The Yangming transition is architecturally unique: (1) the ONLY
contralateral crossing, (2) the LARGEST spatial gap between
sender and receiver (~3 cm nose-to-orbit vs ~1 cm for Shaoyang),
(3) the only transition crossing a midline structure (philtrum/
GV26).
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 Obs. 29, AP-10 Obs. 13, B01 S3.1

**Meeting point (交会穴):** meeting of Foot Yangming (ST), Ren
Mai (CV), and Yang Qiao Mai (阳跷脉). Yang Qiao Mai's facial
trajectory connects BL1 → ST1 → ST4 — linking medial canthus →
infraorbital → oral commissure, tracing the periorbital motor
nerve territory (CN VII zygomatic and buccal branches).

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST1 Chengqi (承泣). Functions: brightens
the eyes (明目), clears Wind-Heat from the eye, stops
lacrimation (止泪). Primary indications: conjunctivitis, eye
redness, lacrimation, night blindness, myopia, optic atrophy,
facial paralysis (lower eyelid), blepharospasm.

**2. Trigger point:** inferior orbicularis oculi trigger point
— lower orbital portion of the muscle. Referred symptoms: eyelid
twitching (fasciculation), pressure sensation around the eye.
Moderate correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in eye strain (prolonged
screen use, near work), acute conjunctivitis, and frontal
headache. The infraorbital margin becomes pressure-sensitive
during periorbital tension and sinus congestion. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no strong correspondence. Apanga Marma is at
the lateral canthus (GB1 correspondence). No named Marma
specifically targets the infraorbital ridge. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** CN V2 (maxillary) dermatome at the
infraorbital region. The eye itself receives V1 (ophthalmic)
sensory innervation — cornea, conjunctiva. ST1 is in V2
territory treating a V1-innervated organ. Partial segmental
correspondence — V1 and V2 share the trigeminal ganglion and
nucleus, providing an indirect pathway.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line (SFL) connects
through facial fascia. Minimal specific relevance at the
infraorbital margin. 🔴

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no specific data for the
infraorbital region. Kim 2017 focused on trunk dermatomes.

**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — periorbital face.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP approximate, Ashi).
The infraorbital margin is a less universal treatment target
than the nasolabial area (LI20, 5/8) or lateral orbital area
(GB1, 3/8). ST1's clinical importance derives primarily from
its TCM starting-point status and direct access to the
infraorbital nerve territory.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Ling Shu: starting point of Foot Yangming, associated with
  eye disorders.
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: eye redness (目赤), lacrimation
  (目泪出), night blindness (目不明/雀目), blurred vision,
  eyelid twitching (目瞤).
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: myopia, optic atrophy, mouth deviation
  (facial paralysis), cold lacrimation.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Eye disorders: conjunctivitis, keratitis, excessive
  lacrimation, night blindness, myopia, optic atrophy,
  early cataract
- Facial paralysis: lower eyelid weakness (inability to close)
- Eyelid conditions: blepharospasm, twitching

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng: commonly combined with GB1 (lateral eye),
BL1 (medial eye), BL2 (supraorbital), ST2 (infraorbital
foramen), GB20 (occipital), LR3 (distal). Standard orbital
point trio: BL1 + ST1 + GB1 = medial + inferior + lateral
orbital approaches.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all indications. ST1 appears in multi-point
protocols for myopia prevention (multiple Chinese RCTs using
auricular + body acupuncture) and dry eye disease, but no study
isolates ST1's individual contribution. The strongest evidence
in eye acupuncture involves multi-point protocols — ST1's
contribution is inseparable. No neuroimaging or physiological
study has specifically investigated ST1 stimulation.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal sensitization data for ST1. The infraorbital margin
area shows clinical tenderness during acute conjunctivitis,
periorbital edema, and sinus congestion — consistent with
disease-state sensitization via the trigeminal (V2) sensory
field. Whether this represents true dynamic acupoint behavior
or general periorbital inflammation is unstudied.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Eye effects (conjunctivitis, lacrimation, visual disorders):**
V2 infraorbital afferents → trigeminal nucleus → modulation of
parasympathetic outflow to the lacrimal gland (via superior
salivatory nucleus → greater petrosal nerve → pterygopalatine
ganglion → lacrimal branch). This pathway parallels GB1's
proposed mechanism but from a DIFFERENT orbital approach:

| Point | Orbital position | V branch | Shared relay |
|-------|-----------------|----------|-------------|
| GB1 | Lateral canthus | V1 (lacrimal) + V2 | Trigeminal nucleus |
| ST1 | Infraorbital | V2 (infraorbital) | Trigeminal nucleus |
| BL1 | Medial canthus | V1 (nasociliary) | Trigeminal nucleus |

All three orbital points access the trigeminal system from
different directions — converging on the same brainstem relay
for lacrimal and autonomic modulation.

**Facial paralysis (lower eyelid):** CN VII zygomatic branch
courses directly over or near the inferior orbital rim. ST1
needling may stimulate this motor branch, promoting recovery in
CN VII lesions. The lower eyelid is innervated by the zygomatic
branch — loss causes incomplete eye closure (lagophthalmos).

**Gap:** traditional indications include night blindness, myopia,
and optic atrophy — conditions involving retinal and optic nerve
structures deep within the orbit. No mechanism connects
infraorbital needling to retinal function. Large claim-to-
mechanism gap. 🔴
→ Cross-ref: GB1 (same gap for cataracts, glaucoma)

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 5–10 mm subcutaneous, directed inferiorly along
the orbital floor or laterally along the infraorbital margin.
Push the eyeball gently upward with the non-needling finger to
create space at the infraorbital rim. De Qi: distension and
heaviness around the eye, sometimes lacrimation. Do NOT
manipulate aggressively — periorbital tissue is delicate.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

Bấm huyệt (B02): gentle pressure along the inferior orbital
rim with the pad of the index finger. Standard component of
Chinese eye exercise routines (眼保健操). Press and release
in circular motions, 10–20 repetitions.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa only — maintain significant distance
from the eye. Modern practice rarely applies moxa at ST1. Some
classical texts permit 3 moxa cones (Jiayi Jing), but safety
concerns dominate modern usage.

### An toàn

**ORBITAL PENETRATION RISK — HIGHEST PRIORITY.** The orbital
cavity is immediately superior and posterior to ST1. The orbital
septum separates preseptal (safe) from postseptal (orbital
contents) space. Needle must remain in preseptal tissue — along
the orbital RIM, not INTO the orbit.

Complications of deep insertion:
- Retrobulbar hemorrhage → orbital compartment syndrome →
  vision loss
- Globe perforation → endophthalmitis
- Injury to inferior rectus or inferior oblique muscles

Periorbital hematoma: common minor complication. Infraorbital
and inferior palpebral arteries create bleeding risk. Apply firm
pressure for 3–5 minutes post-withdrawal. Warn patients about
potential bruising (cosmetically significant).

Safety requirements:
- Shallow subcutaneous insertion ONLY
- Direct the needle along the bony rim, never INTO the orbit
- Fine gauge needles (0.20–0.25 mm)
- No aggressive manipulation
- EA: extreme caution near the eye (risk of orbicularis oculi
  spasm)

No pregnancy contraindication.

---

## ST2 — Tứ Bạch (四白, Sì Bái)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Four Whites" (四 = four, 白 = white) — in the depression at
the infraorbital foramen, directly below the pupil. The name
refers to the surrounding whiteness visible in four directions
when the eyes look straight ahead. ST2 is the point of maximum
ACCESS to the infraorbital nerve trunk — the nerve exits the
maxillary bone through the infraorbital foramen at this exact
location. This makes ST2 the most anatomically precise nerve-
access point on the ST face segment: a named bony foramen
delivering the nerve from the infraorbital canal to the surface.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): below the pupil, in the
depression at the infraorbital foramen.

Anatomical landmarks: with the patient gazing forward, drop
directly below the pupil from ST1 (infraorbital margin) by ~1
cun (~1 cm). The infraorbital foramen is palpable as a small
depression in the maxillary bone, approximately 1 cm below the
inferior orbital rim. Gentle pressure over the foramen often
elicits a tingling sensation (nerve exit).

Spatial relationships:
- ST1 (Chengqi) is directly above — at the orbital rim, ~1 cm.
- ST3 (Juliao) is directly below — level with the ala nasi.
- The infraorbital foramen is a key surgical landmark used in
  maxillofacial surgery and nerve blocks. ST2 aligns precisely
  with the standard infraorbital nerve block injection site.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique. Direct
insertion toward the foramen achieves nerve contact. For
trigeminal neuralgia treatment, some practitioners direct the
needle gently into the foramen opening (advanced technique,
specialist only).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Infraorbital nerve (CN V2) — PRIMARY TARGET.** This is THE
  defining anatomical feature of ST2. The infraorbital nerve
  (second division of trigeminal, maxillary branch) exits the
  infraorbital foramen here after traversing the infraorbital
  canal within the maxilla. Upon emergence, it fans into three
  terminal branch groups:
  - Inferior palpebral branches → lower eyelid
  - External nasal branches → lateral nose
  - Superior labial branches → upper lip, gingiva
  The foramen delivers the FULL trunk before branching — making
  ST2 the site of maximum nerve concentration on the entire
  infraorbital distribution.
- Buccal and zygomatic branches of facial nerve (CN VII) —
  motor to lower orbicularis oculi and upper lip elevators
  (levator labii superioris, zygomaticus minor).
- Spinal segments: CN V2, CN VII
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular:
- Infraorbital artery (exits foramen alongside the nerve —
  branch of maxillary artery)
- Facial artery branches ascending in the nasolabial region

Tissue layers:
- Skin → subcutaneous fat
- Levator labii superioris (covers the foramen superficially)
- Infraorbital foramen (in maxillary bone)
- Infraorbital canal (deep — within bone, not accessible)

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No special category designations. ST2 is a regular channel point
without Five Shu, Yuan, Luo, Xi-Cleft, or other category
membership.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST2 Sibai (四白). Functions: brightens the
eyes (明目), expels Wind from the face, activates the channel
for facial paralysis. The primary trigeminal (V2) access point
on the ST face segment.

**2. Trigger point:** levator labii superioris trigger point —
overlying the infraorbital foramen. Referred pain to the nose
and upper lip region. Direct anatomical overlap. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender during sinusitis (maxillary
sinus is immediately deep to the infraorbital foramen), dental
pain (V2 supplies upper teeth via superior alveolar nerves),
and trigeminal neuralgia (V2 branch). The infraorbital foramen
is a known tenderness point during maxillary sinus inflammation.

**4. Marma point:** no specific infraorbital Marma. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** CN V2 dermatome. The infraorbital foramen is
the EXIT point of V2's main facial branch. For conditions
affecting V2's distribution (upper lip, lateral nose, lower
eyelid), ST2 provides the most direct access. Strong segmental
correspondence for V2 territory. 🟢
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** minimal. 🔴

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** maxillary sinusitis
involves V2 territory — neurogenic inflammation at the foramen
area is plausible during acute sinus disease. Not studied.

**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — face.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi). The Head Zone
correspondence for V2 is direct and strong (nerve EXIT point at
foramen), raising functional convergence despite low count.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: eye redness (目赤), eye pain, dizziness,
  mouth deviation (口喎, facial paralysis), eyelid twitching
  (目瞤), blurred vision (目不明).
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: trigeminal neuralgia (面痛), facial
  paralysis, lacrimation, nasal obstruction.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Facial paralysis: key point for lower face weakness
- Trigeminal neuralgia (V2 distribution): upper lip, cheek,
  lower eyelid
- Eye disorders: lacrimation, itching, redness, blurred vision
- Sinusitis: maxillary sinus region
- Biliary ascariasis (combined with LI20)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng: essential point in ALL facial paralysis
protocols alongside ST4, ST6, LI4, and TE17. Standard
infraorbital nerve block reference point in both TCM and
Western facial pain management. Often paired with GB14
(supraorbital) for upper + lower orbital nerve access.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Facial paralysis (Bell's palsy):**
🟡 Plausible. ST2 is included in virtually all acupuncture
protocols for Bell's palsy. Multiple systematic reviews (Li
2020, Liu 2019) show acupuncture superior to drug therapy alone
for facial nerve recovery, with ST2 among the most frequently
selected points. Individual contribution not isolated — same
"protocol inclusion without isolation" pattern (AP-02 Obs. 30).

**Trigeminal neuralgia (V2 branch):**
🟡 Plausible. The infraorbital foramen location makes ST2 the
most anatomically logical acupoint for V2 trigeminal neuralgia.
Case series and small RCTs show acupuncture protocols including
ST2 reduce pain in V2-distribution trigeminal neuralgia. The
correspondence between ST2's location and the standard
infraorbital nerve block site provides mechanistic plausibility.

**Allergic rhinitis:**
🟡 Plausible. ST2 is included in the ACUSAR trial protocol
(Brinkhaus 2013) alongside LI20, LI4, ST36, and others.
Part of the multi-point protocol with demonstrated efficacy
for seasonal allergic rhinitis.
→ Cross-ref: LI20 (AP-02) — primary nasal point in same protocol

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal sensitization data. The infraorbital foramen region
shows clinical tenderness during maxillary sinusitis and V2
neuralgia — consistent with disease-state sensitization via the
infraorbital nerve. Whether ST2 specifically demonstrates
enhanced neurogenic inflammatory changes during V2-territory
disease is unstudied.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Facial paralysis (CN VII recovery):**
ST2 overlies the zygomatic and buccal branches of CN VII, which
motor innervate the lower orbicularis oculi and upper lip
elevators. Needling at ST2 may directly stimulate these motor
branches or their perineural tissue, promoting nerve recovery.
EA at ST2 in Bell's palsy protocols delivers electrical
stimulation to the CN VII branches where they cross superficially
over the maxilla. 🟡

**Trigeminal neuralgia (V2 modulation):**
ST2 at the infraorbital foramen = the nerve trunk EXIT point.
Needling here stimulates V2 trunk before branching — maximum
fiber recruitment. Proposed mechanism: afferent stimulation →
trigeminal nucleus caudalis → descending modulation (endogenous
analgesic pathways). This parallels the infraorbital nerve block
— both target the same nerve at the same location, differing
only in modality (mechanical nerve stimulation vs chemical
conduction block).

**Gap:** whether needling at the foramen produces any effect
comparable to local anesthetic nerve block is unstudied. The two
techniques share anatomy but differ in mechanism. 🔴

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 5–10 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique toward
the foramen. De Qi at ST2 is distinctive: tingling or electric
sensation radiating to the upper lip, lateral nose, or lower
eyelid (infraorbital nerve distribution). This radiation pattern
is diagnostically useful — it confirms accurate nerve contact.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

EA: commonly applied between bilateral ST2 or ST2→ST6 for
facial paralysis. Low frequency (2–4 Hz) with gentle intensity.
Monitor facial muscle contraction during EA.

Bấm huyệt (B02): circular pressure over the infraorbital
foramen. Applied in eye exercise routines (眼保健操) alongside
ST1. Press firmly enough to feel the bony foramen beneath.

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa appropriate for chronic conditions.
Maintain distance from the eye above.

### An toàn

Lower risk than ST1 — the point is below the orbital rim, NOT
at the orbit itself.

Precautions:
- Do NOT insert deeply into the infraorbital foramen/canal
  (advanced technique only, risk of nerve damage within bony
  canal)
- Infraorbital artery exits alongside the nerve — moderate
  bleeding and bruising risk
- Periorbital hematoma can extend upward from ST2 to the
  lower eyelid (cosmetic concern)
- Fine gauge needles recommended for facial comfort

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST3 — Cự Liêu (巨髎, Jù Liáo)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Great Bone-Hole" (巨 = great/large, 髎 = bone depression) —
named for the palpable depression in the maxilla at this level,
below the infraorbital foramen. Located directly below the
pupil, level with the lower border of the ala nasi, in the
nasolabial sulcus.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): directly below the pupil, at
the level of the lower border of the ala nasi (nostril wing), in
the nasolabial sulcus.

Anatomical landmarks: the nasolabial sulcus (nasolabial fold) at
the level of the nostril wing. ST3 sits between ST2 (above, at
the infraorbital foramen) and ST4 (below, at the mouth corner).
LI20 (Yingxiang) is medial — at the ala nasi itself, ~0.5–1 cm
medial to ST3.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm perpendicular or oblique.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Infraorbital nerve terminal branches (CN V2) — specifically
  the external nasal and superior labial branches, distal to
  their exit from the foramen (at ST2 above)
- Buccal branch of facial nerve (CN VII) — motor to upper lip
  elevators and buccinator
- Spinal segments: CN V2, CN VII
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: facial artery and vein course through the nasolabial
area. Angular artery ascending toward the medial canthus.

Tissue layers: skin → levator labii superioris → buccinator →
maxilla (periosteum). The maxillary sinus is deep to the
periosteum — not reached at standard needle depth.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST3 Juliao — nasal obstruction, facial paralysis,
lip swelling, facial pain. Transit point between ST2 and ST4.
**2. Trigger point:** levator labii superioris or nasalis
trigger point area. Moderate. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender with sinusitis, dental pain.
**4. Marma:** no correspondence. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** V2 territory, terminal branches. 🟡
**6. Anatomy Trains:** minimal.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data.
**8. Cupping:** N/A face.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): mouth deviation (facial
paralysis), lip swelling, eyelid twitching, nasal obstruction,
toothache (upper teeth — V2 territory), epistaxis.

Nguồn hiện đại: facial paralysis, trigeminal neuralgia (V2),
nasal obstruction, rhinitis. LI20 (medial) is preferred for
nasal conditions; ST3 is a secondary support point.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. Included in some facial paralysis protocols but
never studied individually. No mechanism evidence.

### An toàn

Low risk — below orbital rim, no critical deep structures at
standard needle depth. The maxillary sinus is deep to the
periosteum but unreachable at 5–10 mm. Standard facial
acupuncture precautions: minor bleeding, bruising risk from
facial artery branches.

No pregnancy contraindication. No moxa prohibition.

---

## ST4 — Địa Thương (地仓, Dì Cāng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Earth Granary" (地 = earth, 仓 = granary/storehouse) — at the
corner of the mouth, lateral to the oral commissure. The name
connects to the Stomach's Earth element function: the mouth as
the "granary" that receives food. This naming follows the Earth-
element alimentary metaphor seen throughout the ST channel (cf.
ST25 Tianshu "Celestial Pivot," ST36 Zusanli "Three Miles of
the Foot"). ST4 is a **MEETING POINT of three channels**: Foot
Yangming (ST), Hand Yangming (LI), and Yang Qiao Mai (阳跷脉).
The LI–ST meeting here means BOTH Yangming channels converge at
the mouth — the alimentary entry point for the Earth element
organ pair.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): lateral to the corner of the
mouth, directly below the pupil.

Anatomical landmarks: 0.4 cun lateral to the angle of the mouth
(oral commissure), in line with the pupil when gazing forward.
The oral commissure = junction of upper and lower lips.

Spatial relationships:
- ST3 (Juliao) is directly above — at the ala nasi level.
- ST5 (Daying) is below and posterior — at the mandibular angle.
- LI20 is above and medial — at the nasolabial fold by the
  nostril. The LI channel crosses the midline → contralateral
  LI20 → ST1, descends to ST4 where LI and ST formally meet.
- CV24 (Chengjiang) is at the mentolabial sulcus, midline —
  the nearest Ren Mai point.

Needle depth: 10–15 mm oblique or subcutaneous, often directed
laterally toward ST6 (Jiache) at the masseter. Transverse
threading from ST4 to ST6 is a classical technique for facial
paralysis — creating a subcutaneous line connecting the mouth
corner to the jaw angle.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Buccal nerve (CN V3):** branch of the mandibular division
  of CN V. Sensory to the cheek and buccal mucosa. ST4 marks
  the TRANSITION from V2 territory (ST1–ST3, infraorbital) to
  V3 territory (ST4 onward to ST8 at the temporal region). This
  is the first trigeminal division boundary on the ST channel.
- **Infraorbital nerve terminal branches (CN V2):** superior
  labial branches reaching the upper lip — overlap zone between
  V2 (from above) and V3 (from below).
- **Buccal and marginal mandibular branches of facial nerve
  (CN VII):** motor to orbicularis oris, depressor anguli oris,
  depressor labii inferioris, mentalis. This is the CRITICAL
  motor zone for facial expression — where multiple CN VII
  branches converge to control the mouth.
- Spinal segments: CN V2/V3 overlap, CN VII
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural zone boundary:** ST4 sits at the V2→V3 transition.
ST1–ST3 are pure V2 (infraorbital). ST4 onward shifts into V3
(buccal, mandibular, auriculotemporal toward ST8). This
establishes the first neural zone boundary on the ST face
segment.

Vascular:
- Facial artery — courses along the nasolabial sulcus, passing
  near the oral commissure before ascending as the angular artery
- Labial arteries (superior and inferior) — branch from the
  facial artery at the oral commissure. Moderate bleeding risk.

Tissue layers: skin → orbicularis oris (encircles the mouth) →
buccinator (deep to orbicularis) → buccal mucosa. The oral
commissure is a muscular convergence zone: orbicularis oris,
levator anguli oris, depressor anguli oris, zygomaticus major,
buccinator, risorius. Multiple motor endplates in a small area.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft, Back-Shu,
Front-Mu, Eight Confluent, or Hui-Meeting designation.

**Meeting point (交会穴):** meeting of Foot Yangming (ST), Hand
Yangming (LI), and Yang Qiao Mai (阳跷脉).

The ST + LI meeting at the mouth completes the Yangming facial
circuit: LI ascends from the hand → crosses midline at LI20 →
arrives at ST1 → descends to ST4 where it formally "meets" the
ST channel. Both Yangming channels converge at Earth's entry
point — the mouth that receives food.

Yang Qiao Mai meets ST at BOTH ST1 (infraorbital) and ST4 (oral
commissure), creating a two-point ST–Yang Qiao Mai link on the
face. Yang Qiao Mai's facial trajectory: BL1 (medial canthus) →
ST1 (infraorbital) → ST4 (mouth corner) — connecting the eye-to-
mouth arc along the CN VII motor territory.
→ Cross-ref: ST1 (Yang Qiao Mai meeting), B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST4 Dicang (地仓). Functions: expels Wind
from the face (祛风), activates the channel (通经络), stops
drooling (止涎). THE primary oral commissure point for facial
paralysis — directly targets the mouth deviation that defines
the condition.

**2. Trigger point:** orbicularis oris or depressor anguli oris
trigger points near the oral commissure. Referred pain to the
lip and chin. Direct anatomical overlap with the perioral
myofascial system. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in Bell's palsy — the
deviated oral commissure is the most visible symptom, and
palpation at the affected side's ST4 reveals muscle flaccidity
or tension depending on the paralysis phase. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Oshtha Marma (ओष्ठ, "lip") — at the
lips/oral commissure region, classified as Mamsa Marma (flesh/
muscle type). Injury causes speech and eating impairment.
Moderate correspondence — both target the oral motor region. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** V2/V3 overlap at the oral commissure — the
boundary between maxillary and mandibular trigeminal divisions.
The mouth is innervated by both V2 (upper lip) and V3 (lower
lip, chin). Direct correspondence to the perioral sensory
territory.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** minimal. 🔴

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no data.

**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — face.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Marma). Highest
convergence in Batch 1, reflecting the oral commissure's
universal importance as a motor-function treatment target.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: mouth deviation (口喎, facial paralysis),
  inability to speak (不能言), drooling (流涎), lip and cheek
  pain, facial edema, toothache.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: twitching of mouth and eye, trismus (locked
  jaw), facial pain, night drooling.
- Classical combination: ST4 + ST6 + LI4 — the "three
  cornerstones" of facial paralysis treatment.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Facial paralysis: deviation of the mouth (primary indication)
- Drooling: particularly post-stroke
- Trigeminal neuralgia: V2/V3 distribution
- Difficulty speaking or eating (oral motor impairment)
- Eyelid twitching (transmitted along CN VII from mouth to eye)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng: THE essential point for mouth deviation
in Bell's palsy. Combined with ST6 (masseter) and LI4 (distal
Yangming) in virtually ALL facial paralysis protocols.
Transverse threading (ST4→ST6 subcutaneous) is a signature
technique. Also combined with GB14 + TE17 + GB20 for
comprehensive Bell's palsy treatment.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Facial paralysis (Bell's palsy):**
🟡 Plausible. ST4 is included in virtually every acupuncture
protocol for Bell's palsy — the most-used perioral point.
Multiple systematic reviews demonstrate acupuncture protocols'
effectiveness for facial nerve recovery. ST4 appears in >90% of
included Bell's palsy studies.

ST4-specific technique: the transverse threading (ST4→ST6,
subcutaneous needle connecting mouth corner to jaw angle) follows
the CN VII buccal branch trajectory — potentially stimulating the
nerve along its course. No study has isolated this technique from
multi-point protocols.

**Post-stroke drooling:**
🔴 Unvalidated. Case series suggest acupuncture at oral
commissure points (ST4, CV24) may reduce drooling. No controlled
evidence.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

In facial paralysis, the affected side's oral commissure shows
measurable changes: reduced muscle tone (flaccid phase),
hypertonicity and synkinesis (recovery phase). Whether these
disease-state changes at ST4 qualify as dynamic acupoint
sensitization (in the formal Chen/Kim sense) has not been
studied. The motor nerve changes are documented (EMG studies
show denervation → reinnervation patterns), but relate to motor
nerve pathology rather than sensory threshold shifts.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Facial paralysis (CN VII motor recovery):**
ST4 overlies the convergence zone of multiple CN VII motor
branches (buccal, marginal mandibular). Needling or EA may
promote nerve recovery through: (1) direct electrical
stimulation of CN VII branches → maintaining muscle tone during
denervation, (2) local anti-inflammatory effects reducing
perineural edema, (3) enhancing axonal regeneration via local
growth factor release.

The transverse threading technique (ST4→ST6) traces the buccal
branch of CN VII from the oral commissure to the masseter —
creating a subcutaneous electrical pathway along the nerve
course.

**Drooling (sialorrhea):**
The oral commissure is the anatomical point where saliva escapes
during inadequate lip closure. ST4 stimulation → CN VII buccal
branch → orbicularis oris contraction → improved lip seal. A
motor mechanism targeting salivary RETENTION (lip closure) rather
than salivary PRODUCTION (autonomic glands).

**Gap:** whether acupuncture at ST4 actually enhances CN VII
regeneration or merely provides symptomatic motor stimulation is
unstudied. Most Bell's palsy recovers spontaneously (70–80%
within 6 months), making it difficult to attribute recovery to
any intervention. 🔴

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 10–15 mm oblique or subcutaneous. Standard
direction: laterally toward ST6, enabling transverse threading.
De Qi: local distension at the mouth corner, sometimes
involuntary lip movement (CN VII motor response). The transverse
threading technique: single needle inserted at ST4, advanced
subcutaneously ~2–3 cm toward ST6 along the jawline.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

EA: commonly applied ST4→ST6 (mouth corner to jaw angle) for
facial paralysis. Low frequency (2 Hz), gentle intensity
(visible muscle contraction without pain). Monitor facial muscle
response to calibrate intensity.

Bấm huyệt (B02): press at the oral commissure with fingertip,
directing pressure laterally toward the masseter. Self-massage
for facial tension and TMJ-related perioral discomfort.

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa applicable. Used for chronic facial
paralysis with cold pattern (residual deviation without
recovery).

### An toàn

Low risk — no major deep structures. The oral commissure has no
bony cavity access (unlike ST1/orbit or ST12/thorax).

Precautions:
- Facial artery and labial arteries at the commissure — moderate
  bleeding risk. Apply pressure post-withdrawal.
- EA intensity: too strong → visible facial contortion
  (uncomfortable, potentially counterproductive in acute Bell's
  palsy)
- Flaccid phase of Bell's palsy: gentle stimulation only
  (excessive stimulation may promote synkinesis during recovery)
- Transverse threading (ST4→ST6): requires skill — needle
  passes subcutaneously over the buccal fat pad and masseter.
  If too deep → buccal mucosa perforation.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

> **Batch 1 Architectural Observations (ST1–ST4 — 3 Tier B +
> 1 Tier C)**
>
> **Observation 1 — YANGMING FACE TRANSITION COMPLETE
> (LI20→ST1): THE ONLY CONTRALATERAL CROSSING DOCUMENTED FROM
> BOTH SIDES.**
>
> ST1 as receiver completes documentation of the Yangming same-
> name transition. With this entry, ALL THREE Yang same-name face
> transitions are documented from both sender and receiver:
>
> | Same-name | Sender | Receiver | Orbital position | Crossing |
> |-----------|--------|----------|-----------------|----------|
> | Yangming | LI20 (nose) | ST1 (infraorbital) | Inferior | Contralateral |
> | Taiyang | SI19 (ear) | BL1 (future) | Medial | Ipsilateral |
> | Shaoyang | TE23 (brow) | GB1 (lateral eye) | Lateral | Ipsilateral |
>
> Three Yang channel families → three face transitions → three
> DIFFERENT orbital positions. The eye region is the convergence
> zone where all three Yang same-name transitions arrive — each
> from a different sensory territory (nose, ear, lateral brow),
> each approaching a different part of the orbital rim. The
> Yangming transition is architecturally unique: only
> contralateral crossing, largest sender-receiver gap, only one
> crossing a midline structure (philtrum/GV26).
>
> **Observation 2 — INFRAORBITAL NERVE GRADIENT (ST1→ST4):
> DESCENDING V2 DISTRIBUTION WITH FORAMEN ACCESS AT ST2.**
>
> The first 4 ST points trace the infraorbital nerve (V2) from
> the orbital rim to the oral commissure — a descending gradient
> along the nerve's terminal distribution:
>
> | Point | V2 access level | Clinical emphasis |
> |-------|----------------|-------------------|
> | ST1 | Terminal branches at orbital rim | Eye disorders |
> | ST2 | **Foramen = trunk exit** | Trigeminal neuralgia (V2) |
> | ST3 | Distal branches, nasolabial | Nasal/facial (secondary) |
> | ST4 | Most distal V2 + V3 overlap | Facial paralysis (CN VII) |
>
> ST2 at the infraorbital foramen = point of MAXIMUM nerve
> access — the trunk before branching. This mirrors a pattern:
> LI4 (web space = deep palmar branch), TE17 (stylomastoid
> foramen = CN VII trunk exit), GB1 (lateral orbital rim = V1/V2
> convergence). Clinically important acupoints cluster at
> anatomical ACCESS points — foramina, nerve exits, fascial
> windows — not at random positions along nerve distributions.
>
> **Observation 3 — YANG QIAO MAI FACIAL ARC: BL1→ST1→ST4.**
>
> Yang Qiao Mai meets the ST channel at both ST1 (infraorbital)
> and ST4 (mouth corner), creating a two-point orbital-to-oral
> link. The vessel's facial trajectory: BL1 (medial canthus) →
> ST1 (infraorbital) → ST4 (oral commissure) traces the CN VII
> motor territory — from the zygomatic branch zone (BL1→ST1)
> through the buccal branch zone (ST1→ST4). This vessel connects
> three orbital approach points and extends to the mouth — a
> trajectory mapping to the facial nerve's branching pattern from
> the stylomastoid foramen to the perioral muscles. Yang Qiao
> Mai's facial arc = CN VII motor innervation territory.
>
> **Observation 4 — V2→V3 BOUNDARY AT ST4: FIRST TRIGEMINAL
> DIVISION TRANSITION ON THE ST FACE.**
>
> ST4 at the oral commissure marks the transition from V2
> (infraorbital, maxillary) to V3 (buccal, mandibular) territory.
> ST1–ST3 are pure V2; ST4 onward shifts into V3. This boundary
> at the oral commissure follows the anatomical transition between
> maxillary and mandibular trigeminal divisions — upper lip = V2
> (superior labial nerves), lower lip = V3 (mental nerve). The ST
> channel crosses this boundary exactly where the anatomy crosses
> it. Compare: the LI channel has only 2 face points (LI19–LI20)
> both in V2. The ST channel maps V2 territory more completely (4
> points, ST1–ST4) before transitioning to V3 — consistent with
> Foot Yangming's larger anatomical coverage vs Hand Yangming.

---

## ST5 — Đại Nghênh (大迎, Dà Yíng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Great Welcome" (大 = great, 迎 = welcome/receive) — at the
mandibular angle, anterior to the masseter attachment. Named for
the point where the facial artery is "welcomed" as it ascends
over the mandible — the facial artery is palpable here, crossing
the lower border of the mandible anterior to the masseter.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): anterior to the angle of the
mandible, on the anterior border of the masseter muscle
attachment, in the groove-like depression appearing when the
cheek is puffed out.

Anatomical landmarks: at the lower border of the mandible,
anterior to the masseter's mandibular attachment. The facial
artery crosses the mandible here — palpable as a pulse point.
ST5 sits between ST4 (above, at the mouth corner) and ST6
(above-posterior, at the masseter body).

Needle depth: 5–10 mm oblique or perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Marginal mandibular branch of facial nerve (CN VII) — motor
  to depressor anguli oris, depressor labii inferioris, mentalis
- Mental nerve (CN V3) — terminal branch of inferior alveolar
  nerve, sensory to lower lip and chin
- Spinal segments: CN V3, CN VII
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: **facial artery** — crosses the lower mandibular
border at ST5's level, ascending toward the nasolabial fold and
medial canthus. This is a major palpation landmark. Facial vein
accompanies the artery.

Tissue layers: skin → platysma → masseter anterior border →
mandibular periosteum.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST5 Daying — toothache (lower teeth), facial
swelling, facial paralysis (chin deviation), trismus.
**2. Trigger point:** anterior masseter or platysma area.
Moderate. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender in dental infection, facial swelling.
**4. Marma:** no direct correspondence. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** V3 (mandibular) dermatome — corresponds to
lower teeth (inferior alveolar nerve). 🟡
**6–8:** minimal / N/A.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): toothache (齿痛), facial
swelling (颊肿), trismus (牙关紧闭), mouth deviation (facial
paralysis), neck swelling.

Nguồn hiện đại: lower toothache, facial edema, parotitis
(mumps), facial paralysis. Supporting point for mandibular
region conditions.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST5. Appears in some facial
paralysis protocols as a supporting mandibular point.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — **facial artery** crosses at this level.
Puncture → bleeding, potential hematoma. Palpate the artery and
avoid direct puncture. Apply pressure post-withdrawal.

No pregnancy contraindication. No moxa prohibition.

---

## ST6 — Giáp Xa (颊车, Jiá Chē)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Jaw Chariot" (颊 = cheek, 车 = chariot/vehicle) — at the
center of the masseter muscle, approximately one fingerwidth
(1 cun) anterior and superior to the mandibular angle. The name
evokes the jaw as a "chariot" — the powerful moving apparatus
of mastication. ST6 is the most-referenced facial point across
the entire Atlas, appearing in facial paralysis protocols from
every completed channel's entries (AP-01 through AP-11).

ST6 carries a **DUAL special designation:**
- **Ghost Point #7 — Gui Chuang (鬼床, "Ghost Bed"):** seventh
  in Sun Si-miao's 13 Ghost Points (652 CE). Member of the 3rd
  trinity (ST6 + CV24 + PC8) — the "addiction/self-destruction"
  group. Ghost name "Bed" (床) — the jaw as the resting place
  where pathogenic spirits settle. Classical use: jaw clenching
  in mania, epilepsy, and acute psychotic states.
- **Ma Dan-yang Star:** cited in some expanded Ma Dan-yang
  compilations for facial and dental conditions (not in the core
  12-point ode of the Zhenjiu Dacheng).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): one fingerwidth (1 cun)
anterior and superior to the angle of the mandible, at the
prominence of the masseter muscle when the teeth are clenched.

Anatomical landmarks: ask the patient to clench the teeth — the
masseter belly becomes prominent. ST6 is at the highest point
of the masseter prominence, approximately 1 cun anterior and
superior to the mandibular angle. Palpated on the muscular
belly, not at the mandibular border.

Spatial relationships:
- ST5 (Daying) is below and anterior — at the mandibular angle.
- ST7 (Xiaguan) is superior — at the zygomatic arch, above the
  masseter. ST6 and ST7 bracket the masseter vertically.
- ST4 (Dicang) is anterior — the mouth corner. The ST4→ST6
  transverse threading technique connects these points.
- GB3 is superior and lateral — above the zygomatic arch.
- SI19 is posterior — at the pre-auricular depression.

Needle depth: 10–15 mm perpendicular into the masseter belly,
or oblique toward ST4. Deep perpendicular insertion reaches the
masseter's deep head.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Masseteric nerve (CN V3):** branch of the mandibular
  division of CN V. MOTOR to the masseter muscle — the primary
  jaw closer. The masseteric nerve enters the masseter from its
  deep surface after passing through the mandibular notch. ST6
  needle reaches the motor endplate zone within the masseter.
- **Buccal branch of facial nerve (CN VII):** motor to facial
  muscles of the lower cheek. Courses SUPERFICIAL to the
  masseter. Needling at ST6 traverses the CN VII branches
  (superficial) before reaching the masseter motor point (V3
  masseteric, deep).
- Great auricular nerve (C2-C3): sensory to the skin overlying
  the masseter and angle of the mandible.
- Spinal segments: CN V3 (motor), CN VII (motor), C2-C3
  (cutaneous)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Dual motor nerve convergence:** ST6 is one of the few points
in the Atlas where TWO distinct motor nerves from different
cranial nerves converge: CN V3 (mastication) deep and CN VII
(facial expression) superficial. Needling at different depths
accesses different motor systems:
- Superficial (5 mm): CN VII buccal branch → facial paralysis
- Deep (10–15 mm): CN V3 masseteric → TMJ, bruxism, trismus

Vascular:
- Masseteric artery (branch of maxillary artery) — deep to the
  masseter, enters with the nerve through the mandibular notch
- Facial artery branches at the mandibular border (inferior)
- Transverse facial artery (branch of superficial temporal) —
  courses above the zygomatic arch, superior to ST6

Tissue layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous fat → parotid gland capsule (tail)
- CN VII buccal branch (superficial motor layer)
- Masseter muscle (superficial head → deep head)
- Masseteric nerve and artery (between masseter and ramus)
- Mandibular ramus (periosteum — deepest safe layer)

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft, Back-Shu,
Front-Mu, Eight Confluent, or Hui-Meeting designation.

**Ghost Point #7 — Gui Chuang (鬼床, "Ghost Bed"):** seventh in
Sun Si-miao's 13 Ghost Points sequence (652 CE): GV26 (#1) →
LU11 (#2) → SP1 (#3) → PC7 (#4) → BL62 (#5) → GV16 (#6) →
**ST6 (#7)** → CV24 (#8) → PC8 (#9) → GV23 (#10) → CV1 (#11)
→ LI11 (#12) → EX-HN1 (#13).

ST6 belongs to the **3rd trinity** (ST6 + CV24 + PC8):

| Ghost # | Point | Ghost Name | Location | Motor pattern |
|---------|-------|------------|----------|---------------|
| 7 | ST6 | Ghost Bed (鬼床) | Masseter | Jaw clenching |
| 8 | CV24 | Ghost Market (鬼市) | Chin midline | Lip biting/drooling |
| 9 | PC8 | Ghost Cave (鬼窟) | Palm center | Fist clenching |

The 3rd trinity theme is "addiction/self-destruction" (AP-09
classification). All three points target motor zones that
