exhibit involuntary contraction during acute psychotic/manic
episodes: trismus (jaw clenching), lip/tongue biting, and fist
clenching. The trinity may encode OBSERVED MOTOR PATTERNS of
acute psychiatric crisis rather than metaphysical concepts. 🟡

Ghost name "Bed" (床): the jaw as the "bed" or "resting place"
where pathogenic spirits settle — perhaps reflecting the
clinical observation that jaw tension/clenching is a persistent
motor feature in many psychiatric presentations (bruxism in
anxiety, trismus in acute mania, jaw clenching in PTSD).

With ST6, the Atlas now documents Ghost Points at positions #2
(LU11), #3 (SP1), #4 (PC7), #7 (ST6), #9 (PC8), and #12
(LI11) — **6 of 13** documented. Remaining: GV26 (#1), BL62
(#5), GV16 (#6), CV24 (#8), GV23 (#10), CV1 (#11), EX-HN1
(#13).
→ Cross-ref: PC7 Ghost #4 (AP-09), PC8 Ghost #9 (AP-09), LI11
Ghost #12 (AP-02), B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST6 Jiache (颊车). Functions: expels Wind
from the face (祛风), relaxes the jaw (利关), stops toothache
(止牙痛). Primary point for jaw disorders: TMJ dysfunction,
trismus, bruxism, toothache. Central in ALL facial paralysis
protocols across the Atlas.

**2. Trigger point:** **masseter trigger point 1 (TrP1)** —
one of the best-characterized trigger points in the entire
Travell & Simons system. Located at the body of the superficial
masseter, directly at ST6's location. Referred pain: lower
teeth, gums, ear, ipsilateral temple. Taut bands in the
masseter are among the most common findings in TMJ dysfunction.
STRONG correspondence. 🟢
→ Cross-ref: B11 S3.2

**3. Ashi correspondence:** highly reactive in TMJ dysfunction,
bruxism, stress-related jaw clenching, and dental pain.
Palpation frequently reveals taut bands, tenderness, and
restricted jaw opening. One of the most common ashi sites in
clinical practice. 🟢

**4. Marma point:** Hanu Marma (हनु, "jaw/mandible") — at
the mandibular angle/jaw region, classified as Sandhi Marma
(joint type) in some traditions. Injury causes impaired
mastication and speech. Moderate correspondence — both target
the jaw motor function. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** CN V3 (mandibular) dermatome. The masseter is
innervated by the masseteric branch of V3 — direct motor
correspondence. For jaw function conditions (TMJ, bruxism,
trismus), ST6 provides the most direct nerve-muscle access on
any channel. 🟢
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Deep Front Line (DFL) — the masseter
connects via the medial pterygoid and fascial continuity to the
DFL's cranial portion. Indirect connection through deep
masticatory fascia. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no specific data. TMJ
pathology involves neurogenic inflammation in the masseteric and
auriculotemporal nerve territories, but Kim 2017 did not study
cranial points.

**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — face.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP 🟢, Ashi 🟢, Marma, Head
zone 🟢). HIGHEST convergence on the ST face segment. The
masseter TrP1 correspondence is one of the strongest point-to-
point matches in the entire Dorsher dataset. Dual motor nerve
convergence (V3 masseteric + VII buccal) elevates this above
typical facial points.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: toothache (齿痛), trismus (牙关紧闭),
  mouth deviation (口喎, facial paralysis), neck stiffness,
  difficulty speaking.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: facial paralysis, toothache (upper and
  lower), facial swelling, ear pain, tinnitus.
- Sun Si-miao (Bei Ji Qian Jin Yao Fang): Ghost Point #7 for
  "ghost-evil" (鬼邪) — mania, epilepsy, acute psychosis with
  jaw clenching.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- TMJ dysfunction, trismus (lockjaw)
- Facial paralysis (mouth deviation) — THE masseter point
- Toothache (upper and lower teeth)
- Bruxism (teeth grinding)
- Facial swelling (parotitis)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng: appears in facial paralysis protocols
from ALL completed channel entries in the Atlas. Standard
combination: ST4 + ST6 + LI4 + TE17 + GB14 + GB20. The ST4→ST6
transverse threading is a signature technique. For TMJ: ST6 +
ST7 + SI19 + TE21. For Ghost protocol: bilateral ST6 (#7) in
Sun Si-miao's sequential treatment of psychotic episodes.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Facial paralysis (Bell's palsy):**
🟡 Plausible. ST6 is included in virtually ALL acupuncture
protocols for Bell's palsy — the most-used masseter point.
Multiple systematic reviews demonstrate effectiveness of
protocols including ST6 for facial nerve recovery (Liu 2019, Li
2020). ST6 appears alongside ST4, LI4, TE17, GB14 in >90% of
studies. Individual contribution not isolated.

**TMJ dysfunction:**
🟡 Plausible. Acupuncture at ST6 (alone or in protocol with
ST7, SI19) shows reduction in TMJ pain and improvement in mouth
opening in several small RCTs and case series. The masseter
TrP1 correspondence provides strong mechanistic plausibility —
dry needling of the masseter TrP is a well-established treatment
for myofascial TMJ pain.
→ Cross-ref: B11

**Bruxism:**
🔴 Unvalidated. Botulinum toxin injection into the masseter (at
approximately ST6's location) is evidence-based for bruxism —
same anatomical target, different modality. Whether acupuncture
at ST6 produces any comparable effect is unstudied.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

The masseter at ST6 shows clinically measurable changes in TMJ
dysfunction: increased muscle tone, taut band formation,
elevated resting EMG activity. These parallel the trigger point
sensitization model (latent → active TrP). In bruxism: nocturnal
masseter EMG is elevated — the muscle at ST6 is literally more
"active" during disease states. This represents motor
sensitization rather than sensory threshold changes typical of
dynamic acupoints.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4, B11

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Facial paralysis (CN VII recovery):**
ST6 accesses CN VII buccal branch at the superficial level. EA
delivers electrical stimulation to denervated facial muscles via
their CN VII motor branches. The ST4→ST6 threading technique
creates a continuous electrical pathway along the buccal branch
trajectory from mouth to jaw angle.

**TMJ dysfunction / bruxism:**
Deep needling reaches the masseter motor endplate zone (CN V3
masseteric nerve). Proposed mechanism: needle → local twitch
response → taut band release → reduction in masseter
hypertonicity. Identical to the dry needling mechanism for
masseter TrP1 (Hong 1994: LTR = essential component of TrP
deactivation).

**Ghost Point (psychiatric):**
No specific brainstem or cortical pathway identified. Possible
mechanism: masseter relaxation (release of involuntary jaw
clenching) → reduced trigeminal afferent input to the brainstem
→ secondary anxiolytic effect. The trigeminal nerve has extensive
brainstem connections including projections to the reticular
formation, locus coeruleus, and periaqueductal gray. Jaw
relaxation could theoretically modulate arousal and pain
processing circuits. 🔴 Speculative.
→ Cross-ref: PC7 Ghost mechanism gap (AP-09), LI11 Ghost
mechanism gap (AP-02)

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 10–15 mm perpendicular into the masseter belly, or
oblique toward ST4 for transverse threading. De Qi: strong
local aching, heaviness in the jaw, sometimes referred sensation
to the teeth or ear (masseter TrP1 referral pattern). Local
twitch response may be elicited — masseter fasciculation visible
as jaw twitching.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

EA: ST4→ST6 pair for facial paralysis. ST6→ST7 pair for TMJ.
Low frequency (2 Hz) for nerve recovery; higher frequency
(30–100 Hz) for pain/muscle relaxation.

Bấm huyệt (B02): firm digital pressure into the masseter belly,
with the jaw relaxed (mouth slightly open). Self-massage for TMJ
tension: 2–3 minutes of sustained pressure or circular kneading.
One of the most effective self-acupressure points.

Cứu (B08): applicable but less common. Indirect moxa for chronic
facial paralysis with cold pattern.

Dry needling (B11): masseter TrP1 — same anatomical target as
acupuncture at ST6. Technique: locate taut band, insert to
elicit LTR, multiple redirections within the masseter. This
represents the most direct acupuncture-dry needling overlap in
the Atlas — identical point, identical technique, different
theoretical frameworks.
→ Cross-ref: B11 S3.2, S3.3

### An toàn

Moderate risk — parotid gland and facial nerve proximity.

Precautions:
- **Parotid gland:** the tail of the parotid extends over the
  posterior masseter. Deep insertion posteriorly → parotid
  puncture → parotitis risk.
- **Facial nerve (CN VII):** buccal branch crosses superficially
  over the masseter. EA at excessive intensity → facial spasm.
- **Facial artery:** inferior to ST6 at the mandibular border
  (ST5 level). Not directly threatened at ST6's level.
- Masseter soreness: aggressive needling or dry needling → post-
  treatment jaw soreness lasting 24–72 hours. Advise patients.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST7 — Hạ Quan (下关, Xià Guān)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Lower Gate" (下 = lower, 关 = gate/barrier) — at the lower
border of the zygomatic arch, in the depression anterior to the
condyloid process of the mandible when the mouth is closed. The
name forms a paired designation with GB3 Shangguan (上关, "Upper
Gate") — the two points bracket the zygomatic arch from below
(ST7) and above (GB3), creating a **CROSS-CHANNEL GATE PAIR**
at the temporomandibular joint (TMJ). ST7 provides direct access
to the TMJ articular space when the mouth is opened.

ST7 is a **MEETING POINT** of Foot Yangming (ST) and Foot
Shaoyang (GB). Both channels converge at the zygomatic arch —
the anatomical boundary between the temporal and infratemporal
regions.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the face, anterior to the
ear, in the depression between the zygomatic arch and the
mandibular notch. Located with the mouth closed.

Anatomical landmarks: the depression below the zygomatic arch,
anterior to the mandibular condyle. With the mouth CLOSED, the
condyle rests in the mandibular fossa — creating a palpable
depression anterior to it. With the mouth OPEN, the condyle
translates forward and fills the depression — the point becomes
less accessible. (Contrast with SI19 and GB2, which require
mouth OPEN for access.)

Spatial relationships:
- GB3 (Shangguan) is directly above — on the UPPER border of
  the zygomatic arch. ST7 and GB3 are separated by the
  thickness of the arch (~0.5 cun), creating the gate pair.
- ST6 (Jiache) is inferior — at the masseter body. ST6 and ST7
  bracket the masseter vertically (body vs superior attachment).
- SI19 (Tinggong) is posterior — at the pre-auricular depression
  before the tragus. SI19 accesses the TMJ from behind; ST7
  from in front.
- TE21 (Ermen) is superior and posterior to SI19.

Needle depth: 10–15 mm perpendicular. The needle passes through
the masseter's superior attachment. Deep insertion directed
slightly superiorly can approach the TMJ capsule.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Auriculotemporal nerve (CN V3):** sensory branch of the
  mandibular division, courses posterior to the TMJ. Supplies
  the TMJ capsule, external ear, temporal region. The nerve's
  articular branches provide direct TMJ joint innervation.
- **Masseteric nerve (CN V3):** motor to the masseter, passes
  through the mandibular notch immediately deep to ST7.
- **Deep temporal nerves (CN V3):** motor to temporalis muscle,
  emerging from the infratemporal fossa near ST7's deep level.
- Zygomatic branch of facial nerve (CN VII) — crosses the
  zygomatic arch superficially.
- Spinal segments: CN V3 (sensory + motor), CN VII (motor)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular:
- **Superficial temporal artery:** crosses the zygomatic arch
  just anterior to the ear — near ST7's posterior aspect.
  Palpable.
- **Transverse facial artery:** branch of superficial temporal,
  courses anteriorly across the masseter at zygomatic level.
- **Maxillary artery:** deep to the mandibular condyle in the
  infratemporal fossa — NOT accessible at standard depth, but
  deep needling approaches this vessel. SAFETY CONCERN.

Tissue layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous fat → temporal fascia
- Masseter (superior attachment at the zygomatic arch)
- Mandibular notch (between condylar and coronoid processes)
- Infratemporal fossa (deep — containing maxillary artery,
  pterygoid muscles, V3 trunk)

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft, Back-Shu,
Front-Mu, Eight Confluent, or Hui-Meeting designation.

**Meeting point (交会穴):** meeting of Foot Yangming (ST) and
Foot Shaoyang (GB). Both channels converge at the zygomatic
arch.

**Cross-channel gate pair:** ST7 (Lower Gate, 下关) + GB3 (Upper
Gate, 上关). The only named cross-channel gate pair at a bony
landmark in the Atlas. The zygomatic arch separates two
anatomical compartments:

| Gate | Channel | Position | Compartment |
|------|---------|----------|-------------|
| GB3 上关 | GB | Above arch | Temporal fossa |
| ST7 下关 | ST | Below arch | Infratemporal fossa |

The arch as a bony partition: temporal fossa above (superficial
temporal artery, temporalis muscle) and infratemporal fossa
below (maxillary artery, pterygoids, V3 trunk). GB3 and ST7
access these compartments from opposite sides of the same
landmark.
→ Cross-ref: GB3 (AP-11), B01 S3.1

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST7 Xiaguan (下关). Functions: opens the
jaw (开关利窍), benefits the teeth, clears deafness/tinnitus.
Primary TMJ access point — the "lower gate" of the jaw joint.

**2. Trigger point:** lateral pterygoid trigger point (deep to
ST7, in the infratemporal fossa) or upper masseter attachment.
The TMJ capsule itself can be a source of referred pain.
Moderate correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** highly reactive in TMJ dysfunction.
Palpation of the pre-condylar depression reveals significant
tenderness in TMJ pathology. The "click" or "pop" of TMJ disc
displacement may be palpable at ST7 during mouth opening. 🟢

**4. Marma point:** Shankha Marma (शंख, "temple/conch") — at
the temporal region near the TMJ. Classified as Sira Marma
(vessel type). Moderate spatial correspondence. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** CN V3 (mandibular) dermatome. The TMJ capsule
receives V3 articular branches — direct segmental
correspondence for TMJ conditions. 🟢
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** minimal. 🔴
**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** not studied. TMJ
inflammation involves neurogenic sensitization in the
auriculotemporal nerve territory.
**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — face.

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi 🟢, Marma, Head
zone 🟢). Strong for TMJ conditions — direct joint access and V3
articular innervation.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: toothache (齿痛), mouth unable to open
  (口不能开), trismus, facial paralysis, deafness, tinnitus,
  ear pain.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: TMJ clicking, jaw deviation, facial swelling,
  difficulty chewing.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- TMJ dysfunction: pain, clicking, limited opening
- Toothache (upper and lower teeth)
- Facial paralysis
- Deafness, tinnitus (via auriculotemporal nerve)
- Trigeminal neuralgia (V3 distribution)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng: THE primary TMJ access point. Standard
TMJ protocol: ST7 + GB3 (gate pair) + SI19 + TE21 (pre-
auricular) + ST6 (masseter) + LI4 (distal). The gate pair is
used with mouth closed — then mouth opened for SI19 access.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**TMJ dysfunction:**
🟡 Plausible. Multiple RCTs and systematic reviews include ST7
in acupuncture protocols for TMD. A 2017 Cochrane review found
insufficient evidence due to methodological limitations, but
individual RCTs show improvement in pain (VAS reduction) and
maximum mouth opening. ST7 is the most frequently used point in
TMD protocols. Individual contribution not isolated.

**Dental pain:**
🔴 Unvalidated. LI4 (distal) has more evidence for dental
analgesia than ST7 (local).

### Dynamic acupoint properties

TMJ dysfunction produces measurable changes at the ST7 region:
joint capsule inflammation, increased pressure sensitivity over
the condylar depression, and altered joint mechanics (clicking,
crepitus). These correspond to local articular pathology rather
than cutaneous sensory sensitization. The auriculotemporal nerve
territory may show lowered thresholds during active TMJ
inflammation.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**TMJ dysfunction:**
ST7 provides the most direct needling access to the TMJ region
on any channel. Auriculotemporal nerve (V3) supplies the TMJ
capsule — needling stimulates V3 articular afferents →
trigeminal nucleus caudalis → descending modulation.
Additionally, deep needling may reach the lateral pterygoid
muscle or its tendon (inserts on the TMJ disc) — a common
source of TMJ dysfunction.

**Toothache:**
V3 territory — the inferior alveolar nerve (lower teeth) and
superior alveolar nerves (upper teeth via V2) relay through the
trigeminal ganglion. ST7 stimulation of V3 afferents →
trigeminal nucleus modulation → potential attenuation of dental
pain signals. Less direct than LI4's central analgesic mechanism
(PAG pathway).

**Deafness/tinnitus:**
The auriculotemporal nerve also supplies the external ear canal
and tympanic membrane. ST7 stimulation → auriculotemporal
afferents → possible modulation of auditory processing. 🔴
Highly speculative.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 10–15 mm perpendicular with the mouth CLOSED (the
condyle is retracted, creating space). De Qi: deep aching in
the TMJ region, sometimes radiating to the ear or temporal area.
For TMJ treatment, some practitioners insert with mouth slightly
open for more direct joint space access.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

EA: ST7→ST6 pair for TMJ + masseter treatment. ST7→SI19 pair
for TMJ + auricular conditions. Low frequency.

Bấm huyệt (B02): press firmly into the pre-condylar depression
with the mouth closed. Release → open mouth → repeat. Effective
self-care for TMJ tension.

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa for chronic TMJ conditions. Less
commonly used than needling.

### An toàn

**MODERATE-HIGH risk** due to vascular proximity.

Precautions:
- **Maxillary artery:** deep in the infratemporal fossa. Deep
  perpendicular insertion (>15 mm) in thin patients can approach
  this vessel. Avoid excessive depth.
- **Superficial temporal artery:** palpable anterior to the ear
  at zygomatic arch level. Avoid direct puncture.
- **TMJ capsule penetration:** deep insertion may enter the
  joint space — generally not harmful but requires awareness.
- **Middle meningeal artery:** in the infratemporal fossa, deep
  to the lateral pterygoid plate. Unreachable at standard depth
  but represents a theoretical deep-structure risk.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST8 — Đầu Duy (头维, Tóu Wéi)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Head's Binding" (头 = head, 维 = to bind/corner/maintain) — at
the corner of the forehead, 0.5 cun within the anterior hairline,
4.5 cun lateral to the midline (GV24 Shenting). The name
references the point's location at the "corner binding" of the
head — where frontal and temporal regions meet. ST8 is the LAST
point of the ST face-head segment before the channel drops to
the neck (ST9). It also serves as the **ANCHOR POINT** for the
GB temporal arc: GB4, GB5, and GB6 are geometrically interpolated
on the curved line from ST8 to GB7 (AP-11 Obs. 3).

ST8 is a **MEETING POINT of three channels/vessels**: Foot
Yangming (ST), Foot Shaoyang (GB), and Yang Wei Mai (阳维脉).
The character 维 (wéi, "binding/maintaining") appears in BOTH
the point name (头维 Touwei) and the vessel name (阳维脉 Yang
Wei Mai) — a nomenclatural link between the point and the
Extraordinary Vessel it anchors.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): 0.5 cun directly above the
anterior hairline at the corner of the forehead, 4.5 cun lateral
to the midline (GV24).

Anatomical landmarks: at the junction of the frontal and
temporal regions — the "corner" of the forehead where the
hairline turns from frontal (anterior) to temporal (lateral).
Approximately where the frontal and temporal bones meet.

Spatial relationships:
- ST7 (Xiaguan) is far below — at the zygomatic arch. The
  ST7→ST8 gap is the largest between consecutive ST head
  points (~6–7 cm), reflecting the channel's trajectory from
  jaw to temporal hairline.
- GB4 (Hanyan) is posterior — at the 1/4 mark on the ST8→GB7
  curve. First point in the GB temporal arc using ST8 as
  anterior anchor.
- GB13 (Benshen) is medial — 3 cun lateral to GV24 (vs ST8 at
  4.5 cun lateral).
- GV24 (Shenting) is at the midline.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm subcutaneous (transverse insertion),
directed posteriorly along the scalp. Scalp acupuncture
technique — shallow beneath the galea aponeurotica.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Auriculotemporal nerve (CN V3):** temporal branches supply
  the temporal scalp. The SAME nerve that supplies the TMJ
  region at ST7 — demonstrating V3 territory extends from the
  jaw (ST5–ST7) to the temporal scalp (ST8).
- **Zygomaticotemporal nerve (CN V2):** supplies the anterior
  temporal region. At ST8, V2 and V3 overlap — the temporal
  scalp receives dual innervation.
- Temporal branch of facial nerve (CN VII) — motor to frontalis
  (forehead wrinkling).
- Spinal segments: CN V2/V3 overlap, CN VII
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

**Neural zone:** ST8 remains in V3-dominant territory
(auriculotemporal nerve) with V2 overlap (zygomaticotemporal).
The ST face segment has TWO neural zones: V2 (ST1–ST3) and V3
(ST4–ST8), with V2/V3 transition at ST4 and V2/V3 overlap at
ST8. The next point (ST9) drops to the neck — cervical plexus
territory (C2–C3) — creating a major zone boundary.

Vascular:
- **Superficial temporal artery (frontal branch):** courses
  through the temporal scalp, directly at or near ST8. This is
  the artery palpated during temporal arteritis assessment.
  BLEEDING RISK.
- Superficial temporal vein accompanies the artery.

Tissue layers:
- Scalp: skin → dense subcutaneous connective tissue → galea
  aponeurotica → subaponeurotic loose areolar tissue →
  pericranium → skull bone
- At ST8: frontalis (anterior) transitions to temporalis fascia
  (lateral). The galea aponeurotica is firmly adherent.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft, Back-Shu,
Front-Mu, Eight Confluent, or Hui-Meeting designation.

**Meeting point (交会穴):** meeting of Foot Yangming (ST), Foot
Shaoyang (GB), and Yang Wei Mai (阳维脉).

Yang Wei Mai trajectory through ST8: BL63 (ankle) → ... →
GB35 (leg) → ... → SI10 → TE15 → GB21 (shoulder) → ascending
neck → **ST8 (temple)** → GB13–GB15 (forehead) → GB16–GB20
(occiput) → GV16 (Fengfu) → GV15. ST8 is the INFLECTION POINT
where the vessel transitions from ascending body trajectory to
cranial distribution — from shoulder to skull.
→ Cross-ref: TE5 Yang Wei Mai (AP-10), GB35 Yang Wei Mai
(AP-11), B01 S3.1

**Temporal arc anchor:** GB4, GB5, GB6 are defined by position
on the ST8→GB7 curved line — at 1/4, 1/2, 3/4 marks (AP-11
Obs. 3). ST8 is the ANTERIOR anchor of this geometric
interpolation. ST8 belongs to two architectural frameworks
simultaneously: the Yang Wei Mai Extraordinary Vessel system
AND the GB temporal arc geometry.
→ Cross-ref: GB4–GB7 temporal arc (AP-11)

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST8 Touwei (头维). Functions: dispels Wind
(祛风), clears the head (清头目), relieves headache (止痛).
Primary temporal headache and migraine point on the ST channel.

**2. Trigger point:** temporalis muscle trigger point (anterior
fibers). Referred pain to the frontal region and upper teeth.
The anterior temporalis at the hairline corner corresponds to
ST8's location. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in migraine, tension-type
headache, and temporal arteritis. The temporal hairline corner
becomes sensitive during frontal/temporal headaches. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Shankha Marma (शंख, "temple") — at the
temple region, classified as Sira Marma (vessel type). Injury
to the temple is traditionally considered dangerous in Ayurveda.
Moderate correspondence. 🟡

**5. Head zone:** V2/V3 overlap territory. The trigeminovascular
system (V1) is the primary migraine pathway — ST8 accesses V3
(and V2) afferents that converge on the trigeminal nucleus with
V1. Indirect migraine relevance.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Lateral Line (LL) — temporal fascia
connects to the LL's cranial portion. Weak. 🔴
**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no data.
**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — scalp.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Marma).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: headache (头痛), dizziness (头晕), eye
  pain (目眶痛), excessive tearing, blurred vision.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: migraine (偏头痛), frontal headache, vertex
  pain, difficulty turning the head.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Headache: frontal, temporal, migraine
- Dizziness, blurred vision
- Excessive lacrimation
- Ophthalmic conditions (secondary to frontal headache)

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng: used in migraine protocols alongside GB8
(Shuaigu), GB20 (Fengchi), TE5 (Waiguan, Yang Wei Mai opener).
The ST8 + GB8 + TE5 combination accesses migraine territory from
local (temporal) and distal (forearm). ST8 is typically the most
ANTERIOR temporal point in headache protocols — GB8 and GB20 are
posterior.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Migraine / headache:**
🟡 Plausible. ST8 is included in multiple acupuncture protocols
for migraine and tension-type headache. Cochrane reviews (Linde
et al.) show acupuncture effective for migraine prophylaxis —
ST8 appears in some included protocols alongside GB20, GB8, LR3.
Individual contribution not isolated.

The Shaoyang migraine dominance pattern (AP-10 Obs. 2): 8/11
top migraine points are Shaoyang (GB+TE). ST8, as a Yangming
point meeting GB and Yang Wei Mai, provides the cross-channel
node where Yangming accesses the Shaoyang migraine territory.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

The temporal region at ST8 shows clinical tenderness during
active migraine and tension-type headache. The superficial
temporal artery becomes prominent and pulsatile during migraine
— this vascular change relates to trigeminovascular activation,
not sensory threshold changes at the acupoint. Formal dynamic
acupoint studies at ST8 have not been conducted.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Migraine / headache:**
V3 auriculotemporal afferents at ST8 → trigeminal nucleus
caudalis. The trigeminal nucleus receives convergent input from
V1 (supraorbital/migraine), V2 (maxillary), and V3 (mandibular/
temporal). ST8 stimulation may modulate the trigeminovascular
system via V3 input to the shared nucleus. The trigemino-
cervical complex (TCC) integrates trigeminal and upper cervical
(C1–C3) inputs — explaining why occipital points (GB20, at C2)
and temporal points (ST8, at V3) both access the migraine
pathway.

Yang Wei Mai's trajectory through ST8 → GB13–GB20 maps the
headache distribution from temporal to occipital. TE5 (Yang Wei
Mai opener) provides distal access to this trajectory — raising
the question of whether Yang Wei Mai represents a FUNCTIONAL
NEURAL PATHWAY rather than a purely traditional concept. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: TE5 (AP-10), A07 S8

**Gap:** no fMRI or neuroimaging study investigating ST8. The
trigeminovascular mechanism is inferred from anatomy and migraine
acupuncture studies.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 5–10 mm subcutaneous (transverse insertion),
directed posteriorly along the scalp or inferiorly toward GB8.
Scalp acupuncture technique. De Qi: local distension, sometimes
radiating across the temporal region.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

EA: ST8→GB8 pair for temporal headache. Low frequency (2 Hz).

Bấm huyệt (B02): circular pressure at the hairline corner.
Self-massage for temporal headache and eye strain.

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa for chronic headache. Caution with
hair (fire risk).

### An toàn

Moderate risk — **superficial temporal artery** directly at or
near ST8.

Precautions:
- **Superficial temporal artery (frontal branch):** courses
  through the temporal scalp at hairline level. Palpate before
  insertion. Puncture → arterial bleeding (scalp wounds bleed
  profusely due to dense connective tissue preventing vessel
  retraction). Apply firm pressure for 5+ minutes.
- Scalp bleeding: richest blood supply of any body surface.
  Even minor vessels produce noticeable bleeding.
- No pneumothorax or organ risk — skull protection.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

> **Batch 2 Architectural Observations (ST5–ST8 — 3 Tier B +
> 1 Tier C)**
>
> **Observation 5 — GHOST POINT #7 (ST6): 3RD TRINITY MOTOR
> PATTERN HYPOTHESIS.**
>
> ST6 (Ghost Bed) is the 7th Ghost Point, belonging to the 3rd
> trinity alongside CV24 (#8, Ghost Market) and PC8 (#9, Ghost
> Cave). The 3rd trinity targets three motor zones that exhibit
> involuntary contraction during acute psychiatric episodes:
>
> | Ghost # | Point | Motor target | Crisis pattern |
> |---------|-------|-------------|----------------|
> | 7 | ST6 | Masseter (V3) | Jaw clenching/trismus |
> | 8 | CV24 | Mentalis/lip (VII) | Lip biting, drooling |
> | 9 | PC8 | Hand intrinsics (median) | Fist clenching |
>
> The trinity may encode OBSERVED motor patterns of acute mania/
> psychosis — trismus, oral self-harm, fist clenching — rather
> than metaphysical concepts. If so, the 13 Ghost Points
> represent a 7th-century BEHAVIORAL OBSERVATION SYSTEM mapped
> onto accessible treatment points. Consistent with all 6
> documented Ghost Points targeting motor-accessible locations.
> 🟡
>
> Ghost Point Atlas status: 6/13 documented (LU11 #2, SP1 #3,
> PC7 #4, ST6 #7, PC8 #9, LI11 #12).
>
> **Observation 6 — ST7+GB3 CROSS-CHANNEL GATE PAIR: ZYGOMATIC
> ARCH AS ANATOMICAL PARTITION.**
>
> ST7 (Lower Gate) and GB3 (Upper Gate) form the only named
> cross-channel gate pair at a bony landmark in the Atlas. The
> zygomatic arch separates two compartments:
>
> | | GB3 (上关) | ST7 (下关) |
> |---|---|---|
> | Position | Above arch | Below arch |
> | Compartment | Temporal fossa | Infratemporal fossa |
> | Key artery | Superficial temporal | Maxillary (deep) |
> | Key muscle | Temporalis | Masseter + pterygoids |
> | V3 access | Deep temporal nerves | Masseteric + auriculotemporal |
>
> Despite being on different channels, both access V3 — the arch
> is a bone partition, not a nerve boundary. The gate pair
> provides TMJ access from opposite sides: GB3 above the joint,
> ST7 below and anterior to it.
>
> **Observation 7 — ST8 DUAL ANCHOR: YANG WEI MAI INFLECTION
> + TEMPORAL ARC GEOMETRY.**
>
> ST8 is the only point in the Atlas that simultaneously serves
> as: (1) a structural node on an Extraordinary Vessel (Yang Wei
> Mai's ascending body → cranial inflection point), and (2) the
> anterior anchor of a geometric interpolation system on another
> channel (GB4–GB6 at 1/4, 1/2, 3/4 on the ST8→GB7 curve). The
> nomenclatural link is striking: 维 (wéi) in both 头维 (Touwei)
> and 阳维脉 (Yang Wei Mai) — a semantic bridge between two
> otherwise independent architectural systems. Whether this
> reflects intentional naming or coincidence is unknown.
>
> **Observation 8 — ST FACE V3 TERRITORY: FIVE-POINT
> MANDIBULAR-TO-TEMPORAL MAPPING (ST4–ST8).**
>
> Five consecutive ST points map the V3 (mandibular) trigeminal
> distribution from oral commissure to temporal hairline:
>
> | Point | V3 branch | Region |
> |-------|----------|--------|
> | ST4 | Buccal nerve | Oral commissure |
> | ST5 | Mental nerve | Mandibular angle |
> | ST6 | Masseteric nerve | Masseter body |
> | ST7 | Auriculotemporal + masseteric | TMJ / zygomatic |
> | ST8 | Auriculotemporal + zygomaticotemporal | Temporal scalp |
>
> Most complete V3 mapping on any single channel. LI has 0 V3
> points (LI19–LI20 are V2 only). GB enters V3 at GB2. Combined
> with ST1–ST3 (V2), the ST face segment spans TWO trigeminal
> divisions across 8 points — the most complete trigeminal
> mapping of any channel.

## ST9 — Nhân Nghênh (人迎, Rén Yíng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Man's Welcome" (人 = person, 迎 = welcome/receive) — on the
lateral neck, at the anterior border of the sternocleidomastoid
(SCM), level with the tip of the laryngeal prominence, where
the common carotid artery pulsation is palpable. The name
carries deep diagnostic significance: in Ling Shu Ch. 9
(終始), the 人迎 pulse at ST9 and the 寸口 pulse at the
radial artery (LU9 area) form a PAIRED DIAGNOSTIC SYSTEM —
人迎 reflects Yang meridian Qi (外来之气), while 寸口 reflects
Yin organ Qi (内生之气). Comparing the two pulse strengths
reveals Yin-Yang excess or deficiency — one of the most
fundamental diagnostic frameworks in classical acupuncture.

ST9 carries THREE special designations:
1. **Window of Heaven (天牖穴)** — one of the ORIGINAL 5 Window of Heaven
points listed in Ling Shu Ch. 2 and 21.
2. **Sea of Qi upper transport (气海之输)** — Ling Shu Ch. 33
(四海篇) identifies ST9 as a transport point of the Sea of
Qi (气海).
3. **Meeting point (交会穴)** of Foot Yangming (ST) and Foot
Shaoyang (GB).

SAFETY-CRITICAL: the common carotid artery lies directly deep,
and the carotid sinus (baroreceptor zone) is immediately
adjacent. Excessive pressure → baroreceptor reflex →
bradycardia, hypotension, syncope.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral side of the
neck, level with the tip of the Adam's apple (laryngeal
prominence), on the anterior border of the sternocleidomastoid
muscle, where the pulsation of the common carotid artery is
felt.

Anatomical landmarks: with the patient supine, head slightly
turned away, the carotid pulse is palpated at the anterior
border of the SCM at the thyroid cartilage prominence level.
The carotid bifurcation (into internal and external carotid
arteries) is at approximately C3-C4 vertebral level — at or
just above the upper thyroid cartilage border.

Spatial relationships:
- LI18 (Futu): at the SAME LEVEL (laryngeal prominence) but
  positioned between the SCM heads rather than at the anterior
  border. Fellow Window of Heaven point, ~1 cm posterolateral.
- ST10 (Shuitu): directly below, at the lower thyroid
  cartilage level.
- SI17 (Tianrong): posterosuperior, below the mandibular angle.
  Also a Window of Heaven point.
- ST9 and LI18 form the ANTERIOR PAIR of the cervical Window of Heaven
  ring, both accessing the carotid sheath from different angles.

Needle depth: 5–8 mm perpendicular, AFTER displacing the
carotid artery laterally with the non-needling hand. Some
traditions advise AGAINST needling ST9 entirely, limiting to
acupressure or palpation. NEVER needle directly into the
carotid pulsation.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Cutaneous: **transverse cervical nerve (C2-C3)** — cervical
  plexus branch providing sensory innervation to the
  anterolateral neck. This marks the FIRST SPINAL NERVE on the
  descending ST channel — ST1-ST8 are entirely in cranial nerve
  territory (CN V2, V3). The transition from V3 (ST8) to C2-C3
  (ST9) is the sharpest cranial-to-spinal nerve boundary on any
  documented channel (see Obs. 9).
- Deep: **carotid sinus nerve (nerve of Hering)** — branch of
  CN IX (glossopharyngeal) supplying baroreceptors in the
  carotid sinus. The clinically critical neural structure at
  ST9 — activation → NTS → vagal efferent → bradycardia +
  vasodilation.
- **Vagus nerve (CN X):** within the carotid sheath, between
  and posterior to the common carotid artery and internal
  jugular vein
- **Superior cervical sympathetic ganglion:** posterior to the
  carotid sheath — sympathetic innervation to the head, eye,
  and cerebral vasculature
- Ansa cervicalis (C1-C3): motor to infrahyoid strap muscles
- Superior laryngeal nerve (vagal branch): internal branch to
  laryngeal mucosa, external branch to cricothyroid muscle
- Spinal segments: C2-C3 (cutaneous) + CN IX, CN X (deep)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular:
- **Common carotid artery** — the DEFINING structure of ST9.
  Lies deep to the anterior SCM border. Bifurcates into
  internal and external carotid arteries at approximately
  C3-C4 level.
- **Carotid sinus** — slight dilation at the proximal internal
  carotid, just above the bifurcation. Contains baroreceptors
  (stretch-sensitive mechanoreceptors) monitoring arterial
  blood pressure via CN IX → NTS.
- **Carotid body** — chemoreceptor cluster at the bifurcation,
  monitoring blood O₂, CO₂, and pH.
- Internal jugular vein — lateral within the carotid sheath
- Superior thyroid artery — first branch of external carotid
→ Cross-ref: A04

Tissue layers: skin → platysma → investing fascia of neck →
anterior border of SCM → carotid sheath (common carotid
artery, internal jugular vein, vagus nerve).

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft, Back-Shu,
Front-Mu, Eight Confluent, or Hui-Meeting designation.

**Window of Heaven (天牖穴)**

One of the ORIGINAL 5 Window of Heaven points in Ling Shu Ch. 2 and 21:
ST9 (人迎), LI18 (扶突), TE16 (天牖), BL10 (天柱), LU3
(天府). The expanded group of ~10 points forms a cervical ring
around the neck at the body-head transition zone (AP-02
Obs. 26). ST9 and LI18 form the ANTERIOR PAIR, both accessing
the carotid sheath region:
- ST9: anterior SCM border, directly ON the carotid pulse
- LI18: between SCM heads, slightly deeper approach

ST9 is the most VASCULAR of all Window of Heaven points — the only one
defined by arterial pulsation. Where other Window of Heaven points (LI18,
SI16, TE16) access the cervical ring's neural structures, ST9
uniquely accesses the VASCULAR component: the carotid artery
delivering blood to the brain. The Window of Heaven concept — treating
conditions where Qi fails to ascend to the head — maps most
literally to ST9: cerebral blood flow IS the ascending flow.

Window of Heaven Atlas status: 5 of ~10 documented (LI18 AP-02, SI16+SI17
AP-06, TE16 AP-10, ST9 here). Remaining: BL10, CV22, GV16,
PC1, LU3 (noted AP-01 but pre-Window of Heaven-architecture).
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 Obs. 26, AP-01 LU3, AP-06 SI16/SI17,
AP-10 TE16

**Sea of Qi — Upper Transport Point (气海之输)**

Ling Shu Ch. 33 (四海篇, "Four Seas") classifies 人迎 (ST9)
as a transport point associated with the Sea of Qi (气海):
"膻中者，为气之海...其输上在于...人迎." The ST channel is
uniquely prominent in this system, hosting transport points
for 3 of 4 seas: ST9 (Sea of Qi), ST30+ST36 (Sea of
Nourishment), ST37+ST39 (Sea of Blood). Only the Sea of
Marrow (Brain) has no ST representation. See Obs. 11.

**Meeting point (交会穴):** meeting of Foot Yangming (ST) and
Foot Shaoyang (GB) at the lateral neck.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST9 Renying (人迎). Functions: regulates
Qi and Blood, benefits the throat, controls rebellious Qi.
Primary indications: sore throat, goiter, asthma, hypertension
(modern addition). The 人迎 pulse is one of the oldest pulse
diagnosis sites in Chinese medicine.

**2. Trigger point:** SCM sternal division TrPs — referred
pain to the throat, periorbital region, and occiput. The
sternal head TrP is near the anterior SCM border at mid-neck
level, overlapping with ST9. Moderate correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** reactive in sore throat, thyroid
tenderness, neck stiffness. The carotid pulsation area becomes
pressure-sensitive during upper respiratory infections and
thyroid disorders.

**4. Marma point:** **Manya Marma (मन्य)** — on the carotid
artery, lateral neck. Manya means "pride/honor" and is
classified as Sira Marma (vascular type). Fatal or
consciousness-altering if severely struck — consistent with
ST9's baroreceptor mechanism (carotid sinus pressure →
syncope). 🟡
→ Cross-ref: C04

**5. Head zone:** C2-C3 dermatome. Cervical viscerotome for
pharynx, larynx, thyroid — supports throat/voice indications.
The carotid sinus baroreceptor afferents (CN IX) are NOT
dermatomal — they represent a specialized visceral afferent
pathway distinct from the Head zone system.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Lateral Line — SCM component connects
sternum/clavicle to mastoid process. ST9 is at the anterior
border of this lateral fascial chain.
→ Cross-ref: A09 S1.3

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no data for the cervical
carotid region.

**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — major vessels deep.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Marma). Manya
Marma provides the strongest non-TCM correspondence — both
traditions independently identified the carotid pulse as a
point of critical significance and similar danger.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Ling Shu Ch. 9 (終始): 人迎 pulse for Yin-Yang diagnostic
  comparison with 寸口 (radial). 人迎 > 寸口 ratios (1:1
  through 4:1) indicate Yang channel excess. See Obs. 10.
- Ling Shu Ch. 21 (寒熱病): Window of Heaven point — treats
  conditions where Qi is obstructed between body and head.
- Ling Shu Ch. 33 (四海): Sea of Qi transport. Excess →
  chest fullness, dyspnea, facial flushing. Deficiency →
  insufficient Qi for speech.
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: sore throat (咽喉肿痛), goiter
  (瘿气), chest fullness, asthma.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: dizziness, sudden loss of voice, throat
  swelling, difficulty swallowing, facial edema.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Benefits the throat: sore throat, tonsillitis, goiter
- Regulates Qi and Blood: hypertension (modern), dizziness,
  facial flushing
- Controls rebellious Qi: asthma, wheezing, hiccup
- Window of Heaven: sudden voice loss, globus sensation

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng: increasingly used in modern Chinese
acupuncture for hypertension management. Combined with LI4
(distal), LI11 (antihypertensive), LR3 (descending Liver
Yang). The 人迎-寸口 pulse comparison method is largely
historical — modern TCM relies primarily on radial pulse at
LU9 for pulse diagnosis. ST9 palpation is still taught but
rarely used as a paired diagnostic comparison.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

**Hypertension:**
🟡 Plausible. Several Chinese RCTs have investigated
acupuncture at ST9 for essential hypertension, with some
showing acute blood pressure reduction during or after
stimulation. The carotid sinus baroreceptor mechanism provides
strong physiological plausibility — stimulation at the carotid
sinus area → baroreceptor activation → NTS →
parasympathetic ↑ / sympathetic ↓ → BP reduction. This
parallels the established medical technique of carotid sinus
massage for supraventricular tachycardia, suggesting
acupuncture at ST9 may access a similar reflex arc. However,
evidence quality is limited: small sample sizes, inconsistent
protocols, and lack of adequate sham controls.

**Goiter/thyroid disorders:**
🔴 Unvalidated. Traditional indication with no isolating study.

**Throat conditions:**
🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST9 from multi-point throat
protocols.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal sensitization data for ST9. The carotid pulse area
may show altered sensitivity during hypertension (bounding
pulse → greater arterial wall stretch → baroreceptor
adaptation) or during carotid sinus hypersensitivity syndrome
(exaggerated response to minimal pressure). Whether these
represent true dynamic acupoint behavior or general vascular
physiology is unstudied.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Hypertension/cardiovascular effects:**
The most mechanistically clear pathway at ST9:
Stimulation → carotid sinus baroreceptors (mechanoreceptors
in arterial wall) → carotid sinus nerve (Hering's nerve, CN
IX branch) → nucleus tractus solitarius (NTS) → dual efferent
output:
1. Parasympathetic: NTS → dorsal motor nucleus of vagus →
   vagal efferents → heart → bradycardia
2. Sympathoinhibitory: NTS → CVLM → inhibits RVLM → reduces
   sympathetic outflow → vasodilation

Net effect: heart rate reduction + vasodilation → blood
pressure decrease. This is the SAME reflex arc used in
clinical carotid sinus massage for SVT. ST9 has the most
DIRECT NTS access of any documented acupoint: CN IX projects
directly to the NTS from the carotid sinus without spinal
cord relay. Limb-based NTS access points (PC6 via median
nerve, LI11 via radial nerve) require a spinal cord
intermediary, making their pathways longer and more diffuse.
🟡

**Throat/voice effects (sore throat, goiter):**
C2-C3 segmental: cervical viscerotome includes pharynx,
larynx, thyroid. Stimulation → C2-C3 afferents → cervical
dorsal horn → modulation of pharyngeal/laryngeal reflexes.
Superior laryngeal nerve (vagal branch) at this level provides
additional laryngeal access. Proximity to the superior thyroid
artery may allow local vascular effects at the thyroid gland.
🟡

**Window of Heaven (head/brain effects):**
ST9 is the most VASCULAR Window of Heaven point — directly accessing
cerebral blood flow via the carotid artery. The baroreceptor
pathway (above) provides one mechanism. A second: sympathetic
chain modulation at the superior cervical ganglion (posterior
to carotid sheath) could alter cerebral vasomotor tone.
Entirely theoretical for acupuncture at this point. 🔴

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Hào châm (B06): 5–8 mm perpendicular insertion, AFTER
displacing the carotid artery laterally with the non-needling
hand. Deqi may involve a distending or pulsatile sensation.
Duration 15–20 minutes with CAREFUL MONITORING — observe for
signs of carotid sinus reflex (dizziness, bradycardia,
pallor). Remove needle immediately if patient reports visual
changes, lightheadedness, or chest discomfort.

Chỉ áp (B07): acupressure at ST9 requires extreme caution.
Light sustained pressure only — NEVER apply bilateral carotid
pressure simultaneously (risk of cerebral ischemia). Classical
人迎 pulse palpation (for diagnostic comparison with radial
pulse) is the traditional form of ST9 acupressure.

Cứu (B08): moxa rarely used at ST9 due to superficial carotid
artery position. Not classically prohibited but practically
avoided.

Electroacupuncture: CONTRAINDICATED at ST9 in most protocols.
Electrical stimulation at the carotid sinus → uncontrolled
baroreceptor activation → potentially dangerous bradycardia or
asystole.

### An toàn

**HIGH RISK** — one of the most dangerous acupoints on ANY
channel due to direct carotid artery access.

Precautions:
- **Carotid sinus reflex:** the PRIMARY safety concern.
  Pressure on the carotid sinus → baroreceptor activation →
  bradycardia, hypotension, syncope. In patients with carotid
  sinus hypersensitivity, even gentle palpation can cause
  profound bradycardia or asystole.
- **Carotid artery puncture:** the common carotid artery is
  the defining deep structure. Puncture → arterial bleeding,
  potential hematoma compressing the airway, or (rarely)
  arterial dissection.
- **Internal jugular vein:** lateral in the carotid sheath.
- **Vagus nerve:** within the carotid sheath. Direct
  stimulation → vagal response (bradycardia, nausea).

**Absolute contraindications:**
- Bilateral needling simultaneously (cerebral ischemia risk)
- Known carotid sinus hypersensitivity
- Carotid artery stenosis or plaque (stroke risk from plaque
  dislodgement)
- Patients on anticoagulants (arterial puncture bleeding risk)
- Electroacupuncture at or near ST9

No classical moxa prohibition. No specific pregnancy
contraindication (but general carotid risks apply).

---

## ST10 — Thủy Đột (水突, Shuǐ Tū)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Water Prominence" (水 = water, 突 = prominence/protrusion)
— on the anterior neck, at the anterior border of the SCM,
below ST9, at the midpoint between ST9 (laryngeal prominence)
and ST11 (sternal end of clavicle). The name references the
thyroid cartilage ("prominence") and the point's classical
association with swallowing disorders ("water" passage through
the throat).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the anterior border of
the sternocleidomastoid muscle, at the midpoint between ST9
(Renying) and ST11 (Qishe).

Anatomical landmarks: at the lower border of the thyroid
cartilage, on the anterior SCM border. Between ST9 above
(laryngeal prominence level) and ST11 below (suprasternal
notch level). The thyroid gland's lateral lobe lies deep and
medial at this level.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique.
Avoid deep insertion toward the midline (thyroid gland).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Transverse cervical nerve (C2-C3) — cervical plexus,
  cutaneous to anterolateral neck (same as ST9)
- Deeper: ansa cervicalis branches (C1-C3), vagus nerve in
  carotid sheath, sympathetic chain posterior to sheath
- Spinal segments: C2-C3
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: common carotid artery (within carotid sheath, deep
to SCM — pre-bifurcation at this lower level), internal
jugular vein, superior thyroid artery.

Tissue layers: skin → platysma → SCM anterior border →
carotid sheath.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST10 Shuitu — cough, asthma, sore throat,
difficulty swallowing, goiter.
**2. Trigger point:** SCM sternal division — similar TrP
pattern to ST9 but lower on the muscle. Moderate. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** reactive in throat pain, thyroid tenderness.
**4. Marma:** near Nila Marma territory (throat flanking). 🟡
**5. Head zone:** C2-C3 cervical viscerotome. Pharynx/larynx
segmental correspondence.
**6–8:** Anatomy Trains (Lateral Line, SCM) / no neurogenic
data / cupping N/A.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): cough (咳嗽), asthma
(气喘), sore throat (咽喉肿痛), difficulty swallowing
(咽不下), goiter (瘿气).

Nguồn hiện đại: sore throat, asthma, goiter. Supporting
point for throat conditions, often combined with ST9, LI18,
CV22. Less commonly selected than ST9 or LI18 for neck
conditions.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST10. Appears rarely in
multi-point throat protocols.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — carotid sheath structures lie deep to the SCM.
Risk is LOWER than ST9 because ST10 is below the carotid
bifurcation and further from the carotid sinus baroreceptors.

Precautions:
- **Carotid artery:** deep to SCM. Puncture → arterial
  bleeding (without baroreceptor reflex risk of ST9).
- **Thyroid gland:** lateral lobe lies deep and medial. Deep
  medially-directed needling → thyroid puncture.
- Shallow perpendicular needling is safe.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST11 — Khí Xá (气舍, Qì Shè)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Qi Dwelling" (气 = qi/breath, 舍 = dwelling/abode) — at the
superior border of the sternal end of the clavicle, between
the sternal and clavicular heads of the SCM. The name
references the point's location at the throat where breath
(qi) resides — above the trachea and near the voice box.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): at the superior border of
the sternal end of the clavicle, between the sternal head and
the clavicular head of the sternocleidomastoid muscle.

Anatomical landmarks: find the sternal notch. Move laterally
to the junction of the clavicle with the sternum. ST11 is in
the depression between the two SCM heads at this level.
Between ST10 above and ST12 below. The position is similar
to LI18 (between SCM heads) but at a LOWER level — LI18 is
at the laryngeal prominence, ST11 is at the clavicular
sternal junction.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm oblique, directed laterally. Avoid deep
perpendicular insertion toward the midline (trachea, great
vessels).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Supraclavicular nerves (C3-C4)** — cervical plexus
  branches providing cutaneous sensation to the lower neck,
  upper chest, and shoulder. This marks a SEGMENTAL SHIFT from
  C2-C3 (ST9-ST10) to C3-C4 — the progression continues the
  caudal descent through cervical dermatomes.
- Deeper: ansa cervicalis (C1-C3), vagus nerve within carotid
  sheath, approaching subclavian vessels
- Spinal segments: C3-C4
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: common carotid artery deep to SCM (pre-bifurcation),
internal jugular vein, inferior thyroid artery (from
thyrocervical trunk).

Tissue layers: skin → platysma → between SCM heads → carotid
sheath (deeper).

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST11 Qishe — sore throat, goiter, neck stiffness,
hiccup, asthma.
**2. Trigger point:** SCM between heads — referred to throat
and sternal region. Similar to LI18's TrP zone. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** reactive in torticollis, throat conditions.
**4. Marma:** no specific supraclavicular Marma. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** C3-C4 dermatome. Transitioning from cervical
viscerotome toward upper thoracic.
**6–8:** Anatomy Trains (Lateral Line) / no neurogenic data /
cupping N/A.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): sore throat (咽喉肿痛),
goiter (瘿气), neck stiffness (颈项强急), hiccup (呃逆),
asthma (气喘), scrofula (瘰疬).

Nguồn hiện đại: sore throat, goiter, neck pain, hiccup.
Supporting point rarely used independently — adjacent to both
LI18 (Window of Heaven, same SCM-heads position at higher level) and ST12
(critical crossing point below). ST11 functions primarily as
a transitional point between the cervical ring (ST9-ST10)
and the supraclavicular hub (ST12).

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST11.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — transitional zone between the carotid sheath
territory (above) and the supraclavicular vascular territory
(below).

Precautions:
- **Common carotid artery:** deep to SCM, same as ST9-ST10.
- **Subclavian vessels:** beginning to appear at this level
  as the anatomy transitions toward the supraclavicular fossa
  (ST12). Deep inferior-directed needling risks reaching
  subclavian structures.
- **Trachea:** medial. Deep midline-directed needling →
  tracheal puncture (rare with correct angle).
- Shallow oblique needling directed laterally is safe.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST12 — Khuyết Bồn (缺盆, Quē Pén)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Empty Basin" or "Broken Bowl" (缺 = broken/deficient,
盆 = basin/bowl) — in the supraclavicular fossa, the
depression above the clavicle that resembles a broken bowl.
ST12 is the most critical CROSSING POINT in the entire
12-channel system: classical texts state that ALL YANG
CHANNELS converge here — Foot Yangming (ST), Hand Yangming
(LI), Hand Taiyang (SI), Hand Shaoyang (TE), and Foot
Shaoyang (GB). Five Yang channels crossing at a single point
is architecturally unique; no other acupoint hosts this many
channel intersections.

The supraclavicular fossa is where the body's most critical
neurovascular structures transit between neck and trunk:
the brachial plexus (C5-T1), subclavian artery and vein,
and the lung apex. This makes ST12 simultaneously the most
CONNECTED and most DANGEROUS point on the ST channel.
Classical texts specifically warn about deep needling danger.

This is the FIRST documentation of the "all-Yang crossing at
缺盆" concept in the Atlas — none of the completed Yang
channel files (LI, SI, TE, GB) reference ST12 as a crossing
point in their own entries, despite it being one of the most
cited classical claims about channel architecture.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the supraclavicular
fossa, at the midpoint of the superior border of the
clavicle, 4 cun lateral to the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: with the patient seated or supine,
the supraclavicular fossa is the depression above the
clavicle, lateral to the SCM. ST12 is at the midpoint of
this depression. The fossa is bounded by the SCM medially,
the trapezius laterally, and the clavicle inferiorly.

Spatial relationships:
- ST11 (Qishe): above, between SCM heads at the clavicular
  sternal junction.
- ST13 (Qihu): below the clavicle, 4 cun lateral to midline
  — the transition from neck to chest.
- GB21 (Jianjing): at the shoulder summit (midpoint of C7
  spinous process to acromion), more posterior and superior.
  GB21 is the CLOSEST documented point from another completed
  channel — also a meeting point (GB+TE+ST+Yang Wei Mai)
  and also carries pneumothorax risk from lung apex.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm OBLIQUE, directed inferolaterally
ALONG the superior surface of the clavicle. NEVER
perpendicular. NEVER directed inferiorly toward the lung
apex.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- Cutaneous: **supraclavicular nerves (C3-C4)** — cervical
  plexus branches providing sensation to the supraclavicular
  fossa, upper chest, and shoulder
- Deep: **BRACHIAL PLEXUS (C5-T1)** — the trunks emerge
  between the anterior and middle scalene muscles and
  traverse the supraclavicular fossa. This plexus provides
  ALL motor and sensory innervation to the upper limb. At
  ST12, the three trunks (superior C5-C6, middle C7,
  inferior C8-T1) are accessible — concentrating the nerve
  supply for the three arm Yang channels: radial nerve
  (LI, TE), ulnar nerve (SI). See Obs. 12.
- **Phrenic nerve (C3-C5):** descends on the anterior
  surface of the anterior scalene. Damage → diaphragmatic
  paralysis.
- Spinal segments: C3-C4 (cutaneous) + C5-T1 (brachial
  plexus, deep)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular:
- **Subclavian artery:** passes behind the anterior scalene
  and through the supraclavicular fossa. Puncture → arterial
  hemorrhage.
- **Subclavian vein:** anterior and inferior, in front of
  the anterior scalene. Puncture → venous bleeding, air
  embolism risk.
- **Thoracic duct (LEFT SIDE):** arches over the left
  subclavian artery to drain into the left subclavian-IJV
  junction. Left-side deep needling → thoracic duct injury
  → chylothorax.
- Transverse cervical and suprascapular arteries (branches
  of the thyrocervical trunk).
→ Cross-ref: A04

Tissue layers: skin → platysma → supraclavicular fat pad →
omohyoid muscle (inferior belly crosses) → scalene muscles
(anterior and middle) → lung apex (deepest layer). The lung
apex extends 2-3 cm ABOVE the clavicle into the
supraclavicular fossa — this is why deep perpendicular
needling causes pneumothorax.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft, Back-Shu,
Front-Mu, Eight Confluent, Hui-Meeting, Window of Heaven, or
Sea designation.

**All-Yang Channel Crossing Point (交会穴)**

The definitive crossing point of the 12-channel system.
Classical texts (Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing, Zhenjiu Dacheng)
describe the trajectories of 5 Yang channels converging at
the supraclavicular fossa:

| Channel | Six-Channel Family | Trajectory through ST12 |
|---------|-------------------|------------------------|
| **ST** (Foot Yangming) | Yangming | Host — descends from neck to chest |
| **LI** (Hand Yangming) | Yangming | Ascending: hand → arm → shoulder → through supraclavicular to neck/face |
| **SI** (Hand Taiyang) | Taiyang | Ascending: hand → arm → shoulder → through supraclavicular to neck/face |
| **TE** (Hand Shaoyang) | Shaoyang | Ascending: hand → arm → shoulder → through supraclavicular to neck/ear |
| **GB** (Foot Shaoyang) | Shaoyang | Descending: head → neck → supraclavicular → lateral trunk → leg |

The anatomical basis for this convergence: the supraclavicular
fossa is the bottleneck where NECK MEETS TRUNK. All ascending
channel trajectories from the arm and all descending
trajectories from the head must pass through this narrow
corridor. The brachial plexus (C5-T1) passes through ST12,
providing ALL nerve supply to the 3 arm Yang channels —
making the channel crossing neurologically literal. See
Obs. 12.

**Foot Taiyang (BL)** — the 6th Yang channel — is NOT
consistently listed as crossing ST12. The BL trajectory is
posterior (occiput → nape → paravertebral → sacrum → leg),
largely bypassing the anterior supraclavicular region.

Note: none of the completed Yang channel entries (LI AP-02,
SI AP-06, TE AP-10, GB AP-11) reference ST12 in their
own files, despite this being one of the most cited classical
claims. The crossing concept is documented HERE for the
first time in the Atlas.
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI trajectory, AP-06 SI shoulder-neck,
AP-10 TE supraclavicular, AP-11 GB neck descent

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST12 Quepen (缺盆). Functions: descends
Lung Qi, regulates Stomach Qi, unbinds the chest. Primary
indications: cough, asthma, sore throat, shoulder/neck pain,
chest fullness.

**2. Trigger point:** **anterior scalene TrP** — the anterior
scalene TrP refers pain to the anterior chest, shoulder, arm,
and radial hand. Located in the supraclavicular fossa
overlapping ST12. Associated with thoracic outlet syndrome
(TOS) symptoms. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** spontaneously tender in respiratory
disease (scalene tension in asthma exacerbation), upper limb
conditions (TOS, brachial plexopathy), and shoulder-neck
tension. The supraclavicular fossa is a standard palpation
site in clinical examination.

**4. Marma point:** no precisely corresponding named Marma at
the supraclavicular fossa. Amsa Marma is at the shoulder
joint (more lateral). 🔴

**5. Head zone:** C3-C4 (supraclavicular nerve) + C5-T1
(brachial plexus, deep). The lung viscerotome is T1-T4 — the
lung apex at ST12 provides access to the lower boundary of
this viscerotome. Partial segmental overlap for respiratory
indications.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** **Deep Front Line** — the scalene
muscles (anterior, middle, posterior) are a key component,
connecting the cervical spine to the first two ribs. ST12
sits directly over this deep fascial connection, linking neck
to thorax through the deepest anterior fascia.
→ Cross-ref: A09 S1.3

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** no specific data.

**8. Cupping point:** NOT applicable — lung apex, major
vessels immediately deep.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy Trains).
The scalene TrP correspondence is one of the stronger in the
neck region — both TCM and trigger point traditions
independently identify the supraclavicular fossa as a
treatment site for upper limb and respiratory conditions.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Zhenjiu Jiayi Jing: cough (咳嗽), asthma (气喘), sore
  throat (咽喉肿痛), chest fullness (胸满), shoulder pain
  (肩痛), abdominal distension. Specific depth warnings for
  this point.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: chest pain, diaphragmatic spasm, neck and
  shoulder stiffness, scrofula.
- Ling Shu: notes the crossing of multiple Yang channel
  trajectories at the supraclavicular region.
- Multiple classical texts encode the DANGER of deep needling
  at ST12 — one of the earliest anatomical safety warnings in
  medical literature.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- Descends rebellious Qi: cough, asthma, chest fullness
- Benefits the throat: sore throat, tonsillitis
- Relaxes the sinews: shoulder/neck pain, arm pain
- Clinical importance often focused on CROSSING status rather
  than specific local indications

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng: used for upper respiratory conditions,
shoulder-neck syndromes, and thoracic outlet symptoms.
Combined with LI4+LI11 for upper limb pain, GB21+TE15 for
shoulder conditions, CV22 for throat. The crossing-point
status gives ST12 theoretical importance for treating multiple
Yang channels simultaneously. However, most practitioners
approach ST12 with extreme caution or avoid needling entirely
due to pneumothorax risk, preferring acupressure or moxa.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST12 for any indication.
The pneumothorax risk discourages clinical investigation.
ST12 appears in some multi-point protocols for shoulder pain
and respiratory conditions, but its individual contribution
is not assessed.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data for ST12. The supraclavicular fossa
shows clinical tenderness during acute respiratory disease
(scalene muscle tension in asthma exacerbation) and thoracic
outlet syndrome (scalene spasm → tenderness over brachial
plexus). Whether this represents dynamic acupoint behavior
or musculoskeletal pathology is not distinguished.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích

**Respiratory effects (cough, asthma):**
Multiple converging pathways:
1. **Phrenic nerve (C3-C5):** motor nerve to the diaphragm
   descends on the anterior scalene at ST12. Stimulation →
   phrenic afferent activation → modulation of respiratory
   rhythm via the medullary respiratory center. 🟡
2. **Lung apex access:** the visceral pleura and lung apex
   are within the deepest tissue layer. T1-T4 intercostal
   nerve connections to brachial plexus roots provide
   segmental reflex pathways. 🔴
3. **Vagal access:** indirect — vagus nerve from the carotid
   sheath above continues inferiorly near this area. 🔴

**Upper limb effects (shoulder, arm pain):**
Brachial plexus (C5-T1) → ALL upper limb motor and sensory
nerves. Stimulation at the plexus trunks → activation of
upper limb nerve pathways. This is the neural equivalent of
treating all three arm channels simultaneously — the
brachial plexus IS the common trunk before the channels
"diverge" into radial (LI, TE), ulnar (SI), and median
(PC, HT) territories. 🟡

**Gap:** No mechanism study specific to ST12 acupuncture.
The brachial plexus access hypothesis is the strongest
mechanistic basis but entirely untested.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Hào châm (B06): 5–10 mm OBLIQUE insertion, directed
inferolaterally along the superior clavicular surface.
NEVER perpendicular. Deqi may involve radiating or
electrical sensation down the arm (brachial plexus
stimulation). Duration 10–15 minutes.

Many classical and modern texts advise extreme caution or
avoidance of needling at ST12. In teaching contexts, ST12
is often used as a reference point for locating other chest
points rather than as a treatment point itself.

Chỉ áp (B07): acupressure is the SAFEST stimulation method
at ST12. Moderate pressure in the supraclavicular fossa
relaxes the scalene muscles. Commonly used as a clinical
alternative to needling.

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa at ST12 occasionally used for
shoulder-neck cold-pattern conditions.

### An toàn

**EXTREME RISK** — one of the most dangerous acupoints in
the entire Atlas, comparable to or exceeding ST9 in overall
severity due to the convergence of multiple critical
structures.

Precautions:
- **PNEUMOTHORAX:** the PRIMARY safety concern. The lung apex
  extends 2-3 cm above the clavicle into the supraclavicular
  fossa. Deep perpendicular or inferiorly-directed needling →
  puncture of visceral pleura → pneumothorax. Symptoms:
  sudden chest pain, dyspnea, reduced breath sounds. Can
  progress to tension pneumothorax (life-threatening).
  Classical texts recognized this: "缺盆中...中肺" (Zhenjiu
  Jiayi Jing). The 6th pneumothorax-risk point in the Atlas
  (after LI16, LI17 area, GB21, and chest points).
- **Subclavian artery:** deep to the scalene muscles.
  Puncture → high-pressure arterial hemorrhage, potentially
  forming an expanding hematoma compressing the brachial
  plexus and airway.
- **Subclavian vein:** anterior and inferior. Puncture →
  venous bleeding, AIR EMBOLISM risk (negative intrathoracic
  pressure can draw air through the needle track).
- **Brachial plexus (C5-T1):** direct nerve injury → upper
  limb weakness, numbness, or neuropathy.
- **Thoracic duct (LEFT SIDE):** injury → chylothorax
  (lymph accumulation in pleural space).
- **Phrenic nerve (C3-C5):** injury → unilateral
  diaphragmatic paralysis → dyspnea.

**Maximum safe depth:** 5–10 mm oblique only. Some
authorities recommend ≤5 mm. Deep needling is NEVER
justified at ST12.

No specific pregnancy contraindication beyond the extreme
general risk. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

> **Batch 3 Architectural Observations (ST9–ST12 — 2 Tier B +
> 2 Tier C)**
>
> **Observation 9 — CRANIAL-SPINAL NERVE BOUNDARY (ST8→ST9):
> V3→C2 = MINIMAL POSSIBLE GAP.**
>
> ST8 (auriculotemporal nerve, CN V3) → ST9 (transverse
> cervical nerve, C2-C3) marks the sharpest cranial-to-spinal
> nerve transition on any documented channel. The jump from the
> last trigeminal division (V3) to the first cervical dermatome
> (C2) is the MINIMAL POSSIBLE GAP — C1 has no significant
> cutaneous branch in most individuals, making V3→C2 the
> smallest boundary between cranial and spinal sensory
> territories. The ST channel transitions at the exact
> anatomical boundary: temporal scalp (V3) → anterolateral
> neck (C2-C3) with no intermediate point.
>
> Compare documented channels:
> - GB: GB20 (C1-C2 suboccipital) → GB21 (C3-C4) — gradual
>   via the suboccipital region
> - LI: stays in cranial territory at face (V2), channel ends
> - TE: stays in cranial territory at face (V1), channel ends
> - SI: SI19 (V3) → channel ends — no spinal transition
>
> ST is the only documented channel that crosses the cranial-
> spinal boundary within its own point sequence. The three
> arm Yang channels end at the head (cranial territory); GB
> transitions gradually through the suboccipital region. ST
> alone makes the jump in one step — from V3 temporal scalp
> directly to C2-C3 anterolateral neck.
>
> **Observation 10 — 人迎-寸口 DUAL-PULSE DIAGNOSTIC AXIS:
> TWO ARTERIAL PULSE POINTS ON THE TAIYIN-YANGMING PAIR.**
>
> ST9 (人迎, carotid pulse) and LU9 (寸口, radial pulse) form
> the classical Yin-Yang pulse comparison system (Ling Shu
> Ch. 9, 終始). Both are PULSE PALPATION POINTS defined by
> arterial access:
>
> | Point | Artery | Pulse represents | Channel family |
> |-------|--------|-----------------|----------------|
> | ST9 | Common carotid | Yang Qi (六腑之大络) | Yangming |
> | LU9 | Radial | Yin Qi (五脏之大络) | Taiyin |
>
> Both belong to the Taiyin-Yangming "opening" pair — the
> first Yin-Yang pair in the Six-Channel progression. The
> tradition selected the two most accessible ARTERIAL PULSE
> sites in the body (carotid and radial) and placed them on
> complementary Yin-Yang channels for paired diagnostic
> comparison. LU9 is already documented (AP-01) as the primary
> pulse diagnosis point; ST9 provides the Yang complement.
> Modern pulse diagnosis uses the radial pulse exclusively —
> the 人迎-寸口 comparison is largely historical but reveals
> the classical system's anatomical logic: two pulse sites,
> two complementary channels, two aspects of systemic Qi.
>
> **Observation 11 — FOUR SEAS: ST CHANNEL HOSTS TRANSPORT
> POINTS FOR 3 OF 4 SEAS.**
>
> Ling Shu Ch. 33 (四海篇) assigns the ST channel transport
> points for three of the four vital-substance seas:
>
> | Sea | ST transport point(s) |
> |-----|----------------------|
> | Sea of Qi (气海) | ST9 (人迎) |
> | Sea of Nourishment (水谷之海) | ST30 (气冲) + ST36 (足三里) |
> | Sea of Blood (十二经之海) | ST37 (上巨虚) + ST39 (下巨虚) |
> | Sea of Marrow (髓海) | — (no ST representation) |
>
> Five ST points across three seas — more than any other
> channel. Only the Sea of Marrow (Brain) has no ST
> representation. This concentration supports the classical
> designation of the Stomach as "source of post-natal Qi"
> (后天之本): the ST channel provides transport access for
> Qi, Nourishment, AND Blood — the three vital substances
> that post-natal physiology produces and distributes.
>
> **Observation 12 — ST12 ALL-YANG CONVERGENCE: BRACHIAL
> PLEXUS AS NEURAL BASIS FOR ARM CHANNEL CROSSING.**
>
> The classical claim that all Yang channels cross at ST12
> (缺盆) has a precise neural correlate: the brachial plexus
> (C5-T1) passes through the supraclavicular fossa, providing
> ALL nerve supply to the upper limb. The three arm Yang
> channels' primary nerves originate from this plexus:
>
> | Arm Yang channel | Primary nerve | Plexus origin |
> |-----------------|--------------|---------------|
> | LI (Hand Yangming) | Radial nerve | C5-T1 (posterior cord) |
> | SI (Hand Taiyang) | Ulnar nerve | C8-T1 (medial cord) |
> | TE (Hand Shaoyang) | Radial + posterior interosseous | C5-T1 (posterior cord) |
>
> The brachial plexus at ST12 is the neural ORIGIN point
> before the three arm Yang channels "diverge" into their
> respective nerve territories. The crossing is neurologically
> literal: all three arm channel nerves are accessible at a
> single point. For the two foot Yang channels (ST and GB),
> the crossing is trajectory-based — both descend from head
> through neck and transit the supraclavicular corridor.
>
> Notably, the three arm Yin channels' nerves ALSO originate
> from the brachial plexus (median for LU and PC, ulnar for
> HT) — but the classical tradition does NOT describe Yin
> channel crossing at ST12. This selectivity may reflect a
> trajectory observation: Yang channels travel the EXTERNAL
> body surface and visibly cross the supraclavicular region,
> while Yin channels travel the internal/medial surface and
> enter the thorax without crossing 缺盆. The neural anatomy
> does not discriminate Yin from Yang — but the surface
> observation does.

---

## ST13 — Khí Hộ (气户, Qì Hù)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Qi Door" (气 = qi, 户 = door/household) — the point where
Qi enters the chest. Located at the infraclavicular region,
directly below the midpoint of the clavicle. ST13 marks the
TRANSITION from the neck segment (ST9–ST12) to the chest
segment. The "door" imagery is topographically literal:
passing below the clavicle, the channel enters the anterior
chest wall where the thoracic cavity begins.

ST13 is the first point below the clavicle on the ST channel.
Its immediate superior neighbor ST12 (缺盆) is the most
dangerous point on the channel; ST13 carries pneumothorax
risk but lacks the neurovascular complexity of the
supraclavicular fossa.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the anterior chest,
at the lower border of the middle of the clavicle, 4 cun
lateral to the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: at the infraclavicular fossa, directly
below the midpoint of the clavicle, on the midclavicular
line. Between ST12 above (supraclavicular fossa) and ST14
below (1st intercostal space).

Needle depth: 5–10 mm oblique insertion, directed
inferolaterally. Avoid deep perpendicular insertion toward
the lung apex.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Supraclavicular nerves (C3-C4)** — overlap from cervical
  plexus (same as ST11-ST12). Cutaneous sensation of the
  infraclavicular region.
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T1 intercostal nerve** —
  emerging near the first intercostal space. Marks the
  transition from cervical plexus (C3-C4) to intercostal
  nerve territory (T1+).
- Deeper: lateral pectoral nerve (C5-C7), medial pectoral
  nerve (C8-T1), subclavius nerve (C5-C6)
- Spinal segments: C3-C4 / T1
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: branches of the internal thoracic (mammary) artery,
cephalic vein laterally, thoracoacromial artery branches.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → pectoralis major
→ subclavius muscle → first rib → lung apex (deep).

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST13 Qihu — cough (咳嗽), asthma (气喘), chest
fullness (胸满), hiccup (呃逆).
**2. Trigger point:** pectoralis major (clavicular head) —
trigger points in this region refer to anterior chest and
anterior deltoid. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** reactive in chest tightness, respiratory
conditions.
**4. Marma:** no specific infraclavicular Marma. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** C3-C4 / T1 transition zone. Upper lung
viscerotome begins at T1.
**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line (SFL) runs
through the sternal head of pectoralis major directly over
the ST chest line at 4 cun lateral. → Cross-ref: A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** no specific data. 🔴
**8. Cupping:** chest cupping not specific to this level.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): cough (咳嗽), asthma
(气喘), chest fullness/pain (胸满胸痛), hiccup (呃逆),
shortness of breath (少气不足以息).

Nguồn hiện đại: chest pain, cough, asthma. Supporting point
for upper respiratory conditions. Rarely used independently —
adjacent to ST12 (above) and ST14 (below), both with similar
indications.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST13.

### An toàn

Moderate-to-high risk — pneumothorax zone.

Precautions:
- **Lung apex:** extends 2–3 cm above the first rib. Deep
  perpendicular insertion risks pneumothorax, though the
  subclavius muscle and first rib provide some protection.
- Oblique needling directed inferolaterally is safe.
- Risk LOWER than ST12 (no brachial plexus, no subclavian
  vessels directly at this point).

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST14 — Khố Phòng (库房, Kù Fáng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Storehouse" (库 = storehouse/treasury, 房 = room/chamber) —
the chest as storage chamber for the vital organs. Located
in the 1st intercostal space (ICS), 4 cun lateral to the
anterior midline, on the midclavicular line. Part of the
homogeneous ST chest group (ST13–ST18) where points are
differentiated primarily by intercostal level.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the first intercostal
space, 4 cun lateral to the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: locate the clavicle, then the 1st rib
below it. ST14 is in the space between the 1st and 2nd ribs,
on the midclavicular line. Between ST13 above
(infraclavicular) and ST15 below (2nd ICS).

Needle depth: 5–10 mm oblique insertion along the
intercostal space. Insert superior to the lower rib border
to avoid the intercostal neurovascular bundle.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T1 intercostal nerve** —
  1st intercostal nerve. T1 also contributes to the brachial
  plexus via the lower trunk (C8-T1), creating overlap
  between intercostal and limb innervation.
- Deeper: medial and lateral pectoral nerves (motor to
  pectoralis major)
- Spinal segment: T1
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: perforating branches of the internal thoracic
(mammary) artery and vein (anterior intercostal supply).

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → pectoralis
major → external intercostal → internal intercostal →
innermost intercostal → endothoracic fascia → parietal
pleura.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST14 Kufang — cough (咳嗽), chest pain (胸痛),
chest fullness with phlegm (胸满多痰).
**2. Trigger point:** pectoralis major (sternal head, upper
portion) — TrPs refer to anterior chest. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** reactive in respiratory distress, chest wall
pain.
**4. Marma:** no specific 1st ICS Marma. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** T1 dermatome. Upper lung viscerotome.
Cardiac viscerotome begins at T1.
**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line, pectoralis
major sternal head. → Cross-ref: A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** no specific data. 🔴
**8. Cupping:** not level-specific.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): cough (咳嗽), chest pain
(胸痛), chest fullness (胸满), copious phlegm (多痰).

Nguồn hiện đại: chest pain, cough with copious phlegm.
Supporting point within the ST chest group. Rarely used
independently.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST14.

### An toàn

High risk — pneumothorax zone.

Precautions:
- **Pleural cavity:** directly deep to the intercostal
  muscles at the 1st ICS. Chest wall at 4 cun lateral is
  typically 15–30 mm thick; as thin as 10–15 mm in thin
  individuals.
- Insert OBLIQUELY along the intercostal space, superior
  to the lower rib border (avoiding the intercostal
  neurovascular bundle in the costal groove of the rib
  above).
- NEVER deep perpendicular insertion.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST15 — Ốc Ế (屋翳, Wū Yì)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Room Screen" or "Roof Screen" (屋 = house/roof, 翳 = screen/
shade) — the ribs as the "roof" covering the chest organs.
Located in the 2nd intercostal space (ICS), 4 cun lateral to
