the anterior midline. The T2 intercostal nerve's lateral
cutaneous branch is the intercostobrachial nerve, which
provides sensation to the medial arm and axilla — the
anatomical basis for cardiac pain referral to the left arm.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the second intercostal
space, 4 cun lateral to the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: locate the sternal angle (junction of
manubrium and body of sternum) — this identifies the 2nd rib.
ST15 is in the space below the 2nd rib, on the midclavicular
line. Between ST14 above (1st ICS) and ST16 below (3rd ICS).

Needle depth: 5–10 mm oblique insertion.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T2 intercostal nerve** —
  T2 lateral cutaneous branch = **intercostobrachial nerve**,
  providing sensation to the medial arm and axilla. This is
  the primary pathway for cardiac pain referral to the arm.
- Spinal segment: T2
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07 (cardiac viscerotome T1–T5)

Vascular: perforating branches of internal thoracic artery;
T2 intercostal vessels.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → pectoralis
major → external intercostal → internal intercostal →
innermost intercostal → parietal pleura.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST15 Wuyi — chest pain (胸痛), cough (咳嗽),
breast distension/pain (乳痈).
**2. Trigger point:** pectoralis major (sternal head, mid-
portion) — TrPs at this level may reproduce cardiac-type
pain via the intercostobrachial pathway. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** reactive in chest tightness, intercostal
neuralgia.
**4. Marma:** Stanarohita (upper breast) possibly at this
level. 🔴 (uncertain correspondence)
**5. Head zone:** T2 dermatome — within cardiac viscerotome
(T1–T5). Lung viscerotome begins at T3.
**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line (SFL),
pectoralis major sternal head. → Cross-ref: A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** no specific data. 🔴
**8. Cupping:** not level-specific.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): chest pain (胸痛), cough
(咳嗽), breast distension/pain (乳痈乳痛), fullness of
the chest (胸胁支满).

Nguồn hiện đại: chest pain, cough, mastitis, intercostal
neuralgia. The T2 location places ST15 in the cardiac
referral zone but classical sources emphasize respiratory
and breast indications, not cardiac — consistent with the
SP chest segmental paradox (AP-04 Obs. 14/27/35) where
cardiac viscerotome points lack cardiac indications.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST15.

### An toàn

High risk — pneumothorax zone.

Precautions:
- **Pleural cavity:** oblique insertion only. At the 2nd
  ICS, the lung extends close to the chest wall.
- Left side: cardiac structures deeper to the 2nd ICS —
  additional caution warranted.
- Insert superior to the lower rib border.
- NEVER deep perpendicular insertion.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST16 — Ưng Song (膺窗, Yīng Chuāng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Breast Window" (膺 = breast/chest, 窗 = window) — a
"window" into the chest cavity at the breast level. Located
in the 3rd intercostal space (ICS), 4 cun lateral to the
anterior midline. T3 is the classic dermatome for cardiac
referred pain — the segmental level most strongly associated
with angina referral — yet classical indications prioritize
breast and respiratory conditions over cardiac.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the third intercostal
space, 4 cun lateral to the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: one rib below ST15 (2nd ICS), one rib
above ST17 (nipple level, 4th ICS). On the midclavicular
line. In females, breast tissue may overlay this point
depending on breast size and position.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm oblique insertion. In females,
displace breast tissue if present to access the intercostal
space directly.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T3 intercostal nerve** —
  T3 is the classic cardiac referred pain dermatome. Angina
  pectoris classically refers to the T3-T4 distribution.
- Spinal segment: T3
→ Cross-ref: A07 (cardiac viscerotome T1–T5)

Vascular: perforating branches of internal thoracic artery;
T3 intercostal vessels.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → (breast tissue
in females) → pectoralis major → external intercostal →
internal intercostal → innermost intercostal → parietal
pleura.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST16 Yingchuang — cough (咳嗽), chest fullness
(胸胁胀满), breast abscess (乳痈), breast distension
(乳肿).
**2. Trigger point:** pectoralis major TrPs at this level
may reproduce chest pain. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** reactive in mastitis, chest wall pain.
**4. Marma:** Stanarohita (upper breast) region. 🔴
(uncertain correspondence)
**5. Head zone:** T3 dermatome — peak cardiac referral zone.
Also within lung viscerotome (T3–T5).
**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line, pectoralis
major. → Cross-ref: A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** no specific data. 🔴
**8. Cupping:** not level-specific.

**Convergence score: 3/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): cough (咳嗽), chest
fullness (胸胁胀满), breast abscess (乳痈), breast
distension/pain (乳肿).

Nguồn hiện đại: chest pain, mastitis, breast abscess,
intercostal neuralgia. Despite T3 being the peak cardiac
referral dermatome, ST16's classical indications focus on
breast and respiratory — NOT cardiac — conditions. This
continues the SP chest segmental paradox: cardiac viscerotome
points consistently lack cardiac indications in the
classical tradition.
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 Obs. 14/27/35 (SP chest paradox)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST16.

### An toàn

High risk — pneumothorax zone.

Precautions:
- **Pleural cavity:** oblique insertion only.
- **Breast tissue (females):** may overlay this point.
  Displace breast tissue for accurate needling. Do NOT
  needle through inflamed or infected breast tissue
  (mastitis/abscess).
- Left side: heart deeper to the 3rd ICS — additional
  caution with deep insertion (though oblique technique
  prevents cardiac approach).
- NEVER deep perpendicular insertion.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST17 — Nhũ Trung (乳中, Rǔ Zhōng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry) — LANDMARK ONLY**
"Nipple Center" (乳 = breast/nipple, 中 = center/middle).
This is the ONLY point in the Atlas with an ABSOLUTE
PROHIBITION: both needling AND moxa are forbidden (禁刺禁灸).
ST17 is NOT a therapeutic acupoint — it is a LANDMARK used
to locate other points. The nipple serves as a reference
marker for:
- ST18 (Rugen): one rib below the nipple
- CV17 (Danzhong/Shanzhong): on the midline at nipple level
- Other chest point measurements using the nipple-to-nipple
  distance (8 cun) as a proportional standard

This is the FIRST non-therapeutic point in the Atlas.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

Location: the center of the nipple. In males, this
corresponds to the 4th intercostal space on the
midclavicular line. In females, nipple position varies
with breast size, age, and habitus — the 4th ICS reference
is used instead for consistent point location.

The nipple-to-nipple distance is defined as 8 cun in
proportional measurement (骨度分寸法), making the nipple-
to-midline distance 4 cun — the lateral measurement used
for all ST chest points (ST13–ST18).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T4 intercostal nerve** —
  the primary sensory innervation of the nipple-areolar
  complex. T4 is the nipple dermatome.
- The lateral cutaneous branch of T4 provides supplementary
  innervation to the lateral breast.
- Spinal segment: T4

Vascular: periareolar arterial plexus formed by branches of
internal thoracic artery (medial mammary branches) and
lateral thoracic artery. Dense capillary network.

### An toàn — Prohibition

**ABSOLUTE PROHIBITION (禁刺禁灸).**

Both needling and moxa are FORBIDDEN at this point. The
prohibition is documented in classical texts including:
- Jiayi Jing (甲乙经): "禁不可刺灸"
- Tongren Shuxue Zhenjiu Tujing (铜人腧穴针灸图经)

Rationale for prohibition:
- **Nipple sensitivity:** the nipple-areolar complex is one
  of the most densely innervated cutaneous structures in the
  body. Needling causes extreme pain with no therapeutic
  benefit.
- **Vascular density:** the periareolar plexus makes
  bleeding likely.
- **Thermal damage:** moxa at the nipple risks thermal burn
  to highly sensitive tissue with potential for scarring and
  functional impairment (lactation).

This DUAL prohibition distinguishes ST17 from all other
restricted points in the Atlas. Previous moxa prohibitions
(LU8, LR10, LR12, TE23, GB42) all PERMIT needling — only
the moxa modality is restricted. ST17 prohibits ALL
therapeutic modalities.

---

## ST18 — Nhũ Căn (乳根, Rǔ Gēn)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Breast Root" (乳 = breast, 根 = root/base) — at the base
of the breast, one rib below the nipple. Located in the 5th
intercostal space (ICS), 4 cun lateral to the anterior
midline. ST18 is the LAST point in the ST chest segment
before the channel enters the abdominal region at ST19.

ST18 is a key point in the classical LACTATION TRIAD:
SI1 (Shaoze, distal) + CV17 (Danzhong, chest center) +
ST18 (Rugen, local mammary). This three-level protocol
follows the distal-local prescribing principle, with ST18
providing the local mammary access point.
→ Cross-ref: SI1 lactation protocol (AP-06 L147, L205)

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): in the fifth intercostal
space, 4 cun lateral to the anterior midline, one rib below
the nipple (ST17).

Anatomical landmarks: directly below the nipple, at the
inferior border of the breast. In females, the inframammary
fold is the key landmark. Between ST17 above (nipple, 4th
ICS) and ST19 below (upper abdomen, 6 cun above umbilicus).

Spatial note: the transition from ST18 (5th ICS) to ST19
(upper abdomen) involves a significant JUMP — the channel
crosses the costal margin and enters the abdominal cavity.
No ST points are mapped between the 5th ICS and the upper
abdominal wall.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm oblique insertion along the
intercostal space.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T5 intercostal nerve** —
  T5 is the lowest level of the cardiac viscerotome (T1–T5)
  and borders the gastric viscerotome (T6+). This
  transitional position between cardiac and gastric segmental
  territories matches ST18's location between chest and
  abdomen.
- Spinal segment: T5
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07 (cardiac-gastric viscerotome
transition at T5-T6)

Vascular: perforating branches of internal thoracic artery;
T5 intercostal vessels. In females, branches of the lateral
thoracic artery supply the inferior breast.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → (breast tissue
in females, inframammary region) → pectoralis major (lower
fibers) → external intercostal → internal intercostal →
innermost intercostal → parietal pleura.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special acupoint category designations. However, ST18 is
included in the classical lactation formula (SI1 + CV17 +
ST18) as the local mammary component — a PRESCRIPTIVE role
rather than a CATEGORY membership.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST18 Rugen — insufficient lactation (产后乳少),
breast abscess/mastitis (乳痈), chest pain (胸痛), cough
(咳嗽), hiccup (呃逆).
**2. Trigger point:** pectoralis major (lower fibers) —
TrPs at the inferior pectoral border refer to the breast
and anterior chest. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** reactive in mastitis, breast engorgement, chest
wall pain.
**4. Marma:** **Stanamuula** (breast root) — Sira Marma
(blood vessel type). This is the same Marma correspondence
as SP18 (AP-04, 4/8 convergence at SP18). Both ST18 and
SP18 share breast-root function but at DIFFERENT lateral
positions: ST18 at 4 cun (midclavicular, directly below
nipple) vs SP18 at 6 cun (anterior axillary line). 🟢
**5. Head zone:** T5 dermatome — cardiac viscerotome lower
border, transitional to gastric (T6+).
**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line, pectoralis
major lower fibers approaching rectus abdominis. The SFL
continues inferiorly through rectus abdominis into the
abdominal segment. → Cross-ref: A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** no specific data. 🔴
**8. Cupping:** breast cupping practiced for mastitis in
some traditions, though not point-specific.

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Marma) —
highest in the ST chest group, matching SP18's 4/8. The
Stanamuula Marma correspondence is the differentiating
factor.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): insufficient lactation
(产后乳少), breast abscess (乳痈), mastitis (乳痈肿痛),
chest pain (胸痛), cough (咳嗽), hiccup (呃逆), epigastric
pain (胃脘痛).

Nguồn hiện đại: insufficient lactation, mastitis, breast
pain, chest pain, intercostal neuralgia. ST18 is the LOCAL
component of the classical lactation triad:
- **SI1 (Shaoze):** distal point — most specific single
  point for lactation; prolactin elevation data (AP-06)
- **CV17 (Danzhong):** chest center — general chest/mammary
  Qi regulation
- **ST18 (Rugen):** local mammary — direct breast access at
  the inframammary level

Comparative note: SP18 (AP-04) also carries classical
lactation indications and Stanamuula Marma correspondence,
but is NOT included in the standard lactation triad. The
clinical tradition prioritizes ST18 (directly below nipple,
midclavicular) over SP18 (lateral, anterior axillary line)
for lactation protocols.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No RCT isolates ST18. Lactation evidence
clusters around SI1 (some prolactin data) and the triad
protocol rather than any single local point.

### An toàn

High risk — pneumothorax zone (5th ICS).

Precautions:
- **Pleural cavity:** oblique insertion only, as for all ST
  chest points.
- **Breast tissue (females):** especially relevant at the
  inframammary fold. Displace breast tissue for accurate
  intercostal access. Do NOT needle through inflamed or
  infected breast tissue (mastitis/abscess — use distal
  points SI1, SP6 instead).
- **Diaphragm proximity:** T5 level approaches the
  diaphragmatic dome. Very deep insertion (beyond pleura)
  could theoretically reach the diaphragm, though this is
  prevented by standard oblique technique.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

### Batch 4 Architectural Observations

> **Observation 13 — ST17: FIRST ABSOLUTE PROHIBITION —
> DUAL MODALITY BAN (禁刺禁灸).**
>
> ST17 is the ONLY point in the Atlas with both needling AND
> moxa prohibited. All previous prohibitions are modality-
> specific: LU8, LR10, LR12, TE23, GB42 prohibit moxa only
> (needling permitted). ST17's prohibition is absolute — no
> therapeutic intervention of any kind is permitted. Its role
> is purely structural: a COORDINATE used to locate other
> points (ST18, CV17, and the 8-cun nipple-to-nipple
> proportional measurement). This makes ST17 functionally
> distinct from every other point in the Atlas — it is part
> of the channel system but NOT part of the therapeutic
> system.
>
> Updated prohibition inventory:
>
> | Point | Prohibition | Rationale |
> |-------|-------------|-----------|
> | LU8 | Moxa only | Thermal risk to radial artery |
> | LR10 | Moxa only | 五里 vascular danger coding |
> | LR12 | Moxa only | Inguinal artery (classical vs modern reversal) |
> | TE23 | Moxa only | Thermal risk to periorbital structures |
> | GB42 | Moxa only | Dorsal foot thin tissue |
> | **ST17** | **Needling + Moxa** | **Nipple: extreme sensitivity, no therapeutic value** |
>
> **Observation 14 — DUAL ANTERIOR CHEST LINES: ST (4 CUN)
> vs SP (6 CUN) PARALLEL COVERAGE.**
>
> ST13–ST18 at 4 cun lateral (midclavicular line) and
> SP17–SP20 at 6 cun lateral (anterior axillary line) form
> two PARALLEL vertical lines of chest points covering
> similar intercostal levels:
>
> | Level | ST point (4 cun) | SP point (6 cun) |
> |-------|-----------------|-----------------|
> | Below clavicle | ST13 | — |
> | 1st ICS | ST14 | — |
> | 2nd ICS | ST15 | SP20 |
> | 3rd ICS | ST16 | SP19 |
> | 4th ICS | ST17 (landmark) | SP18 |
> | 5th ICS | ST18 | SP17 |
>
> The ST line provides 6 points (more complete vertical
> coverage, including the subclavicular level); the SP line
> provides 4 points (ICS 2–5 only). The ST line lies over
> the body of pectoralis major; the SP line lies at the
> lateral border of pectoralis major approaching serratus
> anterior.
>
> The NUMBERING runs in opposite directions: ST numbers
> DESCEND (ST13 highest → ST18 lowest) while SP numbers
> ASCEND (SP17 lowest → SP20 highest). This reflects the
> overall channel trajectories: ST descends from head to
> foot, SP ascends from foot to head. At the chest, these
> opposite trajectories produce opposite numbering despite
> occupying the same anatomical territory.
>
> Both lines carry pneumothorax risk, and both show the
> segmental-only differentiation pattern (AP-04 Obs. 31):
> tissue architecture is identical across adjacent intercostal
> spaces, with spinal segment as the ONLY differentiating
> variable.
>
> **Observation 15 — MAMMARY ZONE DUAL-CHANNEL LACTATION:
> ST18 (4 CUN) vs SP18 (6 CUN).**
>
> Two channels provide mammary-zone acupoints with lactation
> indications: ST18 (4 cun lateral, 5th ICS, directly below
> nipple) and SP18 (6 cun lateral, 4th ICS, anterior axillary
> line). Both correspond to the Stanamuula (breast root)
> Marma — a Sira (vascular) type. Both achieve 4/8
> convergence (highest in their respective chest groups).
>
> The classical tradition PRIORITIZES ST18: it appears in
> the standard lactation triad (SI1 + CV17 + ST18), while
> SP18 "is not commonly included in published lactation
> protocols despite its classical indication" (AP-04). The
> preference may follow the distal-local prescribing
> principle: the triad uses SI1 (distal finger), CV17
> (chest midline), ST18 (local mammary) — three distinct
> anatomical levels. SP18 at 6 cun lateral would be
> REDUNDANT with ST18 in this protocol (both are local
> mammary), and the ST18 position (directly below nipple)
> provides more anatomically specific breast access.

---

## ST19 — Bất Dung (不容, Bù Róng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Not Contained" (不 = not, 容 = contain/accommodate) — the
name references the inability to receive food, pointing to
gastric retention and epigastric fullness. Located 6 cun
above the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to the anterior midline,
at the upper abdominal wall near the costal margin. ST19 is
the FIRST point in the ST abdominal segment — the channel
enters the anterior abdominal wall after crossing the costal
margin from ST18 (5th ICS, chest). The transition from chest
to abdomen involves a significant anatomical jump: different
tissue architecture (rectus abdominis corridor replaces
intercostal layers), different risk profile (peritoneal
puncture replaces pneumothorax), and different segmental
territory (T7 gastric vs T5 cardiac-gastric transition).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the upper abdomen,
6 cun above the center of the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: xiphoid-to-umbilicus = 8 cun, so ST19
is 2 cun below the xiphoid process, near the lower border of
the costal margin. On the lateral border of the rectus
abdominis muscle. Between ST18 above (5th ICS, chest) and
ST20 below (5 cun above umbilicus).

Spatial relationships: at the same horizontal level as CV14
(Juque, Front-Mu of Heart, midline) and approximately KI21
(Youmen, 0.5 cun lateral). The mediolateral tiling of the
anterior abdominal wall provides four parallel channels at
this level: CV (midline) → KI (0.5 cun) → ST (2 cun) →
SP (4 cun).

Needle depth: 10–15 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T7 intercostal nerve** —
  the 7th intercostal nerve emerges below the 7th rib; its
  anterior branch perforates the rectus sheath near the
  lateral border. T7 is within the gastric viscerotome
  (T6–T9).
- Spinal segment: T7
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07 (gastric viscerotome T6–T9)

Vascular: branches of the superior epigastric artery
(terminal branch of the internal thoracic artery, running
within the rectus sheath); T7 intercostal vessels.

Tissue layers (superficial → deep):
- Skin → subcutaneous fat
- Anterior rectus sheath
- Rectus abdominis muscle
- Posterior rectus sheath (present above arcuate line)
- Transversalis fascia
- Peritoneum

All ST abdominal points (ST19–ST30) share this identical
tissue architecture — the anterior abdominal wall at 2 cun
lateral lies within the rectus abdominis corridor. The ONLY
differentiating variable between adjacent points is the
segmental level of the intercostal nerve. Compare: SP13–SP16
at 4 cun lateral use the oblique muscle corridor (external
oblique → internal oblique → transversus abdominis) — a
different tissue architecture over the same visceral cavity.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST19 Burong — epigastric pain (胃脘痛), vomiting
(呕吐), loss of appetite (食欲不振), abdominal distension
(腹胀), hiccup (呃逆).
**2. Trigger point:** rectus abdominis (upper fibers) —
Travell & Simons describe upper rectus TrPs referring pain
to the epigastric region. Moderate location correspondence.
🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender in acute epigastric conditions, gastric
ulcer pain, biliary colic (right-sided).
**4. Marma:** no specific upper epigastric Marma. Nabhi Marma
(navel region) is 6 cun inferior. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** T7 dermatome = within gastric viscerotome
(T6–T9). CONVERGENCE. Gastric pathology refers pain to the
T7 dermatome. Also overlaps hepatic/gallbladder viscerotome
(T6–T10), especially on the RIGHT side.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2
**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line (SFL). Rectus
abdominis is a primary SFL station; ST19 lies on its upper
portion approaching the costal arch attachment. → Cross-ref:
A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** predicted by Kim 2017 model for
gastric pathology at T7, but not individually confirmed
(Kim 2017 confirmed T10–L1 for colitis, not T6–T9 for
gastric). 🟡
**8. Cupping:** abdominal cupping practiced in some traditions
but not specific to this level. 🔴

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). The upper epigastric location has strong
segmental correspondence to the gastric viscerotome — higher
than the ST chest group (3/8, which lacks viscerotome
matching per the cardiac paradox) but lower than ST25 (7/8,
which adds Marma + confirmed Neurogenic at the umbilical
convergence zone).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): epigastric pain (胃脘痛),
vomiting (呕吐), loss of appetite (不欲食), abdominal
distension (腹胀), hiccup (呃逆), acid regurgitation
(吐酸).

Nguồn hiện đại: epigastric pain, gastric distension,
vomiting. Supporting point for upper GI conditions. The
"Not Contained" naming aligns with gastric retention and
food intolerance — a failure of the Stomach's receiving
function (受纳) at its most proximal abdominal point.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No RCT isolates ST19. Upper abdominal ST
points are understudied compared to ST36 and ST25.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — peritoneal puncture zone.

Precautions:
- **Peritoneal cavity:** deep perpendicular needling can
  penetrate posterior rectus sheath → transversalis fascia →
  peritoneum. Rectus abdominis at this level is relatively
  thin in lean patients.
- **Costal margin proximity:** near the lower border of the
  ribcage. On the RIGHT side, deep insertion could approach
  the liver edge; on the LEFT side, the stomach fundus lies
  deep. Hepatomegaly may bring the liver edge further
  inferior.
- **Superior epigastric vessels:** within the rectus sheath —
  standard precaution.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST20 — Thừa Mãn (承满, Chéng Mǎn)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Receiving Fullness" (承 = receive/contain, 满 = full) —
the name describes the sensation of epigastric fullness.
In the naming sequence, ST19 "Not Contained" (failure to
receive) and ST20 "Receiving Fullness" (excess filling) form
a paired pathological contrast at adjacent points. Located
5 cun above the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to the anterior
midline, 1 cun below ST19.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the upper abdomen,
5 cun above the center of the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: on the rectus abdominis, between ST19
(6 cun above) and ST21 (4 cun above). 3 cun below the
xiphoid process.

Needle depth: 10–15 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T7–T8 intercostal nerve** —
  transitional between T7 and T8. Dermatomal overlap is
  common at this level. Both segments are within the gastric
  viscerotome (T6–T9).
- Spinal segment: T7–T8
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07

Vascular: branches of the superior epigastric artery;
T7–T8 intercostal vessels.

Tissue layers: identical rectus abdominis corridor (see ST19).

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST20 Chengman — epigastric distension (脘腹胀满),
vomiting (呕吐), acid regurgitation (吞酸), loss of appetite
(不欲食), hematemesis (吐血).
**2. Trigger point:** rectus abdominis upper fibers — similar
TrP territory as ST19. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender in epigastric fullness, gastritis.
**4. Marma:** no specific Marma. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** T7–T8 = gastric viscerotome. CONVERGENCE.
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL, rectus abdominis. → Cross-ref:
A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** predicted for gastric pathology. 🟡
**8. Cupping:** not specific. 🔴

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): epigastric distension
(脘腹胀满), vomiting (呕吐), loss of appetite (不欲食),
acid regurgitation (吞酸), hematemesis (吐血), borborygmus
(肠鸣).

Nguồn hiện đại: epigastric pain, gastric distension,
vomiting. The "Receiving Fullness" naming directly describes
the pathological state: the epigastrium receives excessive
fullness.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST20.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — peritoneal puncture zone. Same precautions
as ST19. Deep structures: liver edge (right), stomach body
(central/left).

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST21 — Lương Môn (梁门, Liáng Mén)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Beam Gate" (梁 = beam/ridge, 门 = gate) — named for the
palpable transverse ridge formed by the rectus abdominis
tendinous inscription at this level. Located 4 cun above the
umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to the anterior midline — the
MIDPOINT of the epigastric region (xiphoid-to-umbilicus =
8 cun). ST21 is the first "gate" (门) in the ST abdominal
naming sequence; three of the six upper abdominal points
carry the 门 suffix (ST21 梁门, ST22 关门, ST24 滑肉门).

ST21 is the most commonly selected point in the ST upper
abdominal group (ST19–ST24) for gastric conditions, due to
its position at the anatomical midpoint of the epigastric
region and its alignment with CV12 (Zhongwan, Front-Mu of
Stomach).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the upper abdomen,
4 cun above the center of the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: at the same horizontal level as CV12
(Zhongwan, Front-Mu of Stomach) — the midpoint between
xiphoid and umbilicus. The transverse cross-section at this
level: CV12 (midline) → KI19 (0.5 cun) → ST21 (2 cun) →
SP16 (4 cun). Three channels flank the Front-Mu at
progressively lateral positions.

Needle depth: 10–20 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T8 intercostal nerve** —
  T8 is the CENTER of the gastric viscerotome (T6–T9). This
  provides the strongest segmental correspondence to the
  stomach within the ST upper abdominal group.
- Spinal segment: T8
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07

Vascular: branches of the superior epigastric artery;
T8 intercostal vessels.

Tissue layers: identical rectus abdominis corridor (see ST19).

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations. However, ST21's horizontal
alignment with CV12 (Front-Mu of Stomach) is clinically
significant: CV12 (midline) + ST21 (bilateral, 2 cun lateral)
= a three-point combination frequently used for gastric
disorders. This is a PRESCRIPTIVE relationship, not a
category membership.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST21 Liangmen — gastric pain (胃脘痛), loss of
appetite (不欲食), abdominal distension (腹胀), vomiting
(呕吐), diarrhea (泄泻), undigested food (不消化).
**2. Trigger point:** rectus abdominis mid-upper fibers.
Upper rectus TrPs commonly present at this level in patients
with chronic epigastric pain. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** frequently tender in gastric conditions — one of
the most reactive epigastric palpation sites.
**4. Marma:** no specific Marma. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** T8 = center of gastric viscerotome (T6–T9).
STRONGEST segmental correspondence in the upper abdominal
group. Gastric ulcer pain commonly refers to T8 dermatome.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL, rectus abdominis. → Cross-ref:
A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** predicted for gastric pathology at
T8. 🟡
**8. Cupping:** not specific. 🔴

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). Despite being the most commonly used point
in the upper abdominal group, ST21's convergence score
matches its neighbors — the differentiator is clinical
utility and CV12 pairing, not cross-tradition breadth.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): gastric pain (胃脘痛),
loss of appetite (不欲食), abdominal distension (腹胀),
vomiting (呕吐), undigested food in stool (完谷不化),
diarrhea (泄泻).

Nguồn hiện đại: gastric pain, gastric ulcer, gastritis, loss
of appetite. Most commonly selected ST upper abdominal point
for gastric conditions, typically combined with CV12
(Front-Mu of Stomach) and/or ST36 (He-Sea). The CV12+ST21
combination follows the midline-lateral prescribing principle
(Front-Mu at midline + lateral channel point at the same
segmental level).

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No RCT isolates ST21, though it appears in
multi-point gastric protocols.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — peritoneal puncture zone. Deep structures:
stomach body (central/left), liver left lobe (variable),
transverse colon (below stomach). Same rectus sheath
precautions as ST19–ST20.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST22 — Quan Môn (关门, Guān Mén)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Pass Gate" or "Closing Gate" (关 = close/pass/barrier,
门 = gate) — the second "gate" in the ST abdominal naming
sequence. In the naming progression, ST21 "Beam Gate"
(structural landmark) leads to ST22 "Closing Gate"
(functional barrier). The name suggests the gate controlling
passage of food from the upper to lower digestive tract.
Located 3 cun above the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to the
anterior midline.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the upper abdomen,
3 cun above the center of the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: on the rectus abdominis, between ST21
(4 cun above) and ST23 (2 cun above). At the level 1 cun
below CV12.

Needle depth: 10–20 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T8–T9 intercostal nerve** —
  transitional between the gastric viscerotome center (T8)
  and its lower border (T9). This positions ST22 at the
  gastric-intestinal transition zone.
- Spinal segment: T8–T9
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07

Vascular: branches of the superior epigastric artery;
T8–T9 intercostal vessels.

Tissue layers: identical rectus abdominis corridor (see ST19).

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST22 Guanmen — abdominal distension (腹胀),
borborygmus (肠鸣), diarrhea (泄泻), edema (水肿), loss
of appetite (不欲食), abdominal pain (腹痛).
**2. Trigger point:** rectus abdominis mid-fibers. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender in periumbilical/epigastric conditions.
**4. Marma:** no specific Marma. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** T8–T9 = gastric viscerotome lower border.
Transitional to small intestine territory (T9–T11).
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL, rectus abdominis. → Cross-ref:
A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** predicted for gastric/intestinal
pathology at T8–T9 transition. 🟡
**8. Cupping:** not specific. 🔴

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): abdominal distension
(腹胀), borborygmus (肠鸣), diarrhea (泄泻), abdominal
pain (腹痛), edema (水肿), loss of appetite (不欲食).

Nguồn hiện đại: abdominal distension, diarrhea, edema.
The indication profile SHIFTS at ST22 compared to ST19–ST21:
less emphasis on purely gastric symptoms (vomiting, acid
regurgitation), more emphasis on intestinal symptoms
(borborygmus, diarrhea) and fluid regulation (edema). This
shift corresponds to the segmental transition from gastric
(T6–T9) toward intestinal (T10+) viscerotome territory —
the "closing gate" at the viscerotome boundary.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST22.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — peritoneal puncture zone. Same precautions
as ST19–ST21. Deep structures: greater omentum, transverse
colon, small intestine loops.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST23 — Thái Ất (太乙, Tài Yǐ)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Supreme Unity" or "Great Monad" (太 = supreme/great,
乙 = the second Heavenly Stem; 太乙 = Taoist cosmological
concept of the primordial unity). Unlike the functional
naming of its neighbors (ST19 "Not Contained," ST22 "Pass
Gate"), ST23's name belongs to the cosmological/spiritual
naming tradition. This naming pattern correlates with its
UNIQUE classical feature: ST23 is the ONLY upper abdominal
ST point with prominent psychiatric indications (mania,
epilepsy — 癫狂).

Located 2 cun above the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to the
anterior midline.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the upper abdomen,
2 cun above the center of the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: on the rectus abdominis, between ST22
(3 cun above) and ST24 (1 cun above). At the level of CV10
(Xiawan, Lower Stomach Cavity).

Needle depth: 15–25 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T9 intercostal nerve** —
  T9 is the lower border of the gastric viscerotome (T6–T9)
  and overlaps with the beginning of the small intestinal
  viscerotome (T9–T11).
- Spinal segment: T9
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07

Vascular: branches of the superior epigastric artery;
T9 intercostal vessels.

Tissue layers: identical rectus abdominis corridor (see ST19).

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST23 Taiyi — mental disorders (癫狂 = mania/
psychosis), epilepsy (痫症), gastric pain (胃脘痛),
irritability (心烦), restlessness.
**2. Trigger point:** rectus abdominis mid-fibers. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender in abdominal conditions; the psychiatric
indications have no Ashi correlate.
**4. Marma:** no specific Marma. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** T9 = gastric viscerotome lower border. No
known segmental pathway connects T9 to psychiatric effects.
The psychiatric indications represent a CLASSICAL CLAIM
without obvious segmental basis.
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL, rectus abdominis. → Cross-ref:
A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** predicted for gastric pathology at
T9. 🟡
**8. Cupping:** not specific. 🔴

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). The psychiatric indications are TCM-only —
no other tradition targets this abdominal level for mental
disorders.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): mania/psychosis (癫狂),
epilepsy (痫症), gastric pain (胃脘痛), irritability/
restlessness (心烦不安), abdominal distension (腹胀),
borborygmus (肠鸣).

Nguồn hiện đại: mental disorders, epilepsy, gastric pain.
ST23 is UNIQUE among the ST upper abdominal group (ST19–
ST24) in featuring psychiatric indications as a PRIMARY
classical function. The 太乙 naming (cosmological rather
than anatomical) reinforces this status. Modern context: the
celiac plexus ("solar plexus," the largest autonomic plexus)
lies deep to this region, providing vagal and sympathetic
connections to the CNS — a gut-brain axis that classical
practitioners may have observed empirically.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST23. The psychiatric
indications have no modern investigation.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — peritoneal puncture zone. Same precautions
as ST19–ST22. Deep structures: greater omentum, transverse
colon, small intestine loops.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST24 — Hoạt Nhục Môn (滑肉门, Huá Ròu Mén)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Slippery Flesh Gate" (滑 = slippery/smooth, 肉 = flesh,
门 = gate) — the third and final "gate" in the ST upper
abdominal naming sequence (梁门→关门→滑肉门), named for
the soft, yielding quality of the periumbilical tissue. ST24
is the LAST point before ST25 (Tianshu, Front-Mu of Large
Intestine at the umbilical level) — occupying the gateway
between the upper abdominal gastric segment and the umbilical
intestinal segment.

Located 1 cun above the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to the
anterior midline.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the upper abdomen,
1 cun above the center of the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: on the rectus abdominis, between ST23
(2 cun above) and ST25 (umbilicus level). 1 cun superior
and 2 cun lateral to the umbilicus — the closest ST
abdominal point to Nabhi (navel) without being at the navel
itself.

Needle depth: 15–25 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T9–T10 intercostal nerve**
  — transitional between the gastric viscerotome lower
  border (T9) and the intestinal viscerotome (T10+). ST24
  occupies the EXACT segmental transition from gastric to
  intestinal territory. The next point, ST25 (T10), is
  confirmed segmental for the large intestine.
- Spinal segment: T9–T10
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07, ST25 entry (T10 confirmed)

Vascular: branches of the superior/inferior epigastric
arteries — the anastomotic zone between these two systems
occurs near the umbilical level; T9–T10 intercostal vessels.

Tissue layers: identical rectus abdominis corridor (see ST19).
At this periumbilical level, the rectus abdominis is wider
and thicker than at the upper epigastric level (ST19–ST20),
providing more tissue buffer above the peritoneum.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST24 Huaroumen — gastric disorders (胃病),
vomiting (呕吐), mental disorders (癫狂, shared with ST23),
borborygmus (肠鸣), tongue protrusion (吐舌).
**2. Trigger point:** rectus abdominis periumbilical fibers —
approaching the zone where Dorsher confirmed TrP
correspondence at ST25. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender in upper periumbilical conditions.
**4. Marma:** Nabhi Marma (navel) — approaches the Nabhi
zone (1 cun superior, 2 cun lateral) but does not directly
overlie it. Partial correspondence. 🟡
**5. Head zone:** T9–T10 = gastric-intestinal transition.
Overlaps with both gastric (T6–T9) and large intestinal
(T10–L1) viscerotomes.
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL, rectus abdominis. → Cross-ref:
A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** Kim 2017 model: T9–T10 spans both
gastric and colitis-predicted zones. Transitional. 🟡
**8. Cupping:** not specific. 🔴

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains). The convergence JUMP from ST24 (5/8) to
ST25 (7/8) occurs within 1 cun — the navel functions as a
multi-tradition convergence amplifier, adding Marma
(confirmed Nabhi) and Neurogenic (confirmed Kim 2017 T10–L1)
at the umbilical level.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): gastric disorders (胃病),
vomiting (呕吐), mental disorders/mania (癫狂 — shared with
ST23), tongue protrusion (吐舌), borborygmus (肠鸣).

Nguồn hiện đại: gastric pain, vomiting, mental disorders.
ST24 shares psychiatric indications with ST23 (太乙), forming
a two-point psychiatric sub-group (ST23–ST24) within the
otherwise gastric-focused upper abdominal segment. The
"Slippery Flesh Gate" = the last transition point before
entering the intestinal territory at ST25.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST24.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — peritoneal puncture zone. Same precautions
as ST19–ST23. Rectus abdominis thicker at this periumbilical
level than at upper epigastric levels.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

### Batch 5 Architectural Observations

> **Observation 16 — RECTUS CORRIDOR: 12 ABDOMINAL POINTS,
> ONE TISSUE ARCHITECTURE.**
>
> ST19–ST30 (including Phase 1 ST25) constitute TWELVE
> consecutive points sharing identical tissue layers: skin →
> subcutaneous → anterior rectus sheath → rectus abdominis →
> posterior rectus sheath → transversalis fascia → peritoneum.
> All sit at 2 cun lateral to midline within the rectus
> abdominis corridor. The ONLY differentiating variable
> between adjacent points is the spinal segment of the
> intercostal nerve, ascending from T7 (ST19) through T10
> (ST25) to approximately L1 (ST30).
>
> This is the longest anatomically homogeneous point series
> in the Atlas — 12 consecutive points differentiated solely
> by segmental level. Compare: SP13–SP16 (4 points in the
> oblique corridor, identified as "most homogeneous group"
> in AP-04 Obs. 28). The ST 12-point series exceeds SP's
> 4-point series by 3×, making it the strongest demonstration
> that classical point differentiation can occur through
> segmental specificity alone.
>
> The parallel corridor at 4 cun lateral (SP) uses a
> DIFFERENT tissue architecture — three oblique muscle layers
> instead of rectus sheath — despite covering the same
> visceral cavity. Same peritoneal risk, different access
> path.
>
> **Observation 17 — VISCEROTOME GRADIENT: CLASSICAL
> INDICATIONS TRACK SEGMENTAL ANATOMY T7→T10.**
>
> The classical indication profile shifts systematically
> across the ST upper abdominal segment:
>
> | Level | Points | Segment | Primary indications |
> |-------|--------|---------|---------------------|
> | Upper | ST19–ST21 | T7–T8 | Gastric: pain, vomiting, acid, appetite |
> | Transitional | ST22 | T8–T9 | Mixed: + borborygmus, diarrhea, edema |
> | Lower | ST23–ST24 | T9–T10 | Mixed: + psychiatric (癫狂), intestinal |
> | Umbilical | ST25 | T10 | Purely intestinal (Front-Mu of LI) |
>
> This shift tracks the segmental transition from gastric
> viscerotome (T6–T9) to intestinal viscerotome (T10+). The
> most dramatic jump occurs at ST25 (T10), which has PURELY
> intestinal indications. Classical practitioners differentiated
> abdominal points at 1-cun intervals along the same muscle,
> and the traditional indication specificity encoded segmental
> anatomy centuries before dermatome/viscerotome mapping.
>
> The convergence score follows the same gradient: ST19–ST24
> = 5/8 (gastric match), ST25 = 7/8 (intestinal match +
> navel convergence). The navel is not just an anatomical
> landmark but a cross-tradition amplifier — Nabhi Marma,
> Kim 2017 neurogenic confirmation, and Anatomy Trains
> SFL station all converge specifically at T10.
>
> **Observation 18 — FOUR PARALLEL ABDOMINAL LINES: THE
> ANTERIOR WALL MEDIOLATERAL TILING.**
>
> At the abdominal level, four meridians tile the anterior
> wall at progressively lateral distances:
>
> | Distance | Channel | Umbilical-level point | Tissue |
> |----------|---------|-----------------------|--------|
> | 0 cun | Ren Mai (CV) | CV8 (Shenque) | Linea alba |
> | 0.5 cun | Kidney (KI) | KI16 (Huangshu) | Medial rectus |
> | 2 cun | **Stomach (ST)** | **ST25 (Tianshu)** | Lateral rectus |
> | 4 cun | Spleen (SP) | SP15 (Daheng) | Oblique muscles |
>
> Each successive channel accesses a different tissue
> compartment. The three inner lines (CV, KI, ST) are all
> on rectus abdominis; SP at 4 cun crosses into the oblique
> corridor with its distinct three-layer architecture.
>
> This four-line tiling creates a systematic grid that
> repeats at EVERY level from costal margin to pubic region.
> The ST channel's 12 abdominal points provide the most
> complete vertical coverage (T7 to approximately L1),
> compared to KI (~10 abdominal points) and SP (4 abdominal
> points). At the chest, all channels shift laterally: KI
> to 2 cun, ST to 4 cun, SP to 6 cun — preserving the
> relative spacing while accommodating the ribcage flare.
>
> Clinical consequence: the same visceral organ can be
> accessed from multiple lateral positions at the same
> segmental level (e.g., CV12 + KI19 + ST21 + SP16 all at
> approximately T8, the gastric viscerotome center). The
> classical prescribing tradition selects among these based
> on channel-specific therapeutic intent, not just segmental
> access.

---

## ST26 — Ngoại Lăng (外陵, Wài Líng)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Outer Mound" (外 = outer, 陵 = mound/hill) — named for the
prominence of the lower rectus abdominis, palpable as a soft
mound lateral to the midline below the navel. Located 1 cun
below the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to the anterior midline.
ST26 is the FIRST sub-umbilical ST abdominal point — the
symmetrical mirror of ST24 (1 cun ABOVE the umbilicus) across
the navel axis. ST25 (Tianshu, at the navel) divides the ST
abdominal segment into upper (gastric, ST19–ST24) and lower
(intestinal→urogenital, ST26–ST30).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lower abdomen,
1 cun below the center of the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: on the rectus abdominis, between ST25
(umbilicus level) and ST27 (2 cun below umbilicus). At the
level of CV7 (Yinjiao, 1 cun below umbilicus, midline).

Needle depth: 15–25 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T10–T11 intercostal nerve**
  — T10 = intestinal viscerotome (confirmed at ST25), T11
  begins the transition toward the urogenital zone.
- Spinal segment: T10–T11
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07, ST25 entry (T10 confirmed)

Vascular: branches of the inferior epigastric artery
(ascending within the rectus sheath); T10–T11 intercostal
vessels.

Tissue layers: identical rectus abdominis corridor (see
ST19). At 1 cun below the umbilicus, the posterior rectus
sheath is still present (the arcuate line is typically at
the ST27–ST28 level). Full tissue buffer maintained.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST26 Wailing — abdominal pain (腹痛), hernia
(疝), dysmenorrhea (痛经).
**2. Trigger point:** rectus abdominis infraumbilical
fibers — TrPs in lower rectus refer pain to the lower
abdomen and periumbilical region. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender in lower abdominal conditions,
periumbilical pain, intestinal disturbance.
**4. Marma:** no specific Marma at this level. Nabhi Marma
(navel) is 1 cun superior — close but not overlying. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** T10–T11 = intestinal viscerotome.
CONVERGENCE. Kim 2017 confirmed neurogenic sensitization
in T10–L1 for colitis — ST26 is within this confirmed zone,
unlike the upper abdominal points ST19–ST24 (T7–T9) which
were only partially overlapping.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL, rectus abdominis infraumbilical
segment. → Cross-ref: A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** within the Kim 2017 confirmed
T10–L1 colitis zone. Predicted sensitization at this
dermatome in intestinal pathology. 🟡
**8. Cupping:** not specific. 🔴

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains, Neurogenic). HIGHER than the upper abdominal
group (5/8) because ST26 enters the Kim 2017 confirmed
neurogenic zone (T10–L1). Still below ST25 (7/8) which
additionally carries Nabhi Marma convergence.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): abdominal pain (腹痛),
hernia (疝), dysmenorrhea (痛经), umbilical pain (脐痛).

Nguồn hiện đại: abdominal pain, hernia. ST26 has the
narrowest indication profile of the ST abdominal series —
primarily pain without the organ-specific differentiation
seen at ST25 (intestinal) or ST28 (urinary). Its position
1 cun below the navel places it in a transitional zone
between the intestinal specificity of ST25 and the
urogenital indications emerging at ST27–ST28.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST26.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — peritoneal puncture zone. Same precautions
as ST19–ST25. At 1 cun below the navel, the posterior
rectus sheath is still present, providing full tissue
buffer.

No pregnancy contraindication for superficial needling.
No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST27 — Đại Cự (大巨, Dà Jù)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Great Immensity" (大 = great, 巨 = immense/vast) — a
cosmological name (like ST23 太乙 "Supreme Unity") rather
than an anatomical descriptor, referencing the vast space
of the lower abdominal cavity. Located 2 cun below the
umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to the anterior midline. ST27
marks the onset of urogenital indications — classical
symptom profiles shift from purely intestinal (ST25–ST26)
to include urinary and seminal symptoms, tracking the
segmental transition from T10 intestinal to T11 urogenital
territory.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lower abdomen,
2 cun below the center of the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: on the rectus abdominis, between
ST26 (1 cun below) and ST28 (3 cun below). At the level
of CV5 (Shimen, Front-Mu of Triple Burner, 2 cun below
umbilicus, midline).

Needle depth: 15–25 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T11 intercostal nerve** —
  T11 transitions from intestinal viscerotome (T10–T11)
  toward the urogenital zone (T11–L1). The subcostal nerve
  (T12) begins to contribute at deeper levels.
- Spinal segment: T11
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07

Vascular: branches of the inferior epigastric artery; T11
intercostal vessels.

Tissue layers: identical rectus abdominis corridor (see
ST19). ST27 is near the **arcuate line** (linea
semicircularis) — typically located approximately 1/3 of
the distance from the umbilicus to the pubic symphysis.
Below this line, the posterior rectus sheath becomes
incomplete: only transversalis fascia and peritoneum lie
posterior to the rectus abdominis, reducing the tissue
buffer.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST27 Daju — lower abdominal distension
(少腹胀), urinary difficulty (小便不利), seminal emission
(遗精), hernia (疝).
**2. Trigger point:** rectus abdominis lower fibers — TrPs
refer pain to the lower abdomen and suprapubic region. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender in lower abdominal conditions, urinary
retention, dysmenorrhea.
**4. Marma:** no specific Marma. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** T11 = intestinal-urogenital transitional
zone. The T11 dermatome overlaps with the superior
hypogastric plexus territory (T10–L2 sympathetic).
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL, rectus abdominis. → Cross-ref:
A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** within Kim 2017 confirmed T10–L1
zone. 🟡
**8. Cupping:** not specific. 🔴

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains, Neurogenic).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): lower abdominal distension
(少腹胀满), urinary difficulty (小便不利), seminal emission
(遗精), hernia (疝), insomnia (不得眠 — attributed to Kidney
deficiency pattern in some sources).

Nguồn hiện đại: lower abdominal distension, urinary
disorders, seminal emission. ST27 is the first ST abdominal
point with URINARY indications — tracking the segmental
transition from intestinal (T10) to urogenital (T11+). The
cosmological name 大巨 does not encode clinical function,
unlike ST28's explicit "Water Passage."

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST27.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — peritoneal puncture zone. Near the arcuate
line — the posterior rectus sheath may be incomplete at this
level. Palpate rectus thickness before insertion. In thin
patients, the peritoneum may be closer to the surface than
at upper abdominal levels.

Pregnancy: deep needling contraindicated in later trimesters
(proximity to uterus). No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST28 — Thủy Đạo (水道, Shuǐ Dào)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Water Passage" (水 = water, 道 = passage/way) — the name
EXPLICITLY encodes urinary function, making ST28 the most
transparently named point in the ST abdominal series. Located
3 cun below the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to the anterior
midline, at the level of CV4 (Guanyuan, 关元 — one of the
most important Lower Jiao points in all of acupuncture).
ST28 is the primary urinary point on the ST abdominal
segment, with classical indications centered on water
metabolism disorders — urinary retention, edema, stranguria.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lower abdomen,
3 cun below the center of the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: on the rectus abdominis, between
ST27 (2 cun below) and ST29 (4 cun below). At the level
of CV4 (Guanyuan, 3 cun below umbilicus, midline) — one of
the most clinically important midline Lower Jiao points.

The urinary bladder lies deep to this region when distended,
with its dome rising above the pubic symphysis. The
peritoneal reflection over the bladder dome is accessible
from T11–T12 segmental afferents.

Needle depth: 15–25 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Subcostal nerve (T12)** — primary cutaneous supply at
  this level. T12 enters the urogenital viscerotome zone.
- **Anterior cutaneous branch of T11 intercostal nerve** —
  variable contribution at the superior aspect.
- **Iliohypogastric nerve (L1)** may contribute sensory
  branches at deeper levels.
- Spinal segment: T11–T12
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07

Vascular: branches of the inferior epigastric artery; T12
intercostal/subcostal vessels.

Tissue layers: identical rectus abdominis corridor (see
ST19). BELOW the arcuate line — the posterior rectus sheath
is absent. Posterior to the rectus abdominis: transversalis
fascia → peritoneum ONLY. Reduced tissue buffer compared to
upper abdominal points.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST28 Shuidao — urinary retention (癃闭),
stranguria (淋证), edema (水肿), abdominal distension
(腹胀), dysmenorrhea (痛经), amenorrhea (闭经), hernia (疝).
**2. Trigger point:** rectus abdominis lower-third fibers —
TrPs refer to the suprapubic and inguinal regions. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender in bladder distension, urinary
retention, lower abdominal inflammation.
**4. Marma:** Basti Marma (bladder) is primarily a midline
structure corresponding to CV3–CV4, not the lateral
abdominal wall at 2 cun. No direct correspondence. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** T11–T12 = urogenital zone. CONVERGENCE
for anterior abdominal wall innervation. NOTE: the bladder
detrusor's primary innervation is SACRAL (S2–S4 via pelvic
splanchnic nerves), not thoracolumbar — a segmental
MISMATCH between surface point and deep visceral target.
Possible resolution: the bladder's peritoneal covering and
dome receive T11–T12 afferents, and the superior hypogastric
plexus (T10–L2) provides sympathetic bladder innervation.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL, rectus abdominis. → Cross-ref:
A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** within Kim 2017 confirmed T10–L1
zone. The classical name "Water Passage" encodes the
segmental sensitivity observed at this level during
urogenital pathology. 🟡
**8. Cupping:** not specific. 🔴

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains, Neurogenic). The Head zone convergence at
ST28 carries a caveat: segmental match is for the anterior
abdominal wall (T12) and bladder dome/sympathetic component
(T10–L2), but the primary bladder innervation (S2–S4) has
no segmental connection to this point.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): urinary retention (癃闭),
painful urination/stranguria (淋痛), edema (水肿), abdominal
distension (腹满), hernia (疝), irregular menstruation
(月经不调), amenorrhea (闭经).

Nguồn hiện đại: urinary retention, edema, dysmenorrhea,
abdominal distension. ST28 is the MOST explicitly urinary
point on the ST channel — its name alone ("Water Passage")
encodes the function. Among the 12 ST abdominal points,
this is the clearest example of classical nomenclature
encoding segmental correspondence: 水道 at T12, overlying
the bladder dome.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST28. Urinary retention
protocols typically use CV3, CV4, SP6, BL28 (Back-Shu of
Bladder) — ST28 appears in some protocols but has no
isolated evidence.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — peritoneal puncture zone. Below the arcuate
line — reduced tissue buffer. In patients with bladder
distension, the bladder dome rises above the pubic symphysis
and may be at risk from deep perpendicular needling.

Pregnancy: deep needling contraindicated in 2nd–3rd
trimesters (proximity to uterus). No classical moxa
prohibition.

---

## ST29 — Quy Lai (归来, Guī Lái)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Return" (归来 = return/come back) — classically interpreted
as "the return of menses" (经之归来), referencing the
point's use for restoring menstruation in amenorrhea, or as
the return of displaced organs in hernia reduction. Located
4 cun below the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to the anterior
midline, at the level of CV3 (Zhongji, 中极, Front-Mu of
Bladder). ST29 is the most reproductively focused point in
the ST abdominal series, with classical indications
emphasizing menstrual regulation, hernia, and genital
conditions for both sexes.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lower abdomen,
4 cun below the center of the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
the anterior midline.

Anatomical landmarks: on the rectus abdominis, between
ST28 (3 cun below) and ST30 (5 cun below, pubic level).
At the level of CV3 (Zhongji, Front-Mu of Bladder, 4 cun
below umbilicus, midline). 1 cun above the superior border
of the pubic symphysis.

Needle depth: 15–25 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Subcostal nerve (T12) and iliohypogastric nerve (L1)**
  — primary cutaneous supply at this level. T12–L1 marks
  the inguinal/urogenital viscerotome.
- Spinal segment: T12–L1
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07

Vascular: branches of the inferior epigastric artery; at
this level, the artery is approaching its origin from the
external iliac (deep to the inguinal ligament).

Tissue layers: identical rectus abdominis corridor (see
ST19). Below the arcuate line — posterior rectus sheath
absent.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST29 Guilai — hernia (疝), irregular
menstruation (月经不调), amenorrhea (闭经), leukorrhea
(带下), impotence (阳痿), genital retraction (阴缩),
prolapse (子宫脱垂).
**2. Trigger point:** rectus abdominis inferior fibers —
TrPs refer to the suprapubic and inguinal regions. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender in pelvic inflammatory conditions,
inguinal hernia, dysmenorrhea.
**4. Marma:** no specific Marma. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** T12–L1 = inguinal/urogenital zone. The
reproductive organs receive sympathetic innervation from
T10–L1, overlapping with this point's segmental level.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL, rectus abdominis approaching
pubic attachment. → Cross-ref: A09.
**7. Neurogenic spot:** within Kim 2017 T10–L1 zone. 🟡
**8. Cupping:** not specific. 🔴

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains, Neurogenic).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): hernia (疝), amenorrhea
(闭经/经闭), irregular menstruation (月经不调), leukorrhea
(带下), impotence (阳痿), genital retraction (阴缩/缩阴),
lower abdominal pain (少腹痛), prolapse (子宫脱垂).

Nguồn hiện đại: hernia, menstrual disorders, impotence,
prolapse. ST29 has the broadest reproductive indication
profile of any ST abdominal point — covering both male
(impotence, genital retraction) and female (amenorrhea,
leukorrhea, prolapse) conditions. The name 归来 "Return"
encodes the therapeutic goal of RESTORATION — returning
displaced organs (hernia) or lost functions (menses,
potency).

Classical combination: **GB27 + LR5 + ST29** for hernia.
Three channels (Gallbladder, Liver, Stomach) converge on
hernia treatment from three spatial directions: GB27 from
the lateral abdomen (Dai Mai territory, at ASIS), LR5 from
the medial leg (Liver Luo-Connecting, distal), ST29 from
the anterior abdomen (segmental, local).
→ Cross-ref: AP-11 GB27 (line 5601)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST29.

### An toàn

Moderate risk — peritoneal puncture zone. Below the arcuate
line. In inguinal hernia patients, the abdominal wall may
contain a fascial defect (hernial orifice) that increases
peritoneal penetration risk — palpate for defects before
needling.

Pregnancy: deep needling CONTRAINDICATED in 2nd–3rd
trimesters (proximity to gravid uterus). No classical moxa
prohibition.

---

## ST30 — Khí Xung (气冲, Qì Chōng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Qi Surging" (气 = qi/vital energy, 冲 = surge/rush) —
named for the palpable arterial pulsation of the femoral
artery at the inguinal region and the classical concept of
Qi "surging upward" (气上冲) from the lower abdomen. Located
5 cun below the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to the anterior
midline — at the level of the superior border of the pubic
symphysis where the femoral artery becomes superficially
palpable.

ST30 is the LAST abdominal point on the Stomach channel —
the terminal point of the 12-point rectus abdominis corridor
(ST19–ST30, Obs. 16). After ST30, the channel transitions
to the anterior thigh at ST31 (Biguan), crossing the
inguinal ligament from trunk to lower limb.

ST30 carries TWO special designations:
1. **Meeting point of ST and Chong Mai (冲脉)** — the
   Penetrating Vessel, called the "Sea of Blood" (血海) and
   "Sea of the Twelve Meridians" (十二经之海). SP4 (Gongsun)
   is its Eight Confluent opening point, paired with PC6
   (Neiguan) for Yin Wei Mai.
2. **Sea of Nourishment transport point (水谷之海)** —
   together with ST36 (Zusanli), per Ling Shu Ch. 33
   (Four Seas). The ST channel hosts transport points for
   3 of 4 Seas (Obs. 11).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lower abdomen,
5 cun below the center of the umbilicus, 2 cun lateral to
the anterior midline, at the level of the superior border
of the pubic symphysis.

Anatomical landmarks: at the inguinal region, medial to the
palpable femoral artery pulse. Between ST29 (4 cun below
umbilicus) and ST31 (anterior thigh, below ASIS).

Spatial relationships — the inguinal cross-channel
convergence (medial to lateral):
- CV2 (Qugu, 曲骨): 0 cun (midline, pubic symphysis)
- **ST30 (Qichong): 2 cun** — medial to the femoral vessels
- LR12 (Jimai, 急脉): 2.5 cun — medial to the femoral vein
- Femoral Vein → Femoral Artery → Femoral Nerve
- SP12 (Chongmen, 冲门): 3.5 cun — lateral to the femoral
  artery
→ Cross-ref: AP-12 LR12, AP-04 SP12

Needle depth: 10–15 mm perpendicular. CRITICAL: palpate
the femoral pulse before insertion. Insert MEDIAL to the
pulsation, directing the needle away from the artery.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Iliohypogastric nerve (L1)** — anterior cutaneous
  branch supplies the hypogastric and inguinal skin.
- **Ilioinguinal nerve (L1, with T12 contribution)** —
  courses through the inguinal canal; anterior scrotal/
  labial branches emerge at or near this level.
- **Genitofemoral nerve (L1–L2)** — genital branch courses
  through the inguinal canal; femoral branch supplies skin
  over the femoral triangle.
- Spinal segment: L1
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07

Vascular:
- **Femoral artery** — the external iliac artery becomes
  the femoral artery as it passes under the inguinal
  ligament. At ST30's level, the artery is approaching
  its emergence. The femoral pulse is the PRIMARY landmark.
  **HIGH VASCULAR RISK.**
- Superficial epigastric artery — small branch arising from
  the femoral artery, ascending across the inguinal
  ligament.
→ Cross-ref: A04

Tissue layers: rectus abdominis corridor (see ST19) at its
most inferior extent. Below the arcuate line — posterior
rectus sheath absent. At this inguinal level, the rectus
abdominis thins and the tissue transitions toward the
inguinal ligament attachment.

Deep structures: spermatic cord (males) or round ligament
(females) in the nearby inguinal canal. Peritoneum, small
intestine loops deep.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft,
Back-Shu, Front-Mu, Command Point, Ma Dan-yang Star,
Ghost Point, or Window of Heaven designation.

**Meeting point (交会穴):** meeting of Foot Yangming (ST)
and **Chong Mai (冲脉, Penetrating Vessel)**. The Chong Mai
"originates" (起) from the lower abdomen/uterus and surges
upward through the abdomen and chest (Ling Shu Ch. 38,
Su Wen Ch. 60). ST30 marks where the ascending Chong Mai
intersects the descending ST channel on the anterior wall.

Chong Mai designations:
- **Sea of Blood (血海):** governs blood circulation,
  especially menstrual blood and reproductive function.
- **Sea of the Twelve Meridians (十二经之海):** the "root"
  from which all 12 regular channels derive, connecting the
  Lower Dantian (下丹田) to systemic circulation.

**Eight Confluent relationship:** SP4 (Gongsun) is the
Eight Confluent opening point for Chong Mai. SP4 + PC6
(Neiguan) = the Confluent pair (documented in AP-04). ST30
is the meeting point on the torso — where Chong Mai
physically intersects the regular channel system. Clinical
implication: SP4 (distal, foot) OPENS Chong Mai; ST30
(proximal, abdomen) is the vessel's torso intersection.
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP4 (Chong Mai), AP-09 PC6

**Sea of Nourishment transport point (水谷之海):** per
Ling Shu Ch. 33 (Four Seas), the Sea of Nourishment has
its transport points at **ST30 (Qichong)** and **ST36
(Zusanli)**. Per Obs. 11, the ST channel hosts transport
points for 3 of 4 Seas (Qi at ST9, Nourishment at
ST30+ST36, Blood at ST37+ST39). 5 ST points across 3
seas — more than any other channel.

ST30 + ST36 as a pair:
- ST30 (L1, inguinal): proximal, at the "gate" where Chong
  Mai surges and post-natal nourishment enters distribution.
- ST36 (L4–L5, leg): distal, PROKR2→vagal-adrenal pathway
  for gastric motility and systemic anti-inflammation.
- DIFFERENT locations, DIFFERENT mechanisms, SAME classical
  "Sea" — repeating the pattern that traditional category
  membership does not predict mechanistic unity.
→ Cross-ref: ST36 entry, Obs. 11

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST30 Qichong (气冲). Functions:
regulates Qi dynamics (理气), benefits the Lower Jiao
(利下焦), regulates Chong Mai (调冲脉). Primary indications:
hernia (疝), lower abdominal pain (少腹痛), genital
swelling/pain, irregular menstruation (月经不调), impotence
(阳痿), urinary difficulty (小便不利). As a Chong Mai
meeting point, ST30 addresses reproductive conditions
beyond its segmental territory.

**2. Trigger point:** inguinal/lower rectus junction — TrPs
at the rectus attachment to the pubic crest refer pain to
the suprapubic and inguinal regions. Moderate correspondence.
🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in inguinal hernia
(palpable bulge nearby), groin strain, femoral
lymphadenopathy, and dysmenorrhea with pelvic congestion.
The femoral pulse palpation point may be tender in vascular
conditions. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Lohitaksha Marma (लोहिताक्ष, "Red-
Eyed") — a Sira (blood vessel) Marma at the inguinal crease
where the femoral artery is palpable. Located at the thigh
root/groin fold. ST30 is at the pubic level slightly medial
and superior to the classic Lohitaksha position — approximate
correspondence. SP12 (lateral to the femoral pulse) may be
the closer match. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP12

**5. Head zone:** L1 dermatome = inguinal zone. Overlaps
with urogenital viscerotome (T10–L2 sympathetic outflow to
pelvic organs via superior hypogastric plexus). The nearby
parasympathetic territory (S2–S4 via pelvic splanchnic
nerves) is accessed from the sacral level, not here.
→ Cross-ref: A07 S2.2

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL reaches its inferior attachment
at the pubic crest/symphysis — ST30 is near this endpoint.
The SFL rectus abdominis component terminates here,
transitioning to the lower limb fascial system.
→ Cross-ref: A09

**7. Neurogenic inflammatory spot:** at the edge of Kim
2017's confirmed T10–L1 zone. L1 is the boundary dermatome.
🟡

**8. Cupping point:** not applicable at the inguinal
region. 🔴

**Convergence score: 6/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Head zone,
Anatomy Trains, Neurogenic). The Lohitaksha Marma
correspondence (🟡) is noted but not counted toward the
score due to positional approximation. The inguinal
region's cross-tradition convergence is driven by vascular
landmark access (femoral pulse) rather than tissue-based
treatment targets.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Ling Shu, Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): hernia (疝),
lower abdominal pain (少腹痛), genital swelling/pain, Qi
surging upward (气上冲 — subjective sensation of energy
rising from the lower abdomen), irregular menstruation
(月经不调), impotence (阳痿), urinary difficulty
(小便不利), infertility (不孕).

Nguồn hiện đại: hernia, genital pain/swelling, menstrual
disorders, impotence. ST30 is the primary Chong Mai
meeting point on the ST channel — its reproductive and
menstrual indications extend beyond what L1 segmental
correspondence alone would predict. The classical
"Qi surging upward" symptom (气上冲) may describe the
subjective experience of autonomic arousal in the lower
abdomen, possibly related to pelvic sympathetic activation.

Kinh nghiệm lâm sàng: ST30 appears as a MANDATORY
component in every documented inguinal formula across
four channels:
- **LR12 + CV3 + ST30** — Cold-type hernia (warming Lower
  Jiao, dispersing Cold from Liver channel)
- **SP12 + ST30 + CV3** — inguinal hernia (three inguinal
  points from three channels)
- **SP13 + SP12 + ST30** — inguinal hernia (SP approach +
  ST anchor)
- **LR11 + ST30 + CV2 + BL32 + SP6 + LR3** — Damp-Heat /
  infertility (multi-channel comprehensive)
→ Cross-ref: AP-12 LR12, AP-04 SP12/SP13

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST30 for any indication.
ST30 appears in protocols for male infertility (bilateral
ST30 + CV4 + SP6 + KI3) and dysmenorrhea (ST30 + ST29 +
SP6), but no study isolates ST30's individual contribution.
The Chong Mai concept has no modern validation — no study
has tested whether ST30 stimulation produces effects
specifically related to Chong Mai theory.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal sensitization data for ST30. The inguinal region
shows clinical tenderness in inguinal hernia (mechanical
distension), pelvic inflammatory disease (referred
tenderness via T12–L1 afferents), inguinal lymphadenopathy
(local inflammation), and femoral vascular conditions
(pulse point sensitivity). Whether these represent true
dynamic acupoint behavior per C04 criteria or general
inguinal pathology is unstudied.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST30:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- L1 somatic afferents (iliohypogastric, ilioinguinal,
  genitofemoral nerves) → L1 spinal cord → segmental
  somatovisceral reflex
- Superior hypogastric plexus (T10–L2 sympathetic) receives
  input from L1 level → modulation of uterine tone, vas
  deferens contractility, bladder neck function
- Reproductive effects: L1 sympathetic outflow → pelvic
  organs. Ejaculation and emission are sympathetically
  mediated (T10–L2), explaining male reproductive
  indications. Female menstrual TIMING is hormonal (HPG
  axis), not directly accessible from segmental stimulation
  — mechanism gap for menstrual regulation claims.

A03 (Fascia):
- Rectus abdominis attachment to pubic crest — fascial
  tension at the inguinal ligament. Rectus-inguinal
  ligament junction = potential mechanotransduction site.

A07 (Segmental):
- L1 dermatome corresponds to the inguinal region.
  Classical indications for hernia and genital disorders
  align with L1 territory — CONVERGENCE for local symptoms.
- MISMATCH for menstrual timing and infertility: these
  are HPG-axis mediated. The Chong Mai framework attributes
  these effects to vessel-level mechanism, but no modern
  pathway connects L1 stimulation to hypothalamic-pituitary
  function.

**Chong Mai mechanism — UNKNOWN:**
The classical Chong Mai has no identified anatomical
equivalent as a single structure. Proposed correlates:
- Aorta/IVC: the great vessels traverse Chong Mai's
  classical territory (lower abdomen → chest). 🔴
- Sympathetic trunk: the paravertebral chain connects
  pelvic and thoracic autonomic centers. 🔴
- Mesenteric vascular plexus: blood supply to the GI
  tract, connecting "nourishment" to "blood." 🔴
None established. Chong Mai may represent functional
integration across multiple anatomical systems rather than
a single structure.

**Hernia mechanism — GAP:**
Inguinal hernia is a structural defect (weakened abdominal
wall/inguinal floor). Needling at ST30 cannot repair fascial
integrity. Any therapeutic effect would be indirect: pain
relief via L1 nerve modulation, or local muscle tone
improvement through fascial mechanotransduction. Large
claim-to-mechanism gap. 🔴

**Sea of Nourishment pairing — mechanistic divergence:**
ST30 (L1, segmental/sympathetic) and ST36 (L4–L5, PROKR2→
vagal) are paired for the same "Sea" but use completely
different neural pathways. This parallels the ST25/ST36
contrast within the same meridian: same channel, same organ
target, opposite mechanisms. The "Sea of Nourishment" is a
functional concept grouping mechanistically independent
points.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 10–15 mm perpendicular. CRITICAL: palpate
femoral pulse before insertion. Insert MEDIAL to the pulse,
directing the needle away from the artery. Fine gauge
needle (0.20–0.25 mm). De Qi: distension in the inguinal
region, sometimes radiating to the lower abdomen or
genitalia. Do NOT manipulate aggressively — proximity to
femoral vessels and inguinal canal.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

EA: HIGH CAUTION near femoral vessels. If bilateral ST30
is used, do NOT cross electrodes across the femoral artery.
Low intensity only. Some practitioners prefer non-electrical
techniques at this point.

Bấm huyệt (B02): gentle sustained pressure at the inguinal
region, medial to the femoral pulse. Used for hernia-related
discomfort and dysmenorrhea. Avoid strong compression
directly on the femoral pulse.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa for cold-pattern Lower Jiao
conditions — hernia (寒疝), amenorrhea from Cold, impotence.
Classical sources emphasize warming techniques at ST30 for
Chong Mai disorders. Moxa box covering bilateral ST30 + CV4
+ CV3 = standard Lower Jiao warming protocol.

### An toàn

**HIGH VASCULAR RISK.** Femoral artery is the primary
danger — the artery is superficially palpable at this level.
Deep or laterally directed needling risks arterial puncture
→ hemorrhage, pseudoaneurysm, or arteriovenous fistula.

Safety protocol:
- ALWAYS palpate femoral pulse before needling
- Insert MEDIAL to the pulse, never on or lateral to it
- Perpendicular insertion only — no lateral angulation
- Fine gauge needle, shallow depth (10–15 mm maximum)
- Apply firm pressure for 5 minutes post-withdrawal
- Contraindicated in patients on anticoagulants (high
  hemorrhage risk at this vascular site)

Peritoneal puncture risk: present (below arcuate line),
but secondary concern compared to vascular risk.

Inguinal hernia: in patients with known hernial defect,
assess whether the defect alters normal tissue anatomy
before needling.

Pregnancy: CONTRAINDICATED for deep needling in all
trimesters (proximity to uterus, pelvic vessels). Some
classical sources permit superficial stimulation for labor
facilitation, but modern practice exercises extreme caution.

---

### Batch 6 Architectural Observations

> **Observation 19 — VISCEROTOME CONTINUATION: T10→L1,
> INTESTINAL→UROGENITAL GRADIENT COMPLETES THE ABDOMINAL
> MAP.**
>
> The lower abdominal segment (ST26–ST30) continues the
> viscerotome gradient begun in Obs. 17 (T7→T10):
>
> | Level | Points | Segment | Primary indications |
> |-------|--------|---------|---------------------|
> | Peri-umbilical | ST26 | T10–T11 | Abdominal pain (broad) |
> | Lower | ST27 | T11 | + urinary, seminal emission |
> | Hypogastric | ST28 | T11–T12 | Urinary retention, edema |
> | Suprapubic | ST29 | T12–L1 | Hernia, menstrual, reproductive |
> | Inguinal | ST30 | L1 | Chong Mai, reproductive, hernia |
>
> Combined with Obs. 17 (T7→T10), the full 12-point ST
> abdominal series (ST19–ST30) encodes the COMPLETE anterior
> viscerotome from gastric (T7) through intestinal (T10)
> to urogenital (L1) — a 7-segment span representing the
> entire gastrointestinal and urogenital tract in segmental
> order. This is the longest continuous segmental-indication
> mapping in the Atlas.
>
> The lower sequence adds a dimension absent from the upper:
> DUAL-SEX indications. ST29 covers both male (impotence,
> genital retraction) and female (amenorrhea, prolapse)
> reproductive conditions. The gastric segment (ST19–ST24)
> was sex-neutral; the reproductive segment (ST28–ST30) is
> sex-differentiated.
>
> **Observation 20 — CONVERGENCE GRADIENT: NAVEL PEAK WITH
> ASYMMETRIC DECLINE.**
>
> The convergence score across the ST channel's torso
> segment forms an asymmetric gradient centered on the
> navel:
>
> | Region | Points | Score | Key factor |
> |--------|--------|-------|------------|
> | Chest | ST13–ST18 | 3/8 | Cardiac paradox (no cardiac indications in cardiac viscerotome) |
> | Upper abdomen | ST19–ST24 | 5/8 | Gastric viscerotome match; neurogenic UNCONFIRMED at T7–T9 |
> | **Navel** | **ST25** | **7/8** | **Nabhi Marma + Kim 2017 confirmed T10 + navel amplifier** |
> | Lower abdomen | ST26–ST30 | 6/8 | Kim 2017 confirmed T10–L1 → neurogenic COUNTED |
>
> The decline from the navel peak is ASYMMETRIC: below the
> navel scores 6/8 (vs 5/8 above) because Kim 2017's
> confirmed neurogenic zone (T10–L1) covers the lower
> abdomen but NOT the upper abdomen (T7–T9). The navel
> uniquely adds Nabhi Marma — a convergence amplifier that
> does not extend beyond the umbilical level.
>
> The overall pattern: convergence increases where modern
> science provides confirming data (neurogenic spots, Marma
> overlap) and decreases where it does not. The ABSENCE of
> modern neurogenic data at the upper abdomen (T7–T9) and
> chest (T1–T6) depresses convergence scores — raising the
> question of whether this reflects a genuine cross-tradition
> signal gap or a measurement artifact that future studies
> could resolve.
>
> **Observation 21 — INGUINAL CONVERGENCE: FOUR CHANNELS
> BRACKET THE FEMORAL NEUROVASCULAR BUNDLE.**
>
> The inguinal region contains the densest cross-channel
> convergence in the Atlas below the neck:
>
> | Point | Channel | Distance | Relative to vessels | Risk |
> |-------|---------|----------|---------------------|------|
> | CV2 | Ren Mai | 0 (midline) | Pubic symphysis | LOW |
> | **ST30** | **Stomach** | **2 cun** | **Medial to vessels** | **HIGH** |
