> | LR12 | Liver | 2.5 cun | Medial to femoral vein | HIGH |
> | SP12 | Spleen | 3.5 cun | Lateral to femoral artery | HIGH |
> | GB27 | Gallbladder | ASIS | Lateral abdomen | LOW |
>
> Three channels' highest-risk points (LR12, ST30, SP12)
> occur within 1.5 cun of each other, flanking the femoral
> neurovascular bundle. The medial-to-lateral arrangement:
> ST30 → LR12 → femoral Vein → femoral Artery → femoral
> Nerve → SP12. Cf. ST12 (supraclavicular fossa) where all
> Yang channels cross above the brachial plexus — two
> anatomical bottlenecks (inguinal and supraclavicular)
> concentrate multi-channel convergence at neurovascular
> transit points.
>
> Cross-channel formulas ALL include ST30 as mandatory
> anchor:
> - LR12 + CV3 + **ST30** (Cold-type hernia)
> - SP12 + **ST30** + CV3 (inguinal hernia)
> - SP13 + SP12 + **ST30** (inguinal hernia)
> - LR11 + **ST30** + CV2 + BL32 + SP6 + LR3 (infertility)
>
> ST30 functions as the ANCHOR point for all inguinal
> prescriptions — every documented formula includes it.
> Parallel: ST36 anchors lower leg protocols, ST25 anchors
> intestinal protocols. The ST channel provides the mandatory
> local component at three key levels of the trunk and limb.

---

## ST31 — Bễ Quan (髀关, Bì Guān)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Thigh Gate" (髀 = thigh/hip, 关 = gate/pass) — named for
its position at the "gateway" from the trunk to the thigh,
where the channel crosses the inguinal ligament to enter
the lower limb. Located on the anterior thigh, directly
below the anterior superior iliac spine (ASIS), in the
depression lateral to the sartorius muscle when the thigh
is flexed. ST31 is the FIRST lower limb point on the ST
channel — the transition from the 12-point rectus abdominis
corridor (ST19–ST30) to the quadriceps group. The name
continues the "gate" (关/门) naming pattern on the ST
channel: ST21 梁门 (Beam Gate), ST22 关门 (Shut Gate),
ST24 滑肉门 (Slippery Flesh Gate), ST31 髀关 (Thigh Gate)
— each marking an anatomical boundary or transition zone.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the anterior thigh,
in the depression lateral to the sartorius muscle, at the
level of the lower border of the pubic symphysis, directly
below the anterior superior iliac spine (ASIS).

Anatomical landmarks: at the junction of TFL (tensor
fasciae latae) and sartorius origins from the ASIS. Between
ST30 (inguinal, 5 cun below umbilicus) and ST32 (6 cun
above patella, mid-thigh). The inguinal ligament runs from
the ASIS to the pubic tubercle — ST31 lies just distal to
this landmark, marking the trunk-to-limb transition.

Needle depth: 25–40 mm perpendicular (heavy muscular tissue
at proximal thigh).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Lateral femoral cutaneous nerve (L2–L3)** — pure sensory,
  emerges near ASIS under the inguinal ligament. Responsible
  for anterolateral thigh sensation. The nerve's exit point
  (near the ASIS) places it directly at the ST31 level.
- **Femoral nerve (L2–L4)** — the deep motor and sensory
  trunk lies deep in the femoral triangle, medial to ST31.
  Motor branches to sartorius and rectus femoris arise at
  or near this level.
- Spinal segment: L2–L3
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1, A07

Vascular: branches of the lateral circumflex femoral artery
(ascending branch), superficial circumflex iliac artery.
The femoral artery is medial and deep — NOT at immediate
risk from standard perpendicular needling.

Tissue layers (NEW corridor — transition from abdominal
to limb):
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue → fascia lata
- Sartorius muscle (or lateral to it) → rectus femoris
  (deep)
- Vastus intermedius → periosteum of femur (very deep,
  not reached by standard needling)

This tissue composition is entirely different from the
rectus abdominis corridor (ST19–ST30). There is NO
peritoneal cavity access — the femur and quadriceps group
occupy the deep space. Safety profile shifts from
peritoneal puncture risk to muscular-only risk.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST31 Biguan — thigh pain (髀痛), hip pain,
weakness and atrophy of the lower limbs (下肢痿痹),
hemiplegia (半身不遂), inguinal and lumbar pain.
**2. Trigger point:** sartorius proximal TrPs and TFL
TrPs both refer pain to the anterolateral hip and proximal
thigh. Rectus femoris proximal TrPs also active in this
region. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11
**3. Ashi:** tender in hip flexor strain, meralgia
paresthetica (lateral femoral cutaneous nerve entrapment
at the ASIS), hip osteoarthritis with anterior thigh
referral.
**4. Marma:** Urvi Marma (ऊर्वी, "thigh") — Sira (blood
vessel) Marma on the mid-thigh per classical descriptions.
ST31 is at the PROXIMAL thigh (near ASIS), not mid-thigh
— positional mismatch. ST32 (mid-thigh) may be closer. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** L2–L3 dermatome — primarily somatic
territory for the anterior thigh. No specific visceral
correspondence. 🔴
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL — rectus femoris is a core
component of the Superficial Front Line, continuous from
tibialis anterior (below) through the quadriceps to the
anterior trunk. ST31 is at the proximal origin of this
limb segment.
→ Cross-ref: A09
**7. Neurogenic spot:** no confirmed data for the proximal
thigh. The lateral femoral cutaneous nerve's exit point
near the ASIS is a known site of nerve entrapment
(meralgia paresthetica), but this is a compression
neuropathy, not a neurogenic inflammatory spot per C04
criteria. 🔴
**8. Cupping:** applicable to the thigh but not a
traditional cupping target at this specific site. 🔴

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Significant DROP from the abdominal series
(5–7/8) — the transition to the lower limb removes Head
zone (no visceral), neurogenic (no confirmed data), Marma
(positional mismatch), and cupping convergence. Purely
musculoskeletal cross-tradition signal.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): thigh pain and numbness
(髀痹不仁), hip pain (跨痛), lower limb weakness/atrophy
(下肢痿弱), inguinal pain (少腹引痛), hemiplegia (偏枯).

Nguồn hiện đại: thigh pain, hip pain, lower limb
paralysis/weakness, meralgia paresthetica (lateral femoral
cutaneous nerve compression at ASIS). ST31 is primarily a
LOCAL musculoskeletal point — its indications map directly
to its L2–L3 innervation territory without the visceral
extensions seen in the abdominal series.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST31. ST31 appears in
some hemiplegia rehabilitation protocols (with ST36, SP6,
GB30, LI11) but has no individual evidence.

### An toàn

LOW risk — the safest region since entering the trunk
segment at ST9. No peritoneal access (femur and quadriceps
occupy the deep space). No major vessels at standard needle
depth (the femoral artery is medial and deep in the femoral
triangle).

The lateral femoral cutaneous nerve passes near or through
ST31's zone — sharp electric pain radiating down the
anterolateral thigh during needling indicates nerve contact.
Redirect if this occurs.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa
prohibition.

---

## ST32 — Phục Thố (伏兔, Fú Tù)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Crouching Rabbit" (伏 = crouching/prostrate, 兔 = rabbit)
— named for the prominent bulge of the rectus femoris
muscle when the quadriceps contracts, resembling a
crouching rabbit on the anterior thigh. Located 6 cun
above the superior border of the patella, on the line
connecting ASIS to the superolateral corner of the
patella. ST32 is the mid-thigh point of the ST channel,
positioned over the largest cross-section of the
quadriceps group.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the anterolateral
thigh, 6 cun above the superior border of the patella, on
the line connecting the anterior superior iliac spine
(ASIS) and the superolateral corner of the patella.

Anatomical landmarks: on the rectus femoris muscle belly
— its thickest portion. Between ST31 (proximal, at ASIS
level) and ST33 (3 cun above patella). The point is
approximately at mid-thigh level on most patients.

Needle depth: 25–38 mm perpendicular (thick muscular
tissue — rectus femoris muscle belly).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior division of femoral nerve (L2–L4)** — motor
  branches to rectus femoris pass through or near this
  zone. The nerve to rectus femoris typically enters the
  muscle at the proximal 1/3 — at or near ST32's level.
- **Intermediate cutaneous nerve of thigh (L2–L3)** —
  anterior branch of the femoral nerve providing cutaneous
  supply to the mid-anterior thigh.
- **Lateral femoral cutaneous nerve (L2–L3)** may
  contribute lateral cutaneous supply at this level.
- Spinal segment: L2–L4
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: muscular branches of the femoral artery (via
profunda femoris/deep femoral artery perforating branches).
The femoral artery itself lies in the adductor canal
medially — not directly at risk.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → fascia lata →
rectus femoris → vastus intermedius → periosteum of femoral
shaft. Same quadriceps corridor as ST31, at maximum muscle
bulk.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST32 Futu — thigh pain (股痛), knee pain and
soreness (膝酸), lower limb paralysis/numbness (下肢麻痹),
inguinal region cold (腹股沟冷), beriberi (脚气). Classical
sources note warming techniques at this point for cold-
pattern lower limb conditions.
**2. Trigger point:** rectus femoris central TrPs — the
classic "satellite TrP" of hip trigger points. Refer pain
to the anterior thigh and anterior knee. Strong
correspondence at the muscle belly maximum. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11
**3. Ashi:** tender in quadriceps strain, femoral nerve
irritation, post-surgical knee rehabilitation (quadriceps
atrophy point).
**4. Marma:** Urvi Marma (ऊर्वी, "thigh") — Sira (blood
vessel) Marma on the mid-thigh. ST32's mid-thigh position
approximates this description better than ST31 (too
proximal) or ST33 (too distal). Classical Sushruta location
refers to the femoral vessels on the medial thigh, while
ST32 is anterolateral. 🟡 approximate correspondence.
**5. Head zone:** L2–L3 dermatome — primarily somatic. 🔴
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL — rectus femoris muscle belly.
The SFL's longest single-muscle component. ST32 marks the
approximate center of this component.
→ Cross-ref: A09
**7. Neurogenic spot:** no confirmed data. 🔴
**8. Cupping:** applicable to the mid-thigh; occasional
cupping practice for quadriceps strain recovery. 🔴

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Urvi Marma noted (🟡) but not counted — classical
description refers to the femoral vessels on the medial
thigh, while ST32 is anterolateral.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): thigh pain (股痛), knee
pain and soreness (膝酸), lower limb numbness and paralysis
(下肢不仁), abdominal distension (腹胀), inguinal cold
(腹股沟寒), hernia (疝), beriberi (脚气).

Nguồn hiện đại: thigh pain, knee pain, lower limb
paralysis/weakness. The classical "cold" indications
(inguinal cold, cold hernia) reflect the traditional
association of 伏兔 with Yang warming function in the
quadriceps region. The name "Crouching Rabbit" references
the muscular bulge, not a clinical function — one of few
ST names encoding anatomy rather than therapeutic purpose.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST32.

### An toàn

LOW risk — deep muscular tissue throughout. No visceral or
major vascular access at standard needle depth. The femoral
artery runs in the adductor canal medially, well separated
from the anterolateral needling trajectory.

In patients with femoral nerve injury or neuropathy,
needling in the rectus femoris may elicit strong involuntary
muscle contraction — use caution with electroacupuncture.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa
prohibition.

---

## ST33 — Âm Thị (阴市, Yīn Shì)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Yin Market" (阴 = yin/shade/cold, 市 = market/city) —
traditionally interpreted as "the marketplace of Yin
(cold)" — the point where cold pathology accumulates and
is treated. Classical sources emphasize its role in treating
cold-pattern conditions of the abdomen and knee. Located
3 cun above the superior border of the patella, on the
line connecting ASIS to the superolateral corner of the
patella. ST33 is in the distal quadriceps region where the
muscle mass begins to thin toward the tendinous junction.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the anterolateral
thigh, 3 cun above the superior border of the patella, on
the line connecting the ASIS and the superolateral corner
of the patella.

Anatomical landmarks: at the junction of the rectus
femoris and vastus lateralis, where the muscle bellies
begin to converge toward their common tendon (quadriceps
tendon). Between ST32 (6 cun above patella) and ST34
(2 cun above patella).

Needle depth: 20–30 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior division of femoral nerve (L2–L4)** — motor
  branches to vastus lateralis and rectus femoris.
- **Intermediate and lateral cutaneous nerves of thigh
  (L2–L3)** — cutaneous supply to the anterolateral distal
  thigh.
- Spinal segment: L2–L4
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: descending branch of the lateral circumflex
femoral artery; muscular branches from the femoral artery
via the adductor canal.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → fascia lata →
rectus femoris / vastus lateralis junction → vastus
intermedius → periosteum. The muscle mass is thinner here
than at ST32 — the transition from bulk quadriceps to
converging tendons.

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST33 Yinshi — knee pain and soreness
(膝酸痛冷), lower limb numbness (下肢麻木), abdominal
distension and cold (腹胀冷), hernia. Traditional emphasis
on COLD-pattern conditions distinguishes ST33 from the
adjacent pain-focused points.
**2. Trigger point:** vastus lateralis distal TrPs and
rectus femoris distal TrPs — refer pain to the anterior
and lateral knee. 🟡
**3. Ashi:** tender in distal quadriceps strain,
suprapatellar bursitis, anterior knee pain syndrome.
**4. Marma:** no specific Marma at this level. 🔴
**5. Head zone:** L2–L3 dermatome — somatic. 🔴
**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL — distal quadriceps, approaching
the musculotendinous junction.
→ Cross-ref: A09
**7. Neurogenic spot:** no confirmed data. 🔴
**8. Cupping:** not typical. 🔴

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Consistent with the thigh corridor baseline
established at ST31–ST32.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): knee cold, pain and
soreness (膝酸痛冷), lower limb numbness (下肢麻木/不仁),
abdominal distension (腹胀), inguinal cold (阴疝寒冷).
Classical sources particularly emphasize "cold" (寒/冷)
conditions — consistent with the name "Yin Market" encoding
cold pathology.

Nguồn hiện đại: knee pain, lower limb weakness. The
traditional cold emphasis is unique among the ST thigh
points — ST31 and ST32 focus on pain/paralysis, while ST33
specifically addresses cold-pattern disorders. This may
reflect the point's position at the muscle-tendon
transition (less vascular warmth than the muscle belly
at ST32).

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST33.

### An toàn

LOW risk — muscular tissue only. Same safety profile as
ST31–ST32. No visceral or major vascular access.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa
prohibition.

---

## ST34 — Lương Khâu (梁丘, Liáng Qiū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Beam Hill" (梁 = beam/ridge, 丘 = hill/mound) — named for
the prominent bony ridge formed by the lateral border of
the patella and the quadriceps tendon, palpable as a
"beam-like hill" above the knee. Located 2 cun above the
superolateral border of the patella, in the depression
between the rectus femoris tendon and the vastus lateralis.

ST34 is the **Xi-Cleft (郄穴)** of the Stomach channel —
the 10th Xi-Cleft point documented in the Atlas and the
5th YANG Xi-Cleft (after LI7, SI6, TE7, GB36). Xi-Cleft
points are where Qi and Blood accumulate deeply, used for
ACUTE conditions of their respective organs. The Yang
Xi-Cleft functional split established in AP-02 predicts
that ST34 should treat acute PAIN/INFLAMMATORY conditions
(Yang pattern) rather than blood conditions (Yin pattern)
— and its classical indications (acute gastric pain)
confirm this prediction.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the anterolateral
thigh, 2 cun above the superolateral border of the
patella, in the depression between the lateral border of
the rectus femoris tendon and the vastus lateralis.

Anatomical landmarks: in the inter-muscular groove between
the rectus femoris (medial) and the vastus lateralis
(lateral), at the level where both muscles are transitioning
from belly to tendon. Between ST33 (3 cun above patella)
and ST35 (lateral infrapatellar fossa).

Needle depth: 15–25 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique
toward the knee.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Anterior division of femoral nerve (L2–L4)** — terminal
  motor branches to vastus lateralis and rectus femoris in
  the distal thigh.
- **Lateral cutaneous nerve of thigh (L2–L3)** — cutaneous
  supply to the lateral distal thigh.
- **Intermediate cutaneous nerve of thigh (L2–L3)** may
  contribute.
- Spinal segment: L2–L4
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: descending genicular artery (from femoral artery
in the adductor hiatus); lateral superior genicular artery.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → fascia lata →
inter-muscular septum between rectus femoris tendon and
vastus lateralis → suprapatellar bursa (deep). The inter-
muscular groove provides a natural tissue cleft — a common
anatomical feature at Xi-Cleft point locations (cf. LI7
between ECRB and ECRL, SI6 between ECU and extensor digiti
minimi, TE7 between ED and EDM, GB36 between peroneus
longus and extensor digitorum longus).

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Xi-Cleft (郄穴)** of the Stomach channel (Foot Yangming).

Xi-Cleft architectural inventory — complete Yang set:

| Xi-Cleft | Channel | Polarity | Convergence | Primary acute function | Evidence |
|----------|---------|----------|-------------|------------------------|----------|
| LI7 | Large Intestine | Yang | 3/8 | Face/oral pain (Yangming face) | 🔴 |
| SI6 | Small Intestine | Yang | 2/8 | MSK pain + vision (Taiyang) | 🔴 |
| TE7 | Triple Burner | Yang | 2/8 | Sudden deafness (Shaoyang ear) | 🔴 |
| GB36 | Gallbladder | Yang | 3/8 | Lateral body pain (Shaoyang lateral) | 🔴 |
| **ST34** | **Stomach** | **Yang** | **4/8** | **Acute gastric pain (Yangming GI)** | **🔴** |

ST34 at 4/8 is the HIGHEST convergence among all Xi-Cleft
points (tied with HT6 and PC4). The median Xi-Cleft
convergence is 3/8 — consistently lower than other point
categories (He-Sea, Yuan-Source), reflecting Xi-Cleft
points' typical inter-muscular septum locations rather
than anatomical landmarks.

Yin Xi-Cleft comparison — all target BLOOD conditions:

| Xi-Cleft | Channel | Primary blood function |
|----------|---------|------------------------|
| LU6 | Lung | Hemoptysis |
| HT6 | Heart | Night sweats (cardiac blood) |
| PC4 | Pericardium | Chest blood/cardiac emergency |
| SP8 | Spleen | Menstrual blood + acute pain |
| LR6 | Liver | Uterine bleeding, hemorrhage |

**5/5 Yin = blood. 5/5 Yang = channel-domain pain/
inflammation.** The Yin-Yang functional divergence at
Xi-Cleft is now fully documented across all 10 regular
meridian Xi-Cleft points. Proposed mechanism (AP-02
Obs. 10): Yin channels follow arteries → vascular/blood
effects; Yang channels follow nerves in inter-muscular
septa → neural/inflammatory effects.
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI7 (Obs. 10), AP-10 TE7, AP-11 GB36

Xi-Cleft evidence status: **10/10 documented Xi-Cleft
points are 🔴** (SP8 has dynamic acupoint sensitization
data from Chen 2015 but no clinical trial). Xi-Cleft
remains the LEAST-STUDIED point category in the Atlas.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST34 Liangqiu (梁丘). Functions:
regulates Stomach Qi (和胃理气), alleviates acute pain
(止痛), relaxes sinews and activates collaterals (舒筋通络).
Primary indications: acute gastric pain (胃脘急痛), knee
pain and swelling (膝肿痛), mastitis (乳痈), diarrhea.
As Xi-Cleft, ST34 is the EMERGENCY point for acute
Stomach conditions — used when sudden gastric pain requires
immediate intervention. ST36 (He-Sea, 3 cun below ST35)
handles chronic regulation; ST34 (Xi-Cleft, 2 cun above
patella) handles acute crises.

**2. Trigger point:** rectus femoris/vastus lateralis
junction TrPs — distal TrPs in both muscles refer pain to
the anterior knee and patellar region. The inter-muscular
groove corresponds to a common TrP location. Strong
correspondence for the local (knee) indications; no TrP
explanation for the distant (gastric) indications. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in suprapatellar
bursitis, quadriceps tendinopathy, anterior knee pain
syndrome. May be tender during acute gastric episodes
(referred tenderness via sympathetic activation? — not
formally tested). 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Janu Marma (जानु, "knee") — Sandhi
(joint) Marma at the knee, but classical descriptions
place it AT the knee joint, not 2 cun above. ST35 (at the
infrapatellar fossa) is the closer Janu match. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** L2–L3 dermatome — primarily somatic
(anterior thigh). No direct correspondence with the
gastric viscerotome (T6–T9). The distant gastric
indication is ABOVE the local dermatome — the same "gap"
as ST36 (L4–L5) treating gastric conditions (T6–T9),
resolved at ST36 by the PROKR2→vagal supraspinal pathway.
ST34 has no known equivalent supraspinal mechanism. 🔴

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL — quadriceps tendon transition
zone, at the musculotendinous junction.
→ Cross-ref: A09

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no confirmed data. 🔴

**8. Cupping:** not typical for the suprapatellar area. 🔴

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Consistent with the thigh corridor and with the
Xi-Cleft category median (3/8 across all documented
Xi-Cleft points). Xi-Cleft points cluster in inter-muscular
septa — anatomically less distinctive cross-traditionally
than landmark locations (joints, foramina, arteries).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): acute gastric pain
(胃脘急痛), knee pain and swelling (膝肿痛), inability
to flex/extend the knee, mastitis (乳痈), diarrhea (泄泻),
lower limb numbness.

Nguồn hiện đại: acute gastric pain (primary Xi-Cleft
indication), knee pain, quadriceps strain. The acute
gastric pain indication is the definitive Xi-Cleft
function — used clinically when a patient presents with
sudden-onset epigastric pain that requires immediate
needling. ST36 (He-Sea, below the knee) handles chronic
regulation; ST34 (Xi-Cleft, above the patella) handles
acute crises.

Classical combinations:
- **ST34 + CV12 + PC6 + ST36** — acute gastric pain
  (Xi-Cleft + Front-Mu + Luo-Connecting + He-Sea: four
  categories addressing the same organ from different
  access points)
- **ST34 + ST35 + EX-LE4/5 (Xiyan)** — knee pain (local
  point cluster)

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST34 for either gastric
or knee indications. ST34 appears in some acute gastric
pain protocols but always combined with ST36 and/or CV12
— no study tests ST34's individual contribution.

Xi-Cleft evidence status: 10/10 documented Xi-Cleft
points = 🔴. The ENTIRE category lacks clinical isolation
studies despite frequent clinical use for acute conditions.
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI7, AP-04 SP8

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal sensitization data for ST34 specifically.
However, the Xi-Cleft concept per Ling Shu implies dynamic
behavior: Xi-Cleft points "activate" (become tender, alter
tissue properties) during acute pathology of their
associated organ. SP8 (Spleen Xi-Cleft) demonstrated
experimental sensitization under visceral pathology
(Chen 2015) — the only documented Xi-Cleft sensitization
data in the Atlas.

Clinically, acupuncturists report increased tenderness at
ST34 during acute gastric episodes — anecdotal but
consistent with the dynamic acupoint model. Whether this
represents formal C04 sensitization (altered pressure pain
threshold, enhanced neurogenic response) or general
abdominal wall guarding transmitted via sympathetic
activation to the L2–L4 segment is unknown.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4, AP-04 SP8 (Chen 2015)

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST34:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- L2–L4 femoral nerve afferents → L2–L4 spinal cord →
  segmental processing.
- Gastric Xi-Cleft indication GAP: the stomach's
  viscerotome is T6–T9, but ST34's segmental level is
  L2–L4. NO direct segmental connection. The distant
  gastric effect requires a SUPRASPINAL pathway.
- Possible pathway: L2–L4 somatic input → ascending
  spinal tract → brainstem integration → vagal efferent
  modulation of gastric function. This parallels the ST36
  PROKR2 pathway (L4–L5 → vagal) but at a different
  spinal level and with NO identified molecular mediator.
- Note: L2 sympathetic outflow reaches the inferior
  mesenteric ganglion (lower GI/pelvic), NOT the celiac
  ganglion (gastric). Somatosympathetic reflex from L2–L4
  cannot directly target the stomach. Supraspinal mediation
  is the only viable pathway for the gastric indication.

A03 (Fascia):
- Inter-muscular septum between rectus femoris and vastus
  lateralis — fascial cleft enriched in nerve endings and
  proprioceptors. May provide enhanced neural recruitment
  per unit stimulation input.

A07 (Segmental):
- LOCAL (knee) indications: L2–L4 matches. CONVERGENCE.
- DISTANT (gastric) indications: T6–T9 target, L2–L4
  source. MISMATCH. Requires supraspinal mediation.

**Xi-Cleft inter-muscular septum hypothesis:** Xi-Cleft
points' typical location in tissue clefts (inter-muscular
septa, periosteal grooves) may explain their "emergency"
function. At tissue clefts, nerve fibers are more
concentrated and accessible, allowing stronger peripheral
nerve recruitment at lower stimulation thresholds. This
could explain why Xi-Cleft points are clinically preferred
for acute conditions — not because they activate different
pathways, but because they achieve greater neural
recruitment. Untested. 🔴

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 15–25 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique
toward the knee. The inter-muscular groove provides a
natural tissue channel for needle insertion. De Qi:
distension and heaviness in the suprapatellar region,
sometimes with sensation propagating toward the knee or
up the anterior thigh.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

EA: low-to-medium frequency. For acute gastric pain,
bilateral ST34 EA is a common clinical approach. ST34-
to-ST36 electrode placement follows the SFL trajectory.

Bấm huyệt (B02): firm sustained pressure in the inter-
muscular groove. The groove provides a tactile landmark
for self-treatment — palpable between the two tendinous
structures. Patient can self-apply during acute gastric
episodes.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa for chronic knee conditions with
cold/dampness pattern. Not typically moxaed for acute
gastric conditions (acute Xi-Cleft use = needling).

### An toàn

LOW risk — muscular/tendinous tissue only. No visceral or
major vascular access. The descending genicular artery is
small and deep — not at meaningful risk from standard
needling.

Suprapatellar bursa: deep to the insertion point.
Aggressive deep needling could enter the bursa — generally
not harmful but introduces infection risk to a synovial
space. Maintain sterile technique.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa
prohibition.

---

## ST35 — Độc Tị (犊鼻, Dú Bí)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Calf's Nose" (犊 = calf/young cow, 鼻 = nose) — named for
the two depressions flanking the patellar ligament when the
knee is flexed, which together resemble a calf's nostrils.
ST35 occupies the LATERAL depression (lateral knee eye);
the MEDIAL depression is the extra point EX-LE4/5 (medial
knee eye, 内膝眼). Together they form the **Xiyan pair
(膝眼, "Knee Eyes")** — the most frequently used local
combination for knee pathology across all documented
protocols.

ST35 is the primary KNEE POINT on the Stomach channel,
positioned at the joint itself — the culmination of the
descending thigh segment (ST31–ST34) and the transition
to the lower leg where ST36 (Zusanli) begins the most
evidence-rich territory on any channel.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the knee, in the
depression lateral to the patellar ligament, when the knee
is flexed.

Anatomical landmarks: in the lateral infrapatellar fossa.
The patellar ligament runs from the inferior pole of the
patella to the tibial tuberosity — ST35 is in the soft
tissue depression immediately lateral to this ligament.
The medial equivalent (EX-LE4/5) is in the medial
infrapatellar fossa.

Spatial relationships at the knee:
- **ST35**: lateral infrapatellar fossa (anterior-lateral)
- **EX-LE4/5**: medial infrapatellar fossa (anterior-medial)
- **SP9**: medial knee, pes anserinus (below joint line)
- **GB34**: lateral knee, fibular head (below joint line)
- **LR7**: posterior-medial knee, gastrocnemius (above joint)
- **LR8**: medial popliteal crease (at joint line)
- **ST36**: anterolateral leg, 3 cun below ST35

Between ST34 (2 cun above patella) and ST36 (3 cun below
ST35). The channel trajectory crosses the patella and
continues to the anterolateral leg.

Needle depth: 15–25 mm oblique, directed toward the center
of the joint space or toward the opposite knee eye. The
infrapatellar fat pad (Hoffa's fat pad) lies deep to the
insertion point.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve (L3–L4)** —
  the primary cutaneous nerve of the anterior knee. This
  branch of the saphenous nerve (itself a terminal branch
  of the femoral nerve) crosses the patellar ligament,
  providing sensation to the skin below the patella.
- **Lateral articular branches from common peroneal nerve
  (L4–S1)** — joint afferents to the lateral knee
  compartment.
- **Recurrent articular branches from tibial nerve** —
  joint afferents.
- Spinal segment: L3–L4 (cutaneous); L4–S1 (articular)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: inferior lateral genicular artery (branch of
popliteal artery), patellar arterial rete (anastomotic
network around the patella).

Tissue layers:
- Skin → subcutaneous tissue → peritenon/retinaculum
- **Infrapatellar fat pad (Hoffa's fat pad)** — highly
  innervated and vascularized adipose tissue within the
  knee joint space. Contains abundant type IVa nerve
  fibers (nociceptors) and sympathetic fibers. The fat
  pad's rich innervation contributes to anterior knee
  pain (Hoffa's disease/fat pad impingement).
- Joint capsule / synovial membrane

Neural transition: ST35 marks the shift from FEMORAL nerve
territory (L2–L4, from ST31–ST34) to a zone of overlap
where femoral terminal branches (saphenous) and COMMON
PERONEAL (L4–S1) articular branches converge. Below ST35,
ST36 enters full deep peroneal nerve territory — the same
nerve system that GB30 accesses at its trunk (sciatic) and
GB34 accesses pre-bifurcation (common peroneal).
→ Cross-ref: AP-11 GB30, GB34

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

No Five Shu, Yuan-Source, Luo-Connecting, Xi-Cleft,
Back-Shu, Front-Mu, Command Point, Ma Dan-yang Star,
Ghost Point, or Window of Heaven designation.

ST35 lacks formal category designation but holds an
unofficial status as the PRIMARY local knee point in the
TCM therapeutic system. Its importance derives from clinical
frequency, not classical categorization — parallel to LI15
(most-included shoulder point, no special category).
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI15

The Xiyan pair:
- **ST35 (Dubi)**: lateral knee eye — regular meridian
  point (Foot Yangming)
- **EX-LE4/5 (Xi Nei Yan, 膝内眼)**: medial knee eye —
  EXTRA point (not on any regular meridian). WHO coding
  varies by edition (EX-LE2 in some, EX-LE4 or EX-LE5
  in others); the pair concept is stable regardless of
  code.
- Together = "膝眼" (Knee Eyes), used as a paired unit in
  the majority of knee acupuncture protocols documented
  across all completed Atlas channels.
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP9, AP-11 GB34, AP-12 LR7/LR8

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST35 Dubi (犊鼻). Functions: dispels
Wind-Damp from the knee (祛风湿), activates the channel
and alleviates pain (通络止痛), benefits the knee joint
(利膝关节). Primary indications: knee pain (膝痛), knee
swelling (膝肿), difficulty in flexion/extension (屈伸不利),
patellar ligament pain, knee Bi syndrome (膝痹). ST35 is
the single most important anterior knee access point — its
location at the infrapatellar fossa provides direct needle
access to the joint capsule through the fat pad.

**2. Trigger point:** infrapatellar fat pad TrPs and
patellar tendon enthesopathic points. Fat pad impingement
(Hoffa's disease) creates localized pain at exactly the
ST35 location. Quadriceps insertion TrPs also refer to
this area. Strong correspondence. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: B11

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in virtually ALL
anterior knee pathologies — osteoarthritis, patellar
tendinopathy, fat pad impingement, meniscal tear (anterior
horn), chondromalacia patellae. Among the highest-frequency
Ashi points in the lower limb. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Janu Marma (जानु, "knee") — Sandhi
(joint) Marma at the knee. Classical Sushruta description:
located at the knee joint, injury causes lameness
(pangulya). Size: 3 angula. The infrapatellar fossa is
within the Janu Marma zone. 🟡
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP9 (also knee-level, also Janu zone)

**5. Head zone:** L3–L4 cutaneous; L4–S1 articular. No
visceral correspondence — purely musculoskeletal. However,
the knee joint's rich articular innervation (Hilton's law:
nerves crossing a joint supply both the joint capsule and
the overlying skin) creates dense sensory convergence at
this site. 🔴

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL — the patellar ligament is a
core structure of the Superficial Front Line, connecting
the quadriceps through the patella to the tibial tuberosity
and continuing as the tibialis anterior fascia. ST35 sits
at the SFL's most prominent bony relay point.
→ Cross-ref: A09

**7. Neurogenic spot:** the infrapatellar fat pad contains
type IVa nociceptors that become sensitized in knee OA
(Dragoo 2012) — documented neurogenic inflammatory response
within the fat pad tissue. This is LOCAL tissue
sensitization, distinct from the dermatome-based neurogenic
spots at other points, but represents the same phenomenon
(neural sensitization → point sensitization). 🟡

**8. Cupping:** not applicable at the infrapatellar fossa
(bony, small area). 🔴

**Convergence score: 5/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Marma,
Anatomy Trains). The neurogenic fat pad sensitization (🟡)
is noted but not counted toward the convergence score as
it represents local tissue pathology rather than segmental
neurogenic inflammation per the Kim 2017 model. ST35 has
the HIGHEST cross-tradition convergence of any thigh-to-
knee ST point (ST31–ST34 all score 4/8), driven by the
knee joint's status as a major anatomical landmark across
all traditions.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Ling Shu, Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): knee pain
(膝痛), swelling and redness of the knee (膝红肿),
difficulty in bending and stretching (屈伸不利), numbness
around the knee (膝麻木), knee Bi syndrome (膝痹/膝风),
weak legs with difficulty walking (脚弱不能行).

Classical source note: Ling Shu Ch. 2 describes Dubi as
the location where "the Qi of the Stomach channel enters
the knee" — positioning it as the GATEWAY for Yangming Qi
to access the joint.

Nguồn hiện đại: knee osteoarthritis (primary), patellar
tendinopathy, infrapatellar fat pad syndrome, post-surgical
knee rehabilitation. ST35 is the most frequently included
point in knee acupuncture protocols — appearing in every
documented knee combination across the Atlas channels:
- **LR7 + GB34 + ST35 + ST36** — knee disorders (Zhen Jiu
  Zi Sheng Jing, 1220)
- **LR7 + EX-LE (Xiyan)** — knee redness, swelling, pain
  (Yu Long Ge, 1329)
- **SP9 + GB34 + ST35/EX-LE** — medial + lateral +
  anterior knee (comprehensive modern protocol)
- **ST35 + EX-LE4/5 (Xiyan pair)** — minimal local
  protocol for knee OA
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP9, AP-11 GB34, AP-12 LR7/LR8

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🟡 Plausible. ST35 has the strongest PROTOCOL evidence of
any point in Batch 7, though no study isolates ST35 alone:

Knee OA protocols including ST35:
- Berman 2004 (Annals of Internal Medicine): large RCT
  showed acupuncture superior to sham for knee OA.
  Protocol included ST35/Xiyan pair as core local points
  plus ST36, SP9, GB34. Significant improvements in
  WOMAC scores at 8 and 26 weeks.
- Witt 2005 (Lancet): RCT — acupuncture group included
  knee-local points (ST35 area) with improvements in
  WOMAC function and pain subscales.
- Multiple systematic reviews include knee OA trials
  where ST35/Xiyan pair is the most common local point
  selection.

Critical note: ST35's evidence is ALWAYS within multi-
point protocols — the "protocol inclusion without
isolation" gap identified at LI15 (AP-02). However, the
Xiyan pair (ST35 + EX-LE4/5 = just 2 local points) forms
a MINIMAL protocol tested in several Chinese RCTs,
providing closer-to-isolated evidence than typical multi-
point trials. Rating elevated to 🟡 (vs 🔴 for most non-
isolated points) because the 2-point Xiyan protocol
approximates single-point testing.
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI15 (protocol inclusion without
isolation)

### Dynamic acupoint properties

Knee points demonstrate clear dynamic behavior in knee OA:
- Increased pressure pain sensitivity at ST35 correlates
  with OA severity (clinical observation, multiple reports).
- Hoffa's fat pad sensitization: type IVa nociceptors in
  the infrapatellar fat pad upregulate substance P and CGRP
  in OA joints (Dragoo 2012). This represents LOCAL tissue
  sensitization — the fat pad becomes a pain generator,
  increasing the point's tenderness and potentially its
  therapeutic responsiveness.
- Whether ST35 meets formal C04 dynamic acupoint criteria
  (sensitization in the ABSENCE of local pathology) is
  debatable — OA CREATES local pathology at the needle
  site, blurring the line between "dynamic acupoint" and
  "tender pathological tissue."
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST35:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- Infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve (L3–L4) → dorsal
  horn of L3–L4 → segmental pain modulation at the knee.
- Articular branches from common peroneal and tibial nerves
  → rich joint afferent input. Hilton's law applies: nerves
  supplying the knee joint muscles also supply the joint
  capsule and overlying skin.
- Gate control mechanism: needling at ST35 activates Aβ and
  Aδ mechanoreceptors in the fat pad and peritendinous
  tissue, potentially closing the "gate" to C-fiber
  nociceptive input from the inflamed joint.
- Anti-inflammatory: local needling → axon reflex → CGRP/
  substance P release → local blood flow increase →
  clearing of inflammatory mediators from the joint space.
  The fat pad's rich innervation makes it an efficient
  target for axon-reflex-mediated modulation.

A03 (Fascia):
- Patellar retinaculum and fat pad — highly innervated
  fascial structures. Needle manipulation in the fat pad
  may release fascial adhesions and decompress sensitized
  nerve endings.

A07 (Segmental):
- L3–L4 segmental match for knee indications. FULL
  CONVERGENCE — ST35 is a purely LOCAL point with no
  distant organ claims. No supraspinal pathway needed.

**MINIMAL MECHANISM GAP:** ST35's indications are entirely
LOCAL. Segmental match is complete (L3–L4). No distant
organ effects to explain. This is the mechanistic OPPOSITE
of ST36 (which requires the complex PROKR2→vagal
supraspinal pathway to explain distant gastric effects from
L4–L5). Two adjacent ST points bracket the extremes of
acupuncture mechanism: local-segmental (ST35) vs distant-
supraspinal (ST36).

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 15–25 mm oblique, directed toward the center
of the joint space or toward the opposite knee eye
(EX-LE4/5). Knee must be FLEXED (~90°) to expose the
infrapatellar fossa — the depression disappears with knee
extension. De Qi: distension and heaviness within the knee
joint, sometimes with a sensation of warmth spreading
around the patella.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

Xiyan pair technique: bilateral needling with one needle
in ST35 (lateral) and one in EX-LE4/5 (medial), both
directed toward the center. Some practitioners connect the
two needles with EA — creating a current path through the
infrapatellar fat pad and anterior joint space.

EA: 2–15 Hz, low-medium intensity. ST35-to-EX-LE (trans-
articular) or ST35-to-ST36 (longitudinal channel). The
Xiyan pair EA is one of the most studied electrode
configurations in knee OA trials.

Bấm huyệt (B02): firm pressure in the lateral
infrapatellar fossa with the knee flexed. Self-applicable
for pain relief. The bony landmarks (patella edge, patellar
ligament, tibial plateau) provide clear tactile guidance.
→ Cross-ref: B02

Cứu (B08): indirect moxa for cold-pattern knee Bi. Direct
moxa on small cones (traditional technique for knee cold/
dampness). Classical sources specifically recommend moxa at
Dubi for cold-predominant knee conditions.

### An toàn

LOW-MODERATE risk. The primary concern is entry into the
joint space:

- **Joint capsule penetration:** oblique needling directed
  into the joint enters the synovial space through the fat
  pad. This is INTENTIONAL in some protocols (intra-
  articular acupuncture for knee OA) but requires strict
  asepsis — introducing infection into a synovial space
  causes septic arthritis, a surgical emergency.
- **Infrapatellar fat pad:** highly vascularized — mild
  post-needling bleeding/bruising is common. Usually
  self-limiting.
- **Infrapatellar branch of saphenous nerve:** the nerve
  crosses the anterior knee and may be contacted during
  needling, causing sharp electric pain. Redirect if this
  occurs.

No major vascular risk — genicular arteries are small.

Infection prevention is the PRIMARY safety concern — joint
space access elevates the consequence of poor sterile
technique from a minor soft tissue issue to potential
septic arthritis.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa
prohibition.

---

### Batch 7 Architectural Observations

> **Observation 22 — TRUNK-TO-LIMB TRANSITION: INGUINAL
> LIGAMENT CROSSING RESETS CONVERGENCE, TISSUE, AND RISK.**
>
> The ST30→ST31 transition marks the channel crossing the
> inguinal ligament from trunk to lower limb — the most
> dramatic regional change since the face→neck transition
> (ST8→ST9, Obs. 12). Three simultaneous resets occur:
>
> | Parameter | Trunk (ST19–ST30) | Limb (ST31–ST35) |
> |-----------|-------------------|-------------------|
> | Innervation | Intercostal T7–T12 + subcostal T12 + iliohypogastric L1 | Femoral L2–L4, lateral femoral cutaneous L2–L3 |
> | Tissue | Rectus abdominis → peritoneum | Quadriceps → femur (no cavity) |
> | Deep risk | PERITONEAL PUNCTURE | None (muscular only) |
> | Convergence | 5–7/8 | 4–5/8 |
> | Visceral | Gastric → intestinal → urogenital viscerotome | None (purely somatic) |
>
> The convergence DROP from trunk to limb is driven by the
> loss of three cross-tradition systems: Head zone (no
> visceral correspondence at L2–L4), neurogenic spots (no
> confirmed data for the thigh), and Marma (no thigh Marma
> at precise ST locations). This pattern is consistent with
> SP: abdominal SP (5–7/8) vs thigh SP (3–4/8) showed the
> same convergence decline at the trunk-to-limb boundary.
>
> The safety profile IMPROVES dramatically: from 12
> consecutive peritoneal-risk points (ST19–ST30, the
> longest continuous high-risk segment in the Atlas) to
> zero-risk muscular tissue. The femur and quadriceps mass
> eliminate all visceral access. ST31–ST34 represent the
> first SAFE zone on the ST channel since the face (ST1–
> ST8), after 22 consecutive safety-critical points
> spanning neck, chest, and abdomen (ST9–ST30).

> **Observation 23 — YANG XI-CLEFT PATTERN COMPLETE: 5/5
> CONFIRM CHANNEL-DOMAIN ACUTE EMERGENCY TARGETING.**
>
> ST34 completes the Yang Xi-Cleft inventory. With all 5
> Yang Xi-Cleft points documented (LI7, SI6, TE7, GB36,
> ST34), the functional pattern is formally confirmed:
>
> | Yang Xi-Cleft | Channel | Acute domain | Organ match? |
> |---------------|---------|--------------|--------------|
> | LI7 | Yangming (Hand) | Face/oral pain | ✗ (not LI organ) |
> | SI6 | Taiyang (Hand) | MSK + vision | ✗ (not SI organ) |
> | TE7 | Shaoyang (Hand) | Sudden deafness | ✗ (not TE visceral) |
> | GB36 | Shaoyang (Foot) | Lateral body pain | ✗ (not GB organ) |
> | **ST34** | **Yangming (Foot)** | **Acute gastric** | **✓** |
>
> ST34 is the ONLY Yang Xi-Cleft that targets its own
> organ's acute pathology. The other 4 target the channel's
> TRAJECTORY domain (face, shoulder/eye, ear, lateral body)
> rather than the organ's visceral function. ST34's
> exception is explained by the ST channel's unique anatomy:
> the longest Yang meridian (45 points, face to toe)
> physically traverses the gastric region at ST19–ST30,
> creating organ-trajectory overlap absent in other Yang
> channels' Xi-Cleft locations.
>
> Complete Yin-Yang contrast:
> - **Yin Xi-Cleft (5/5):** ALWAYS blood → ORGAN function
> - **Yang Xi-Cleft (4/5):** USUALLY channel-trajectory
>   pain → CHANNEL domain (exception: ST34 = organ match)
>
> The proposed autonomic mechanism (AP-02 Obs. 10): Yin
> channels follow arteries → vascular/blood effects; Yang
> channels follow nerves in inter-muscular septa →
> neural/inflammatory effects. This is now the most
> robustly documented polarity pattern in the Xi-Cleft
> category. Remaining question: do the 2 extraordinary
> vessel Xi-Cleft points (BL63 for Yang Qiao Mai, GB63
> for Yang Wei Mai) follow the Yang pattern?
> → Cross-ref: AP-02 LI7 (Obs. 10), AP-10 TE7, AP-11 GB36

> **Observation 24 — KNEE MULTI-CHANNEL ARCHITECTURE:
> DENSEST JOINT CONVERGENCE IN THE ATLAS.**
>
> ST35 (Dubi) completes the knee point inventory from the
> anterior approach. The knee now has documented points
> from 4 meridians plus extra points, creating the densest
> multi-channel convergence at any JOINT in the Atlas:
>
> | Point | Channel | Approach | Innervation | Role |
> |-------|---------|----------|-------------|------|
> | ST35 | Stomach | Ant-lateral | Saphenous L3–L4 | Primary local knee |
> | EX-LE4/5 | Extra | Ant-medial | Saphenous L3–L4 | Xiyan pair partner |
> | GB34 | Gallbladder | Lateral | CPN L4–S1 | Hui-Meeting Tendons |
> | SP9 | Spleen | Medial | Saphenous L3–L4 | He-Sea/Water |
> | LR7 | Liver | Post-medial | Saphenous L3–L4 | Knee gate |
> | LR8 | Liver | Post-medial | Saphenous L3–L4 | He-Sea/Water |
> | ST36 | Stomach | Ant-lateral (below) | Deep peroneal L4–L5 | Command Point leg |
>
> Three nerve systems converge: saphenous (4 points),
> common peroneal (1 point), and deep peroneal (1 point).
> The saphenous-to-peroneal transition occurs AT the knee
> (ST35 saphenous → ST36 deep peroneal) — making the knee
> a NEURAL JUNCTION as well as a skeletal joint.
>
> Cross-tradition knee protocol architecture:
> - **Medial + lateral:** SP9 + GB34 (most common pair)
> - **Anterior bilateral:** ST35 + EX-LE4/5 (Xiyan pair)
> - **Full ring:** SP9 + GB34 + ST35 + EX-LE (3 channels)
> - **Classical (1220):** LR7 + GB34 + ST35 + ST36
>
> The knee rivals the supraclavicular fossa (ST12, Obs. 12)
> and the inguinal region (Obs. 21) as a multi-channel
> convergence hub — but with a crucial difference: at the
> knee, channels converge on the SAME THERAPEUTIC TARGET
> (joint function) rather than merely crossing through an
> anatomical bottleneck. ST12 and the inguinal hub are
> transit convergences; the knee is a TARGET convergence.
>
> Mechanistic note: ST35 is the simplest mechanism in the
> Atlas (local-segmental, L3–L4, no distant claims), while
> its neighbor ST36 has the most complex (PROKR2→vagal,
> L4–L5, distant gastric effects). Two adjacent points
> bracket the full mechanistic spectrum.
> → Cross-ref: AP-04 SP9, AP-11 GB34, AP-12 LR7/LR8

---

## ST37 — Thượng Cự Hư (上巨虚, Shàng Jù Xū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Upper Great Void" (上 = upper, 巨 = great, 虚 = void/hollow)
— named for the pronounced depression palpable between the
tibial crest and the fibula on the anterolateral leg. "Upper"
distinguishes it from ST39 (下巨虚, "Lower Great Void"),
3 cun below. Located 6 cun below ST35 (Dubi), 3 cun below
ST36 (Zusanli), on the tibialis anterior muscle.

ST37 is the **Lower He-Sea (下合穴) of the Large Intestine**
— a point on the STOMACH channel that treats the LARGE
INTESTINE organ. Ling Shu Ch. 4 (本输篇): "大肠合入于巨虚
上廉" — the Large Intestine's He converges at the Upper
Juxu. The Lower He-Sea system solves an anatomical problem:
the LI channel's own He-Sea (LI11 at the elbow) is too
distant from the abdominal organs to serve as a Fu organ
Qi collection point. The ST leg channel, traversing the
anterior leg, provides a "proxy" access point.

Together with ST36 (Lower He-Sea of ST) and ST39 (Lower
He-Sea of SI), the ST channel hosts **3 of 6 Lower He-Sea
points** — the primary collection channel for Yang Fu organ
Qi at the leg.

ST37 is also a **Sea of Blood transport point** (十二经之海,
Ling Shu Ch. 33) — one of two ST points (with ST39)
designated as transport for the Sea of the Twelve Meridians.
→ Cross-ref: Obs. 11 (Four Seas)

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the anterolateral
leg, 6 cun below ST35 (Dubi), one finger-breadth (1 cun)
lateral to the anterior crest of the tibia.

Anatomical landmarks: on the tibialis anterior muscle, in
the same corridor as ST36 but 3 cun more distal. Between
ST36 (3 cun below ST35) and ST38 (8 cun below ST35). At
approximately the junction of the upper and middle thirds
of the leg.

Needle depth: 25–38 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Deep peroneal nerve (L4–L5)** — motor branches to
  tibialis anterior. The deep peroneal nerve runs the full
  length of the anterior compartment, accessible at ST36
  through ST39.
- **Lateral sural cutaneous nerve** — superficial sensation
  to the lateral leg.
- Spinal segment: L4–L5
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: anterior tibial artery and vein — the primary
neurovascular bundle of the anterior compartment, running
between tibialis anterior and extensor digitorum longus.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → crural fascia →
tibialis anterior muscle → interosseous membrane. Same
layered structure as ST36 — the entire ST36–ST39 corridor
shares the anterior compartment anatomy.

PROKR2 question: ST36's PROKR2+ neurons are concentrated
in deep fascia and periosteum (16.7% of lumbar DRG, Liu
2021). ST37, 3 cun below, is still within the same lumbar
DRG territory (L4–L5) and the same deep fascial compartment.
Whether PROKR2+ density is equivalent at ST37 or decreases
distally along the muscle belly is UNKNOWN — the Liu 2021
study did not map PROKR2 distribution at sub-dermatome
resolution.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Lower He-Sea (下合穴) of the Large Intestine.**

Ling Shu Ch. 4 (本输篇): "大肠合入于巨虚上廉" — "The
Large Intestine's He converges at the Upper Juxu." The
complete Lower He-Sea system:

| Lower He-Sea | For organ | On channel | Same channel? |
|---|---|---|---|
| ST36 | Stomach | ST | Yes — He-Sea = Lower He-Sea |
| **ST37** | **Large Intestine** | **ST** | **No — proxy** |
| ST39 | Small Intestine | ST | No — proxy |
| GB34 | Gallbladder | GB | Yes — He-Sea = Lower He-Sea |
| BL39 | Triple Burner | BL | No — proxy (debated) |
| BL40 | Bladder | BL | Yes — He-Sea = Lower He-Sea |

Pattern: Yang HAND channels (LI, SI, TE) require proxy
points on Yang LEG channels because their own He-Sea points
(LI11, SI8, TE10) are at the elbow — too distant from
abdominal organs. Yang LEG channels (ST, GB, BL) have
He-Sea already on the leg, so their own He-Sea serves dual
function. Collection channel hierarchy: ST (3) > BL (2) >
GB (1).
→ Cross-ref: AP-11 GB34 (Obs. 19)

**Sea of Blood transport point (十二经之海):** Ling Shu
Ch. 33 — ST37 and ST39 together serve as transport for the
Sea of Blood / Sea of the Twelve Meridians. Distinct from
the Lower He-Sea designation: Lower He-Sea = Fu organ Qi
access; Sea of Blood = systemic blood circulation. ST37
carries BOTH.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST37 Shangjuxu (上巨虚). Functions:
regulates Large Intestine (调大肠), clears damp-heat
(清湿热), resolves stagnation (理气导滞). Primary
indications: diarrhea (泄泻), dysentery (痢疾), abdominal
pain, borborygmus, appendicitis (阑尾炎), constipation.
Classical maxim: "大肠病取上巨虚" ("for LI disease, select
Shangjuxu"). As Lower He-Sea of LI, ST37 is the PRIMARY
lower limb access for LI organ disorders — the principle
"合治内府" (He-Sea treats internal Fu organs, Ling Shu
Ch. 4).

**2. Trigger point:** tibialis anterior mid-belly TrPs. At
6 cun below the knee, ST37 is in the mid-portion of the
tibialis anterior. Travell & Simons: TrP1 (more proximal =
ST36 level) and TrP2 (more distal). ST37 at intermediate
position. Referred pain: anterolateral leg, dorsum of foot.
No visceral referral — the colonic indication has NO TrP
correlate. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in anterior compartment
strain, shin splints (medial tibial stress syndrome), and
occasionally in chronic colonic dysfunction (referred
tenderness? — not formally tested). 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no specific Marma at the mid-leg
anterolateral surface. Indrabasti Marma (calf center) is
posterior (gastrocnemius), not anterior. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** L4–L5 dermatome — somatic (lateral leg).
Colon viscerotome = T10–L2. **Segmental mismatch**: ST37
does NOT correspond to the Large Intestine's autonomic
innervation. Same non-segmental gap as all Lower He-Sea
points — the effect on colonic function must be mediated
through supraspinal pathways. 🔴

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line (SFL) —
tibialis anterior, continuous from ST36 through ST37–ST39
to the dorsal foot.
→ Cross-ref: A09

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no confirmed data at this level for
colonic pathology. Kim 2017 neurogenic spots for GI disease
appear at T10–L1 dermatome (abdominal skin), not L4–L5
(leg). 🔴

**8. Cupping:** not typical for the anterolateral leg. 🔴

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Consistent with the tibialis anterior corridor
baseline. The colonic indication — ST37's defining function
— has NO cross-tradition support outside TCM. Same pattern
as TE6 for constipation: the most important classical
function is TCM-exclusive.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Ling Shu Ch. 4 (本输): Lower He-Sea of LI — "When disease
  is in the Large Intestine, select Shangjuxu (上巨虚)."
- Ling Shu Ch. 33 (四海): Sea of Blood transport point.
- Jiayi Jing: dysentery (下利), abdominal pain, borborygmus,
  poor appetite, knee and lower leg pain.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: diarrhea, dysentery (especially with blood
  and mucus 赤白痢), abdominal distension, appendicitis,
  lower limb weakness.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Diarrhea and dysentery (PRIMARY)** — Lower He-Sea
  function. ST37 is the primary lower limb point for acute
  diarrhea and dysentery (bacillary and amoebic).
- **Appendicitis (阑尾炎)** — modern empirical indication.
  The extra point EX-LE7 (Lanweixue 阑尾穴, "Appendix
  Point") is located approximately 2 cun below ST36 — about
  1 cun proximal to ST37. The overlap between EX-LE7 and
  ST37 reinforces the anterior compartment's association
  with colonic pathology.
- **Constipation** — colonic function regulation.
- **Lower limb pain, weakness** — local musculoskeletal via
  L4–L5 segmental.

Classical combinations:
- **ST37 + ST25 + ST36** — acute diarrhea/dysentery (Lower
  He-Sea + Front-Mu of LI + Command Point of Abdomen —
  three category access points for one organ).
- **ST37 + ST39** — Lower He-Sea pair for upper and lower
  intestinal disorders.
- **ST25 + SP14 + ST36 + ST37** — chronic constipation
  (Liu 2016 Ann Intern Med protocol, n=1,075).
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE6 constipation discussion

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for isolated ST37 effect.

Protocol inclusion: Liu et al. 2016 (Annals of Internal
Medicine) — largest acupuncture constipation RCT (n=1,075,
15 hospitals). EA at ST25/SP14/ST36/ST37 showed significant
improvement vs sham. ST37 was included in the protocol but
its individual contribution was NOT isolated. The study
validates the COMBINATION, not the individual point.
→ Cross-ref: AP-10 TE6

Cochrane (Cao et al. 2023): acupuncture for constipation —
ST25 "strongly recommended," ST37 not separately rated. The
evidence base attributes effects to multi-point protocols.

No study tests ST37 alone for colonic function. The Lower
He-Sea designation — ST37's defining role — has extensive
classical authority but zero controlled evidence for the
specific claim that needling ST37 independently modulates
Large Intestine function.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal sensitization data. The Lower He-Sea concept
implies dynamic activation: ST37 should become more
responsive (tender, altered tissue) when LI pathology is
present. Whether this follows C04 sensitization patterns
or represents a different functional state change is
untested.

Clinically, practitioners report increased tenderness along
the anterior compartment in patients with acute colonic
inflammation — but this could reflect general sympathetic-
mediated referred guarding rather than point-specific
sensitization.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST37:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- Deep peroneal nerve (L4–L5) → lumbar DRG → spinal cord →
  ascending tracts.
- Lower He-Sea colonic indication GAP: colon viscerotome =
  T10–L2, but ST37 = L4–L5. NO direct segmental connection.
  Supraspinal pathway required.
- Proposed pathway (paralleling ST36 and GB34): L4–L5
  somatic afferents → ascending spinal tract → brainstem
  NTS/DMVN → vagal efferents → colonic smooth muscle
  modulation. This is the UNIVERSAL LOWER HE-SEA MECHANISM
  proposed at GB34 (AP-11 Obs. 19): leg point → brainstem
  relay → vagal → specific Fu organ.
- If confirmed, ST37→colonic function would be the THIRD
  documented Lower He-Sea supraspinal pathway (after
  ST36→gastric and GB34→gallbladder), establishing the
  pattern as systematic rather than coincidental.
- PROKR2 question: whether ST37 shares ST36's PROKR2+
  neuron-mediated vagal activation is UNKNOWN. Same deep
  fascial compartment, same lumbar DRG territory, but
  PROKR2 distribution at sub-dermatome resolution unmapped.

A03 (Fascia):
- Anterior compartment fascia. Same intermuscular plane as
  ST36. Needle rotation → connective tissue winding →
  mechanotransduction.

A07 (Segmental):
- LOCAL (leg/knee) indications: L4–L5 matches. CONVERGENCE.
- DISTANT (colonic) indications: T10–L2 target, L4–L5
  source. MISMATCH. Supraspinal pathway required.

A08 (Muscle):
- Tibialis anterior mid-belly. Local twitch response
  obtainable.

**ST37 vs ST36 — why does 3 cun shift organ specificity?**

Both ST36 (L4–L5) and ST37 (L4–L5) are within the same
spinal segment, same nerve, same muscle, same fascial
compartment. The classical tradition assigns them to
DIFFERENT organs (Stomach vs Large Intestine). From a
modern neuroanatomical perspective, there is no identified
mechanism that would allow 3 cun (~6 cm) along the same
nerve to selectively activate different vagal targets.
Three possible explanations:

1. **Motor endplate gradient:** PROKR2+ neurons concentrate
   at the motor endplate zone (proximal, ~ST36). More
   distal points may activate different afferent populations
   with different central projections.
2. **No real specificity:** both points activate a general
   vagal-intestinal pathway; the organ distinction is a
   conceptual framework rather than physiological reality.
3. **Dose-response difference:** proximal (ST36, stronger
   motor endplate) vs distal (ST37, more fascial) stimulation
   produces different input profiles → different autonomic
   output via the same general pathway.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 25–38 mm perpendicular. Same tissue corridor
as ST36 — tibialis anterior belly, deep peroneal nerve. De
Qi: distension and heaviness in the anterolateral leg.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

EA: commonly used for colonic indications. Bilateral ST37
EA for acute diarrhea/dysentery. ST25-to-ST37 electrode
configuration used in constipation protocols (Liu 2016).

Bấm huyệt (B02): self-applicable along the tibialis
anterior. Palpation landmark: 1 cun lateral to tibial
crest, 6 cun below ST35 (half the distance from knee to
ankle). Firm sustained pressure for colonic symptoms.

Cứu (B08): traditionally used for cold-pattern diarrhea
(cold invading LI). No moxa prohibition.

### An toàn

LOW risk — deep muscular tissue throughout. Same safety
profile as ST36 and the entire tibialis anterior corridor.
No visceral or major vascular access at standard depth.

Anterior tibial artery runs deep between tibialis anterior
and extensor digitorum longus. Standard perpendicular
needling does not reach it.

In patients with anterior compartment syndrome, avoid deep
needling or EA.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa
prohibition.

---

## ST38 — Tiều Khẩu (条口, Tiáo Kǒu)

**Evidence tier: C (Minimum Viable Entry)**
"Strip Mouth" (条 = strip/line, 口 = mouth/opening) —
named for the narrow cleft between the tibialis anterior
and extensor digitorum longus tendons, which becomes
palpable at this level as the muscles transition from belly
to tendon. Located 8 cun below ST35 (Dubi), 2 cun below
ST37 and 1 cun above ST39, on the anterolateral leg.

ST38 has no special category designations. Its primary
clinical interest lies in the **contralateral shoulder
technique** (条口透承山, "Tiaokou through to Chengshan")
— an empirical treatment for frozen shoulder (adhesive
capsulitis) where ST38 on the OPPOSITE leg is needled with
deep insertion directed toward BL57 (Chengshan), while the
patient actively moves the affected shoulder. This is the
most widely cited contralateral distal technique in TCM.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the anterolateral
leg, 8 cun below ST35 (Dubi), one finger-breadth (1 cun)
lateral to the anterior crest of the tibia.

Anatomical landmarks: on the tibialis anterior, between
ST37 (6 cun below ST35) and ST39 (9 cun below ST35). At
approximately the junction of the middle and lower thirds
of the leg, where tibialis anterior transitions from muscle
belly to tendon.

Needle depth: 25–35 mm perpendicular; up to 50–60 mm for
through-needling toward BL57 (traverses the anterior
compartment → interosseous membrane → deep posterior
compartment).

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Deep peroneal nerve (L4–L5)** — motor branches to
  tibialis anterior (distal).
- **Lateral sural cutaneous nerve** — cutaneous supply.
- Spinal segment: L4–L5
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Through-needling toward BL57: the deep insertion crosses
the interosseous membrane and enters the deep posterior
compartment, potentially engaging the **tibial nerve
(L4–S3)**. This transcompartmental approach recruits BOTH
deep peroneal (anterior) and tibial (posterior) nerve
trunks — broader neural access than standard perpendicular
needling at any single-compartment point.

Vascular: anterior tibial artery and vein deep to the
point. Through-needling risks encountering the posterior
tibial vessels in the deep posterior compartment.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → crural fascia →
tibialis anterior (muscle-tendon transition) → extensor
digitorum longus → interosseous membrane → (deep posterior
compartment if through-needling).

### Phân loại huyệt

No special category designations.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM:** ST38 Tiaokou (条口). Functions: relaxes sinews
and benefits the shoulder (舒筋利肩), harmonizes the
stomach (和胃). Primary indications: shoulder pain and
inability to raise the arm (肩痛不举), leg pain and
weakness, gastric pain. The shoulder indication is the
DEFINING function — ST38 is the most commonly cited distal
point for frozen shoulder in TCM education.

**2. Trigger point:** tibialis anterior distal TrPs. At
this level, muscle transitions to tendon. Referred pain:
dorsum of foot and great toe. NO shoulder referral pattern.
🟡

**3. Ashi:** tender in anterior compartment overuse, shin
splints. 🟡

**4. Marma:** no specific Marma. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** L4–L5 dermatome — somatic. Shoulder =
C5–C6. NO correspondence. 🔴

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL — tibialis anterior segment.
The SFL does NOT connect directly to the shoulder. However,
the **Spiral Line (SPL)** traces: tibialis anterior → IT
band → external oblique → CONTRALATERAL internal oblique →
serratus anterior → rhomboid → rotator cuff. This spiral
connection is the most plausible fascial mechanism for
the contralateral shoulder effect and would explain the
CONTRALATERAL (not ipsilateral) specificity. Untested.
→ Cross-ref: A09

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no confirmed data. 🔴

**8. Cupping:** not typical. 🔴

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). The contralateral shoulder indication — ST38's
headline function — has NO cross-tradition support. Like
TE6 for constipation, it is TCM-exclusive.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ (Jiayi Jing, Dacheng): knee and lower leg pain
(膝胫痛), lower limb coldness and numbness (足下热胫寒),
inability to stand for long, shoulder inability to raise
(肩不举).

Nguồn hiện đại:
- **Frozen shoulder (PRIMARY empirical):** the contralateral
  technique — needle ST38 on the OPPOSITE side from the
  affected shoulder, apply strong stimulation (lift-thrust,
  rotation) or through-needle toward BL57, while the patient
  actively moves the painful shoulder. One of the most widely
  taught single-point techniques in clinical TCM training.
- **Leg pain, shin splints** — local musculoskeletal via
  L4–L5 segmental.
- **Gastric pain** — less emphasized, inherited from channel
  association.

The shoulder indication appears in classical sources but
gained prominence through modern clinical practice and TCM
textbook propagation. It is the most cited example of
"contralateral distal needling" (对侧远端取穴) in the field.

Shoulder protocol contrast (all documented shoulder points):
- LI15: anterolateral, C5–C6 segmental (LOCAL). 🟡
- SI9–SI11: posterior, C5–C6 (LOCAL). Protocol inclusion.
- TE14: posterolateral, C5–T1 (LOCAL). 🟡
- GB21: superior, C3–C4 + CN XI (LOCAL). 🟡
- **ST38: anterolateral LEG, L4–L5 (NON-LOCAL).** 🔴
→ Cross-ref: AP-02 LI15, AP-06 SI9–SI11, AP-10 TE14,
AP-11 GB21

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No sham-controlled RCT isolates ST38 for
frozen shoulder or any other indication.

Several uncontrolled Chinese case series report high
efficacy for the contralateral shoulder technique, but
methodological quality is uniformly poor (no sham, no
blinding, short follow-up). The technique is clinically
popular but evidence-free.

Contrast: LI15, TE14, SI9–SI11, GB21 all have at least
protocol-level inclusion in frozen shoulder systematic
reviews. ST38 appears in NONE of the major English-language
SRs — its use is almost entirely within Chinese-language
clinical practice.

### An toàn

LOW risk for standard perpendicular needling — muscular
tissue only. Same safety profile as the tibialis anterior
corridor.

**Through-needling caution (条口透承山):** deep insertion
(50–60 mm) toward BL57 crosses the interosseous membrane
into the deep posterior compartment. Risks: posterior tibial
artery/vein injury, tibial nerve damage. Requires precise
angular control and sufficient clinical experience. NOT
recommended for beginners.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa
prohibition.

---

## ST39 — Hạ Cự Hư (下巨虚, Xià Jù Xū)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Lower Great Void" (下 = lower, 巨 = great, 虚 = void/
hollow) — paired name with ST37 (上巨虚, "Upper Great
Void"), referring to the same tibial-fibular depression
but 3 cun more distal. Located 9 cun below ST35 (Dubi),
3 cun below ST37, 1 cun below ST38, on the anterolateral
leg approaching the ankle.

ST39 is the **Lower He-Sea (下合穴) of the Small Intestine**
— a point on the STOMACH channel that treats SMALL INTESTINE
organ disorders. Ling Shu Ch. 4: "小肠合入于巨虚下廉" —
"The Small Intestine's He converges at the Lower Juxu."
Together with ST36 (ST) and ST37 (LI), this completes the
**Lower He-Sea trio** on the ST channel.

ST39 is also a **Sea of Blood transport point** (十二经之海,
Ling Shu Ch. 33), paired with ST37.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the anterolateral
leg, 9 cun below ST35 (Dubi), one finger-breadth (1 cun)
lateral to the anterior crest of the tibia.

Anatomical landmarks: on the tibialis anterior, between
ST38 (8 cun below ST35) and ST40 (8 cun above lateral
malleolus). At approximately the lower third of the leg,
where tibialis anterior transitions from muscle belly to
tendon.

Needle depth: 25–35 mm perpendicular.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Deep peroneal nerve (L4–L5)** — distal motor branches.
  Thinner than at ST36–ST37 level as the nerve continues
  distally toward the ankle.
- **Lateral sural cutaneous nerve** — cutaneous supply.
- Spinal segment: L4–L5
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: anterior tibial artery and vein — same
neurovascular bundle as the entire corridor.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → crural fascia →
tibialis anterior (tendon transition) → extensor digitorum
longus → interosseous membrane. At 9 cun below the knee,
the tibialis anterior is predominantly tendinous — less
contractile tissue than at ST36–ST37.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Lower He-Sea (下合穴) of the Small Intestine.**

Ling Shu Ch. 4 (本输篇): "小肠合入于巨虚下廉" — "The
Small Intestine's He converges at the Lower Juxu."

This completes the Lower He-Sea trio on the ST channel:
- ST36: Lower He-Sea of Stomach (own organ)
- ST37: Lower He-Sea of Large Intestine (Yangming proxy)
- **ST39: Lower He-Sea of Small Intestine (Taiyang proxy)**

The cross-pair assignment is notable: SI belongs to the
Taiyang (太阳) pair, not Yangming (阳明). Its Lower He-Sea
is hosted on the Yangming leg channel (ST), not its own
Taiyang leg channel (BL). By contrast, TE (Shaoyang hand)
has its Lower He-Sea on BL39 (Taiyang foot), not GB
(Shaoyang foot). The assignment pattern is NOT simply
"same-name hand → same-name foot." Only LI → ST follows
that rule.

The SI channel's own He-Sea (SI8 at the elbow) addresses
primarily musculoskeletal conditions (elbow pain, epilepsy)
with virtually NO intestinal indications. The Lower He-Sea
system compensates: ST39 provides the intestinal treatment
function that SI8 cannot deliver.
→ Cross-ref: AP-06 SI8, AP-11 GB34 (Obs. 19)

**Sea of Blood transport point (十二经之海):** Ling Shu
Ch. 33, paired with ST37.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST39 Xiajuxu (下巨虚). Functions:
regulates Small Intestine (调小肠), clears damp-heat
(清湿热), separates clear from turbid (分清泌浊). Primary
indications: lower abdominal pain (少腹痛), diarrhea,
dysentery, small intestine Qi pain (小肠气痛). The
"separates clear from turbid" function maps the SI's
classical role: receiving from the stomach, absorbing
nutrients ("clear"), and passing waste ("turbid") to
the Large Intestine.

**2. Trigger point:** tibialis anterior distal TrPs /
extensor digitorum longus TrPs. At 9 cun below the knee,
the muscle is primarily tendinous — TrP correspondence is
weaker than at the proximal belly (ST36–ST37). Referred
pain: dorsum of foot. No abdominal referral. 🟡

**3. Ashi:** tender in anterior compartment strain, tibialis
anterior tendinopathy. Less frequently tender than the
proximal belly. 🟡

**4. Marma:** no specific Marma at this level. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** L4–L5 dermatome — somatic. Small intestine
viscerotome = T9–T10. Same segmental mismatch as all Lower
He-Sea points. 🔴

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL — distal tibialis anterior
segment, approaching the tendon. Continuous with ST36–ST38
above and ST41 (ankle) below.
→ Cross-ref: A09

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no confirmed data. 🔴

**8. Cupping:** not typical. 🔴

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy
Trains). Identical to ST37 and ST38. The entire tibialis
anterior corridor maintains 4/8 baseline — consistent
with limb points distant from anatomical landmarks.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Ling Shu Ch. 4 (本输): Lower He-Sea of SI — "When disease
  is in the Small Intestine, select Xiajuxu (下巨虚)."
- Ling Shu Ch. 33 (四海): Sea of Blood transport point.
- Jiayi Jing: lower abdominal pain (少腹痛), diarrhea
  (泄泻), dysentery, testicular pain (睾丸痛), lumbago.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: small intestine Qi pain (小肠气痛), lower
  abdominal distension, diarrhea, lower limb weakness.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Lower abdominal pain (PRIMARY)** — SI territory. ST39
  addresses the lower abdomen (SI region) while ST37
  addresses upper/lower abdomen (LI region). The geographical
  specificity parallels intestinal anatomy: SI (proximal GI
  tract) → lower abdominal pain; LI (distal GI tract) →
  diarrhea, dysentery.
- **Diarrhea, dysentery** — overlapping with ST37 but with
  SI-type emphasis (watery, undigested food vs mucous/bloody).
- **Breast disorders** — classical SI-breast connection (SI
  channel traverses the breast region). Compare SI1 lactation.
- **Lower limb pain** — local musculoskeletal.

Classical combinations:
- **ST39 + ST37** — Lower He-Sea pair for combined intestinal
  treatment (upper + lower GI).
- **ST39 + CV4 + ST25** — lower abdominal pain (Lower He-Sea
  + Ren Mai + Front-Mu of LI: multi-level access).
- **ST39 + SI4** — SI channel + SI Lower He-Sea: arm-leg
  pairing for SI organ disorders.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No study isolates ST39 for any indication.

The SI channel is the LEAST STUDIED regular meridian for
organ-specific effects. SI8 (He-Sea) has no intestinal
evidence; SI4 has no intestinal evidence; and ST39 (Lower
He-Sea of SI) has no intestinal evidence. The entire claim
that needling ST39 modulates Small Intestine function rests
entirely on classical authority.
→ Cross-ref: AP-06

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal data. Same framework as ST37: the Lower He-Sea
concept implies disease-state activation, but untested at
ST39 specifically. The distal position (tendon transition)
may reduce dynamic sensitivity relative to the proximal
motor endplate zone (ST36–ST37).

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST39:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- Deep peroneal nerve (L4–L5) → lumbar DRG → spinal cord →
  ascending tracts. Same primary pathway as ST36–ST38.
- Lower He-Sea SI indication GAP: small intestine
  viscerotome = T9–T10, but ST39 = L4–L5. NO direct
  segmental connection. Supraspinal pathway required.
- Same proposed mechanism as ST37 and GB34: L4–L5 afferents
  → brainstem NTS/DMVN → vagal efferents → small intestine
  smooth muscle modulation.

A03 (Fascia):
- Anterior compartment fascia at the tendon transition zone.
  Less contractile tissue than at ST36–ST37 — potentially
  less mechanotransduction via needle rotation.

A07 (Segmental):
- LOCAL (leg) indications: L4–L5 matches. CONVERGENCE.
- DISTANT (SI) indications: T9–T10 target, L4–L5 source.
  MISMATCH.

**ST37 vs ST39 — 3 cun shifts organ from LI to SI:**

Both ST37 (L4–L5) and ST39 (L4–L5) are within the same
spinal segment, same nerve, same muscle. The classical
tradition assigns them to DIFFERENT organs (Large Intestine
vs Small Intestine). No identified mechanism allows ~6 cm
of linear distance along the same nerve to activate
different vagal targets selectively. The three hypotheses
proposed at ST37 apply equally here: (1) micro-anatomical
afferent differences, (2) no real specificity, or (3)
dose-response gradient from proximal muscle to distal
tendon.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 25–35 mm perpendicular. More tendinous tissue
than at ST36–ST37. De Qi may be less prominent due to
reduced muscle mass. Needle grasp less frequent at this
level.

EA: bilateral ST39 EA for lower abdominal pain. ST37–ST39
electrode pairing covers both LI and SI Lower He-Sea
simultaneously — a common intestinal disorder configuration.

Bấm huyệt (B02): self-applicable. Same palpation landmark
— 1 cun lateral to tibial crest, 9 cun below the knee.

Cứu (B08): applicable. Traditionally used for cold-type
diarrhea and abdominal cold. No moxa prohibition.

### An toàn

LOW risk — muscular/tendinous tissue only. Same safety
profile as the tibialis anterior corridor. No visceral or
major vascular access.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa
prohibition.

---

### Batch 8 Architectural Observations

> **Observation 25 — LOWER HE-SEA TRIO COMPLETE: ST
> CHANNEL AS PRIMARY FU ORGAN COLLECTION CHANNEL.**
>
> ST36 (Stomach) + ST37 (Large Intestine) + ST39 (Small
> Intestine) = 3 of 6 Lower He-Sea points hosted on the
> ST channel. The Ling Shu Ch. 4 principle "六腑皆出足三阳"
> (all six Fu organs emerge at the three Yang leg channels)
> assigns the ST channel as PRIMARY collector:
>
> | Lower He-Sea | Organ | Channel | Mechanism |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | ST36 | Stomach | ST | PROKR2→vagal (CONFIRMED) |
> | ST37 | Large Intestine | ST | Proposed vagal (UNTESTED) |
> | ST39 | Small Intestine | ST | Proposed vagal (UNTESTED) |
> | GB34 | Gallbladder | GB | Proposed vagal (observed) |
> | BL39 | Triple Burner | BL | Unknown |
> | BL40 | Bladder | BL | Unknown |
>
> All 6 share the same architectural problem: leg points
> (L4–S2) treating abdominal organs (T6–L2). Only ST36 has
> a confirmed molecular mechanism. The proposed universal
> Lower He-Sea mechanism — leg afferent → brainstem relay →
> vagal efferent → specific Fu organ — requires confirmation
> at 5 of 6 points.
>
> The ST channel's 3-point concentration follows Yangming's
> role as the "most Qi and Blood" (多气多血) channel — the
> highest-volume Yang channel providing the broadest Fu
> organ access at the leg.
> → Cross-ref: AP-11 GB34 (Obs. 19), ST36 entry
>
> **Observation 26 — TIBIALIS ANTERIOR CORRIDOR: 4 POINTS,
> ONE MUSCLE, 3 DIFFERENT ORGAN TARGETS.**
>
> ST36–ST37–ST38–ST39 span ~6 cun (~12 cm) along the
> tibialis anterior, all sharing the deep peroneal nerve
> (L4–L5), the same fascia, and the same SFL segment. Yet
> the classical tradition assigns three different organ
> targets: Stomach (ST36), Large Intestine (ST37), Small
> Intestine (ST39). ST38 claims contralateral shoulder
> effects with no organ designation.
>
> This is the longest same-muscle acupoint series in the
> Atlas. The question of how linear distance along a single
> muscle produces organ-specific effects is the central
> mechanism question for the Lower He-Sea system.
>
> Compare with the QUADRICEPS CORRIDOR (ST31–ST34, Obs. 22):
> 4 points on one muscle group with UNIFORM indications
> (knee/thigh pain). The tibialis anterior corridor is
> architecturally distinct because the tradition assigns
> HETEROGENEOUS organ targets to a neurally HOMOGENEOUS
> region. The quadriceps corridor demonstrates that same-
> nerve proximity → same function (expected). The tibialis
> anterior corridor challenges this assumption.
>
> Gradient hypothesis: PROKR2+ neurons concentrate at the
> motor endplate zone (proximal, ~ST36 level). As points
> move distally from endplate (ST37, ST38, ST39), the
> tissue shifts from contractile muscle to tendon, the
> available afferent fiber population may change, and the
> central projection pattern may differ. Untested.
>
> **Observation 27 — CONTRALATERAL SHOULDER TECHNIQUE:
> LARGEST CLAIM-MECHANISM GAP FOR A SPECIFIC TECHNIQUE.**
>
