> ST38 for frozen shoulder (条口透承山) is the most widely
> cited contralateral distal technique in TCM. All documented
> shoulder points in the Atlas operate through LOCAL
> mechanisms:
>
> | Point | Position | Nerve | Mechanism |
> |-------|----------|-------|-----------|
> | LI15 | Anterolateral shoulder | C5–C6 | Local segmental |
> | SI9–SI11 | Posterior shoulder | C5–C6 | TrP / segmental |
> | TE14 | Posterolateral shoulder | C5–T1 | TrP / segmental |
> | GB21 | Superior shoulder | C3–C4, CN XI | TrP / segmental |
> | **ST38** | **Anterolateral LEG** | **L4–L5** | **Unknown** |
>
> ST38 has NO segmental match (C5–C6 shoulder vs L4–L5 leg),
> NO local shoulder anatomy, NO TrP referral to the shoulder,
> NO Head zone correspondence. The through-needling technique
> (条口透承山) engages both deep peroneal and tibial nerves
> via transcompartmental insertion — broader neural access —
> but neither nerve has any known projection to the shoulder
> girdle motor or sensory cortex.
>
> The ONLY proposed pathway is the Spiral Line (Myers):
> tibialis anterior → IT band → external oblique →
> contralateral internal oblique → serratus anterior →
> rhomboid → rotator cuff. This spiral fascial connection
> would explain the contralateral (not ipsilateral)
> specificity. But it is entirely theoretical — no study has
> tested whether mechanical stimulation at the tibialis
> anterior produces measurable tension change at the
> contralateral rotator cuff.
>
> Compare: SI3 contralateral technique for stiff neck
> (AP-06) has a narrower gap (C8 → cervical, within the
> spinal cord's reach). ST38's gap spans C5-C6 ↔ L4-L5 —
> the widest segmental distance for any contralateral
> technique in the Atlas.
> → Cross-ref: AP-02 LI15, AP-06 SI3/SI9–SI11, AP-10 TE14,
> AP-11 GB21

---

## ST40 — Phong Long (丰隆, Fēng Lóng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Abundant Bulge" (丰 = abundant/prosperous, 隆 = bulging/
prominent) — named for the prominently bulging musculature
at this level of the leg, where the tibialis anterior and
peroneus longus are at their fullest. Located 8 cun above
the tip of the lateral malleolus, approximately at the
midpoint of the leg. Lateral to ST38 (Tiaokou) by ~1 cun.

ST40 is the **Luo-Connecting point (络穴)** of the Stomach
channel — the 10th Luo documented in the Atlas (after LU7,
LI6, SP4, HT5, SI7, PC6, TE5, LR5, GB37). The Luo vessel
branches from ST40 and connects to the Spleen channel,
establishing the Interior-Exterior coupling between the
Earth Yin-Yang pair.

Host-Guest (主客) Yuan-Luo pairing:
- **SP as host:** SP3 (Yuan) + ST40 (Luo of ST) = treat
  Spleen via SP's own Yuan + ST's Luo
- **ST as host:** ST42 (Yuan) + SP4 (Luo of SP) = treat
  Stomach via ST's own Yuan + SP's Luo
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP3 (line 816), SP4 (line 1153)

ST40 is TCM's **primary phlegm-resolving point** (化痰要穴).
Zhu Danxi (朱丹溪, Yuan dynasty): "治痰第一要穴" — "the
foremost essential point for treating phlegm." Phlegm (痰)
in TCM encompasses both visible phlegm (sputum, mucus) and
invisible phlegm (无形之痰) — a broad pathological concept
covering metabolic accumulation, dizziness, mental
cloudiness, and tissue stagnation.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the anterolateral
aspect of the leg, 8 cun above the tip of the lateral
malleolus, two finger-breadths (middle finger) lateral to
the anterior crest of the tibia. Lateral to ST38 by ~1 cun.

Anatomical landmarks: at the junction of tibialis anterior
and peroneus longus muscles, where the lateral compartment
musculature is most prominent. At approximately the midpoint
of the leg (tibial tuberosity to lateral malleolus = 16 cun).

Needle depth: 25–38 mm perpendicular or slightly oblique.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Deep peroneal nerve (L4–L5)** — motor branches to
  tibialis anterior (medial access).
- **Superficial peroneal nerve (L4–S1)** — emerges between
  peroneus longus and EDL at approximately this level. ST40's
  more lateral position brings it closer to the superficial
  peroneal territory than ST36–ST39.
- **Lateral sural cutaneous nerve** — cutaneous supply.
- Spinal segment: L4–L5 (deep), L4–S1 (superficial)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

ST40's lateral position places it at the TRANSITION between
the anterior compartment (deep peroneal nerve) and the
lateral compartment (superficial peroneal nerve). This dual-
compartment access is unique among the lower leg ST points.

Vascular: anterior tibial artery and vein (deep). No major
vascular risk.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → crural fascia →
junction of tibialis anterior (medially) and peroneus longus
(laterally). Deep needle reaches the interosseous membrane.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Luo-Connecting: YES** — 络穴 of the ST channel. 10th Luo
documented (LU7, LI6, SP4, HT5, SI7, PC6, TE5, LR5, GB37,
ST40). 5th Yang Luo (after LI6, SI7, TE5, GB37).

Luo vessel pathology (Ling Shu Ch. 10, 经脉):
- **Excess (实):** throat obstruction (喉痹), sudden loss of
  voice (暴喑)
- **Deficiency (虚):** lower limb atrophy (足不收), leg
  weakness (胫枯)

The excess pathology (throat/voice) is notable: L4–L5
affecting the throat is ANOTHER non-segmental claim on the
ST leg — joining the Lower He-Sea colonic (ST37, T10–L2),
gastric (ST36, T6–T9), and small intestinal (ST39, T9–T12)
gaps. The throat is vagally innervated (CN X), suggesting
the same L4–L5 → brainstem → vagal mechanism proposed for
the Lower He-Sea system may apply to Luo pathology.

The deficiency pathology (leg weakness) IS segmentally
appropriate (L4–L5 → local musculature). Pattern across Luo
points: local/deficiency indications match segmentally;
distant/excess indications require supraspinal pathways.

Luo functional spectrum now documented (10/12):
- Channel-local: LI6, SI7
- Extraordinary vessel: LU7 (Ren Mai), SP4 (Chong Mai),
  PC6 (Yin Wei Mai), TE5 (Yang Wei Mai)
- Sensory specialty: HT5 (speech), GB37 (vision)
- **Systemic: ST40 (phlegm)** — broadest scope of any Luo

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST40 Fenglong (丰隆). Functions:
transforms phlegm and dampness (化痰祛湿), harmonizes
Stomach and Spleen (和胃健脾), calms the spirit (安神).

Phlegm indications span multiple organ systems:
- Visible: productive cough, copious sputum, wheezing
- Digestive: nausea, vomiting, epigastric distension
- "Invisible" (无形之痰): dizziness, vertigo, mental
  cloudiness, epilepsy (癫痫), mania (狂证)
- Modern extensions: obesity, hyperlipidemia

Classical maxim: "治痰第一要穴" (Dan Xi Xin Fa). Same
prescriptive authority class as ST36 for gastric, LI4 for
face/mouth, SP6 for gynecological conditions.

**2. Trigger point:** TrPs at the tibialis anterior–peroneus
longus junction. Referred pain: anterolateral leg, lateral
ankle. NO visceral or respiratory referral — the phlegm
indication has NO TrP correlate. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in lateral compartment
strain, peroneal tendinopathy, general leg fatigue after
prolonged standing. Local musculoskeletal only. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no specific Marma at the mid-lateral
leg. Indrabasti Marma (calf center) is posterior, not
anterolateral. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** L4–L5 dermatome — somatic. No Head zone
for respiratory or metabolic pathology at this level. 🔴

**6. Anatomy Trains:** dual-line access:
- **Superficial Front Line (SFL):** tibialis anterior,
  continuous from ST36–ST39 to the dorsal foot.
- **Lateral Line (LL):** peroneus longus, from lateral
  malleolus up through the IT tract to the shoulder.
ST40 accesses BOTH lines — the only ST point with documented
dual Anatomy Trains access.
→ Cross-ref: A09

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data at L4–L5 for respiratory or
metabolic pathology. 🔴

**8. Cupping:** not typical for the lateral leg. 🔴

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy Trains).
The phlegm indication — ST40's defining function — has NO
cross-tradition support. Continues the pattern: the most
important classical function is often TCM-exclusive (ST37
colonic, ST38 shoulder, ST40 phlegm).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Ling Shu Ch. 10 (经脉): Luo pathology — excess: throat
  obstruction, voice loss; deficiency: leg atrophy.
- Dan Xi Xin Fa (朱丹溪): "治痰第一要穴."
- Jiayi Jing: cough with copious sputum, chest oppression,
  epilepsy, leg pain and weakness.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: transforms phlegm, calms spirit, clears
  Stomach heat. Epilepsy, mania, productive cough, wheezing,
  dizziness, facial edema.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Phlegm conditions (PRIMARY):** productive cough,
  bronchitis, asthma with phlegm. ST40 is the universal
  first-choice point in ANY phlegm protocol.
- **Mental/neurological:** epilepsy, mania, schizophrenia
  (phlegm misting the orifices 痰迷心窍). Combined with
  PC5, HT7.
- **Dizziness and vertigo:** phlegm-dampness obstructing
  clear Yang.
- **Metabolic (modern):** obesity, hyperlipidemia — modern
  extensions of "invisible phlegm."
- **Local:** lower leg pain, weakness.

Classical combinations:
- **ST40 + SP3** — Host-Guest for Spleen deficiency with
  phlegm (Yuan-Luo coupling).
- **ST40 + CV12 + SP9** — phlegm-dampness in the middle
  burner (standard three-point phlegm protocol).
- **ST40 + PC5 + HT7** — epilepsy/mania with phlegm.
- **ST40 + LU5** — productive cough with copious sputum.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for isolated ST40 effect on phlegm/metabolic
outcomes.

Animal studies: some rodent models show acupuncture at ST40
area affects lipid metabolism (cholesterol, triglycerides)
and inflammatory markers. Preliminary, typically without sham
controls, cannot establish point specificity.

Clinical: several Chinese-language trials report ST40-based
protocols for hyperlipidemia and obesity. Low methodological
quality (inadequate blinding, no sham, small samples). ST40
appears in multi-point protocols, not isolated.

ST40+KI1 EA (10 Hz) reduced seizure frequency in an animal
epilepsy model — combination, not ST40 alone.
→ Cross-ref: AP-08 KI1

The core challenge: "phlegm" encompasses conditions that
modern medicine classifies as unrelated (respiratory mucus,
metabolic syndrome, epilepsy). No single mechanism explains
ALL phlegm indications. Most parsimonious explanation:
"phlegm" is a clinical CATEGORY organizing diverse
pathologies by shared quality (turbidity, obstruction,
accumulation), not a single physiological entity.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal sensitization data. Practitioners report increased
tenderness and a "boggy" subcutaneous quality at ST40 in
patients with phlegm-dampness constitution (痰湿体质) —
consistent with subcutaneous tissue changes in metabolic
conditions, but not formally quantified.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST40:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- Deep peroneal nerve (L4–L5) + superficial peroneal nerve
  (L4–S1) → dual compartment input → lumbar DRG → ascending.
- **Phlegm indication GAP:** phlegm affects lung (T3–T5),
  stomach (T6–T9), brain (CN X). L4–L5 has NO segmental
  connection. Supraspinal pathway required — same L4–L5 →
  brainstem NTS/DMVN → vagal mechanism proposed for the
  Lower He-Sea system.
- **Luo excess (throat):** throat = CN X + CN IX.
  L4–L5 → throat = same supraspinal pathway. Mechanistically
  consistent with ST36's PROKR2→vagal axis.

A03 (Fascia):
- Anterior-lateral compartment junction — dual fascial plane
  access at the intermuscular septum. Needle rotation →
  connective tissue winding through two compartments.

A07 (Segmental):
- LOCAL (leg): L4–L5 matches. CONVERGENCE.
- DISTANT (phlegm, throat): multiple organ targets. MISMATCH.

A08 (Muscle):
- Tibialis anterior–peroneus longus junction. Motor endplate
  zones accessible from both muscles.

**Phlegm mechanism assessment (plan.md Q.11):**

Is "phlegm" (痰) mappable to Track A systems?
1. Mucus secretion: vagally mediated. If ST40→vagal pathway
   exists (paralleling ST36), could modulate airway
   secretions. Testable but untested.
2. Metabolic syndrome: systemically regulated. Indirect via
   vagal-adrenal anti-inflammatory pathway conceivable.
   Speculative.
3. Mental cloudiness/epilepsy: CNS effects via ascending
   brainstem pathways. Unconfirmed.
4. **Conceptual umbrella (most likely):** "phlegm" is NOT a
   single physiological entity but a clinical CATEGORY. Each
   phlegm sub-indication may require a different mechanism.
   Parallels the "Qi" concept — clinically useful, not
   reducible to one process.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 25–38 mm perpendicular. Target the junction
between tibialis anterior and peroneus longus. De Qi:
distension and heaviness radiating to the lateral leg.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

EA: bilateral ST40 at 2–4 Hz reported in Chinese literature
for hyperlipidemia protocols.

Bấm huyệt (B02): palpation landmark — 2 finger-breadths
lateral to tibial crest, midpoint of the leg. Firm sustained
pressure for phlegm symptoms.

Cứu (B08): traditionally used for cold-phlegm patterns
(寒痰). No moxa prohibition.

### An toàn

LOW risk — deep muscular tissue. No visceral access. ST40's
lateral position reduces proximity to the interosseous
membrane compared to ST37–ST39.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

## ST41 — Giải Khê (解溪, Jiě Xī)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Untying Stream" (解 = untie/release, 溪 = stream/ravine)
— named for the depression between the extensor hallucis
longus and extensor digitorum longus tendons that opens
("unties") when the ankle is dorsiflexed, revealing a
stream-like groove. Located at the dorsal ankle crease,
at the midpoint between the two malleoli.

ST41 is the **Five Shu River/Fire (经/火) point** of the
Stomach channel — the **tonification point** (Fire generates
Earth, 火生土 — mother nourishes child). The paired channel
counterpart is SP2 (Dadu, Spring/Fire) — also tonification
for Spleen. Same element (Fire) occupies different Five Shu
positions on the paired channels (River vs Spring),
confirming the Yin-Yang Five Shu orthogonality (AP-01
Obs. 22).

ST41 marks an anatomical transition: the tibialis anterior
corridor (ST36–ST40) ends and the dorsal foot series
(ST42–ST45) begins. The ankle crease is the hinge between
muscular and tendinous/bony territory.

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): at the junction of the
dorsum of the foot and the leg, in the depression between
the tendons of extensor hallucis longus (medially) and
extensor digitorum longus (laterally), approximately level
with the tip of the lateral malleolus.

Anatomical landmarks: at the dorsal ankle crease, midpoint
of the anterior ankle. The inferior extensor retinaculum
(cruciate ligament of the ankle) crosses directly over this
area, binding the extensor tendons to the ankle.

Cross-channel ankle architecture:
- **ST41:** anterior (between EHL + EDL). Deep peroneal.
- **SP5:** medial-inferior to medial malleolus. Saphenous.
- **LR4:** anterior to medial malleolus. Saphenous +
  superficial peroneal.
- **GB40:** anterior-inferior to lateral malleolus.
  Superficial peroneal + sural.
- **KI3:** posterior to medial malleolus. Tibial.
- **BL60:** posterior to lateral malleolus. Sural.
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP5 (Obs. 19), AP-12 LR4, AP-11 GB40

Needle depth: 10–15 mm perpendicular. Shallow — tendinous
tissue over the ankle joint, no significant muscle belly.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Deep peroneal nerve (L4–L5)** — the terminal trunk
  passes between EHL and EDL tendons under the extensor
  retinaculum at precisely this level, before bifurcating
  into: (a) medial branch (sensory, to 1st–2nd web space),
  (b) lateral branch (motor, to extensor digitorum brevis).
  ST41 is at the PRE-BIFURCATION point — comparable to
  LI11's pre-bifurcation position on the radial nerve
  (AP-02 Obs. 18).
- **Medial dorsal cutaneous nerve** (branch of superficial
  peroneal, L4–S1) — crosses superficially.
- Spinal segment: L4–L5
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: **anterior tibial artery → dorsalis pedis artery
transition.** The anterior tibial artery passes under the
extensor retinaculum at this level and becomes the dorsalis
pedis artery. ST41 is at the NAME-CHANGE point of this
arterial continuity. Pulsation palpable.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → inferior
extensor retinaculum → tendons of EHL and EDL → ankle
joint capsule. No muscle belly — entirely tendinous and
ligamentous. Periosteum of tibia/talus as floor.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Five Shu: River/Fire (经/火)** — Tonification point.

Fire generates Earth (火生土) in the Sheng cycle. Fire is
the "mother" of Earth. By tonifying the mother element,
the child (Earth/Stomach) is strengthened.

Yang Earth Five Shu system for ST:

| Five Shu | Element | Point | Five Element Role |
|----------|---------|-------|-------------------|
| Well | Metal | ST45 | Sedation |
| Spring | Water | ST44 | Ke Subject |
| Stream | Wood | ST43 | Ke Controller |
| **River** | **Fire** | **ST41** | **Tonification** |
| He-Sea | Earth | ST36 | Horary (DONE, Phase 1) |

Paired channel tonification comparison:
SP2 (Spring/Fire) = Tonification. Same element, same role,
different Five Shu position. The ELEMENT determines the Five
Element role; the Five Shu position is determined by the
Yin/Yang sequence.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST41 Jiexi (解溪). Functions: clears
Stomach heat (清胃热), calms the spirit (安神), eliminates
dampness (利湿), benefits the ankle joint. As tonification
point, strengthens Stomach Earth when deficient.

Indications: headache (Yangming ascending heat), dizziness,
facial edema, abdominal distension, constipation (Yangming
dryness), epilepsy, mania, ankle pain/swelling.

**2. Trigger point:** no direct TrP at the ankle crease —
tendinous tissue without muscle belly. Proximal TrPs in
tibialis anterior and EHL may refer through this area. 🔴

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in anterior ankle
impingement, dorsiflexor strain, ligament sprain. Clinically
significant for ankle rehabilitation. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** **Gulpha Marma** — Sushruta classifies
the ankle joint as a Sandhi (joint) type Marma. Gulpha
encompasses the entire ankle joint complex. 4th ankle point
documented within Gulpha territory (after SP5, LR4, GB40).
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP5 (Obs. 19), AP-11 GB40

**5. Head zone:** L4–L5 dermatome — matches local ankle
indications. Does NOT match defining functions (headache,
dizziness, epilepsy = supraspinal). 🔴 for primary
indications.

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL anchor at the ankle — tibialis
anterior tendon crosses the retinaculum, transitioning to
the dorsal foot. ST41 is the SFL TRANSITION POINT from leg
to foot.
→ Cross-ref: A09

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data. 🔴

**8. Cupping:** not applicable at the ankle crease. 🔴

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, Ashi, Marma, Anatomy
Trains). Consistent with ankle-area baseline (SP5: 3/8,
GB40: 4/8, LR4: 3/8).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Nanjing: River (经) points treat conditions that "come and
  go" (喘呼寒热) — intermittent dyspnea, chills and fever.
- Jiayi Jing: headache, facial edema, dizziness, epilepsy,
  abdominal distension, ankle pain.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: clears Yangming heat, benefits sinews,
  calms spirit. Facial swelling, constipation, mania, knee
  and ankle swelling.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Headache and dizziness** — Yangming ascending heat/
  dampness. Combined with ST8, GV20.
- **Epilepsy and mania** — spirit indication at Fire point.
  Combined with GV26, PC6.
- **Facial edema** — Yangming fluid metabolism.
- **Ankle pain and swelling (LOCAL)** — primary local point
  for anterior ankle pathology.
- **Constipation** — Yangming dryness pattern (distinct
  from ST37's Lower He-Sea colonic mechanism).

Classical combinations:
- **ST41 + ST36** — tonification pair for Stomach deficiency
  (River/Fire + He-Sea/Earth horary).
- **ST41 + ST44** — heat regulation pair (Fire tonification
  balanced by Water Ke Subject).
- **ST41 + GB40** — bilateral ankle combination for ankle
  joint conditions (anterior + lateral access).

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No sham-controlled evidence for isolated
ST41 effect on any indication. River (经) points as a
category are the least studied of the Five Shu — He-Sea
and Well points dominate the literature.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal data. The ankle crease area becomes swollen and
tender in ankle pathology (anterior impingement, synovitis),
which may represent dynamic sensitization at the joint level.
Not tested with C04 methodology.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST41:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- Deep peroneal nerve PRE-BIFURCATION: still a single trunk
  carrying both sensory and motor fibers. Needling at a pre-
  bifurcation site accesses ALL fiber types of the terminal
  nerve — maximum neural diversity per needle. Analogous to
  LI11 radial nerve pre-split (AP-02 Obs. 18).
- L4–L5 → local ankle/foot: segmental MATCH.
- L4–L5 → headache, dizziness, epilepsy: segmental MISMATCH.
  Supraspinal pathway required.

A03 (Fascia):
- Extensor retinaculum: dense fascial band spanning the
  ankle. Needle penetrates this retinaculum into the tendon
  sheath — confined fascial compartment.

A07 (Segmental):
- LOCAL: L4–L5 = ankle/foot. CONVERGENCE.
- DISTANT: headache (CN V), epilepsy (cortical). MISMATCH.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 10–15 mm perpendicular. Shallow insertion
between EHL and EDL tendons. Excessive depth risks ankle
joint capsule. De Qi: localized distension, may radiate to
dorsum of foot.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

Bấm huyệt (B02): dorsiflex the foot to reveal the two
tendons, press the depression between them. Self-applicable
for ankle pain.

Cứu (B08): applicable. No moxa prohibition. Indirect moxa
preferred due to tendinous tissue.

### An toàn

LOW risk — superficial tendinous tissue over the ankle joint.
No visceral access.

Anterior tibial/dorsalis pedis artery runs between the
tendons at depth. Pulsation often palpable — needle lateral
to the pulse to avoid arterial puncture.

Excessive depth risks ankle joint capsule penetration — limit
to 10–15 mm.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

## ST42 — Xung Dương (冲阳, Chōng Yáng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Surging Yang" (冲 = surge/rush, 阳 = yang) — named for
the prominent pulsation of the dorsalis pedis artery felt
at this point, the "surging" quality of Yang Qi rising from
the foot. Classical alternative name: 趺阳 (fūyáng, "instep
yang"), used interchangeably in the Neijing for pulse
diagnosis.

ST42 is the **Yuan-Source point (原穴)** of the Stomach
channel — the 10th Yuan-Source documented in the Atlas (after
LU9, LI4, HT7, SI4, PC7, TE4, LR3, SP3, GB40). As a Yang
channel Yuan, ST42 is SEPARATE from the Five Shu system:
it occupies its own position between ST41 (River/Fire) and
ST43 (Stream/Wood), confirming the Yang Yuan ≠ Five Shu
pattern at 10/12 (5 Yin overlap, 5 Yang separate).

Host-Guest (主客) Yuan-Luo pairing:
- **ST as host:** ST42 (Yuan) + SP4 (Luo of SP) = treat
  Stomach via own Yuan + paired channel's Luo
- **SP as host:** SP3 (Yuan) + ST40 (Luo of ST) = treat
  Spleen (see ST40 entry above)
→ Cross-ref: AP-04 SP4 (line 1153)

ST42 is at the **dorsalis pedis artery pulse** — one of
three classical pulse diagnostic sites:
- 寸口 LU9 (radial, wrist) — Yuan-Source of LU
- 人迎 ST9 (carotid, neck) — Window of Heaven, Sea of Qi
- **趺阳 ST42 (dorsalis pedis, foot) — Yuan-Source of ST**
→ Cross-ref: Obs. 11 (Four Seas), ST9 entry

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the dorsum of the
foot, at the highest point of the dorsal foot arch, in the
depression between the 2nd and 3rd metatarsal bones and
the cuneiform bone, where the dorsalis pedis artery
pulsates.

Anatomical landmarks: between the tendons of extensor
hallucis longus (medially) and extensor digitorum longus
to the 2nd toe (laterally). Proximal to the 2nd
metatarsophalangeal joint. The dorsalis pedis artery
pulsation is the PRIMARY landmark for locating ST42.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm perpendicular or oblique. Shallow —
thin subcutaneous tissue over metatarsal bones.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Deep peroneal nerve, medial branch (L4–L5)** — the
  sensory terminal branch, running alongside the dorsalis
  pedis artery between 1st and 2nd metatarsals. By this
  level, the deep peroneal has fully bifurcated (at ST41).
- **Intermediate dorsal cutaneous nerve** (branch of
  superficial peroneal, L4–S1) — sensory to the dorsal
  foot surface between 2nd–4th rays.
- Spinal segment: L4–L5
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: **dorsalis pedis artery** — continuation of the
anterior tibial artery, running on the dorsum of the foot
between 1st and 2nd metatarsals. At ST42, the artery is
superficial (1–3 mm depth), covered only by skin, fascia,
and extensor tendons. Pulsation easily palpable.

Branches at this level: first dorsal metatarsal artery,
arcuate artery (crossing laterally), deep plantar artery
(perforating to sole).

Tissue layers: skin → thin subcutaneous tissue → extensor
tendons → dorsal interosseous fascia → metatarsal
periosteum. No significant muscle belly.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

**Yuan-Source: YES** — 原穴 of the Stomach channel.

The Yin/Yang Yuan-Source divergence is now confirmed at
**10/12** — 5th Yang confirmation (after LI4, SI4, TE4,
GB40):

| Channel | Polarity | Yuan-Source | = Five Shu? |
|---------|----------|-------------|-------------|
| LU | Yin | LU9 | = Stream/Earth ✓ |
| LI | Yang | LI4 | ≠ Five Shu ✗ |
| HT | Yin | HT7 | = Stream/Earth ✓ |
| SI | Yang | SI4 | ≠ Five Shu ✗ |
| PC | Yin | PC7 | = Stream/Earth ✓ |
| TE | Yang | TE4 | ≠ Five Shu ✗ |
| SP | Yin | SP3 | = Stream/Earth ✓ |
| LR | Yin | LR3 | = Stream/Earth ✓ |
| GB | Yang | GB40 | ≠ Five Shu ✗ |
| **ST** | **Yang** | **ST42** | **≠ Five Shu ✗** |

100% consistency across 10 data points: ALL 5 Yin overlap
(Yuan = Stream/Earth); ALL 5 documented Yang separate
(Yuan = standalone). Remaining: BL (Yang, predicted
separate) and KI (Yin, predicted overlap). The Nan Jing
Ch. 62 explanation: Yang channels receive extra Qi from
San Jiao (三焦), granting a 6th transport point between
Stream and River.
→ Cross-ref: AP-11 GB40, AP-10 TE4

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST42 Chongyang (冲阳). Functions:
calms the spirit (安神), harmonizes Stomach (和胃), clears
Yangming heat (清阳明热). As Yuan-Source, accesses the
Stomach's "original Qi" (原气) for both diagnosis and
treatment.

Indications: toothache (Yangming heat), facial edema,
mental disorders (epilepsy, mania), gastric pain, dorsal
foot pain. Classical: 趺阳 pulse palpation for assessing
Stomach Qi vitality — present pulse = intact post-natal Qi;
absent = grave prognosis.

**2. Trigger point:** extensor digitorum brevis (EDB) TrPs
on the lateral dorsal foot. EDB bellies underlie the dorsal
foot — TrPs refer to the dorsal surface. ST42 overlaps the
medial edge of EDB territory. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in dorsal foot overuse,
metatarsal stress, tight-fitting footwear compression.
Common tender point in runners. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** no specific Marma at the dorsal mid-
foot. Gulpha (ankle) is more proximal, Kshipra (web space)
more distal. ST42 falls between Marma zones. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** L4–L5 dermatome — matches local foot.
Does NOT match defining functions (toothache = CN V3,
epilepsy = cortical). 🔴 for primary indications.

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL distal anchor — tibialis anterior
tendon inserts at medial cuneiform/1st metatarsal base, and
dorsal foot extensors continue the SFL to the toes. ST42 is
at this distal SFL terminus.
→ Cross-ref: A09

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data. 🔴

**8. Cupping:** not practical on the dorsal foot. 🔴

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy Trains).

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Su Wen: 趺阳脉 pulse diagnosis — "when the Chongyang
  pulse is present, Stomach Qi is intact."
- Ling Shu: Yuan-Source point for accessing Stomach's
  original Qi.
- Jiayi Jing: facial edema, toothache, mouth deviation,
  epilepsy, mania, gastric pain.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: calms spirit, harmonizes Stomach, clears
  heat. Facial swelling, deviated mouth, dorsal foot pain.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Toothache (Yangming heat)** — upper teeth, where ST
  channel passes through the upper gum.
- **Facial edema and swelling** — Yangming fluid metabolism.
- **Mental disorders** — Yuan-Source spirit function.
  Combined with GV26, PC6.
- **Gastric pain** — direct organ access via Yuan.
- **Dorsal foot pain (LOCAL)** — tendinous/metatarsal
  pathology.

Classical combinations:
- **ST42 (Yuan) + SP4 (Luo)** — Host-Guest for Stomach
  conditions. SP4 also opens Chong Mai, making this
  combination multi-layered.
- **ST42 + ST36** — Yuan-Source + He-Sea for comprehensive
  Stomach treatment.
- **ST42 + LI4** — dual Yuan-Source for Yangming headache
  and toothache.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated. No sham-controlled evidence for isolated
ST42 therapeutic effect.

The dorsalis pedis pulse at ST42 is a standard clinical
assessment in modern vascular medicine (peripheral arterial
disease screening). The same anatomical landmark serves
both TCM pulse diagnosis and modern vascular assessment —
a rare convergence of traditional and evidence-based
diagnostic practice at one site.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No formal sensitization data. Dorsalis pedis pulse amplitude
at ST42 may vary with gastrointestinal state (postprandial
vs fasting) via sympathetic/parasympathetic tone, but this
has not been studied in acupoint dynamics context.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST42:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- Deep peroneal nerve medial branch (L4–L5) — sensory
  terminal. Post-bifurcation: fewer fiber types than at
  ST41 (pre-bifurcation). Neural access decreasing distally
  as the nerve thins toward digital terminals.
- L4–L5 → local foot: CONVERGENCE.
- L4–L5 → toothache (CN V3), epilepsy (cortical): MISMATCH.
  Supraspinal pathway required.

A03 (Fascia):
- Dorsal interosseous fascia. Thin fascial layer over
  metatarsals. Limited fascial bulk compared to proximal
  leg points.

A07 (Segmental):
- LOCAL: L4–L5 = foot. CONVERGENCE.
- DISTANT: toothache, spirit, gastric. MISMATCH for all.

**Pulse diagnostic mechanism (plan.md Q.12):**

The classical three-pulse system (寸口/人迎/趺阳) uses
arterial palpation at three sites to assess systemic
vitality. Two are Yuan-Source points (LU9, ST42), one is
Window of Heaven (ST9).

Modern parallel: the dorsalis pedis pulse is standard
peripheral vascular assessment. Absence indicates peripheral
arterial disease. The TCM interpretation (absent 趺阳 =
Stomach Qi failure) maps to modern vascular insufficiency —
both indicate compromised distal perfusion.

Yuan-Source pulse pattern: LU9 (radial), ST42 (dorsalis
pedis), and to a lesser extent HT7 (ulnar) and LR3 (near
dorsalis pedis) — multiple Yuan-Source points sit at major
arterial palpation sites. This suggests 原 (source) may
describe where organ vitality surfaces as palpable pulse.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 5–10 mm perpendicular or oblique, AVOIDING the
dorsalis pedis artery. Palpate the pulse FIRST, insert
LATERAL to the pulsation. De Qi: localized soreness on the
dorsum of the foot.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

Bấm huyệt (B02): find the dorsalis pedis pulse at the
highest point of the foot arch, press firmly lateral to
the artery.

Cứu (B08): applicable. No moxa prohibition. Indirect moxa
preferred given thin tissue and arterial proximity.

### An toàn

MODERATE risk — dorsalis pedis artery directly at the point.

**Arterial puncture precaution:** the dorsalis pedis artery
is superficial (1–3 mm depth) and pulsation is the PRIMARY
landmark. Always palpate the pulse and insert LATERAL to it.
Hematoma risk if punctured — apply firm pressure post-
needling.

In patients with peripheral arterial disease, the pulse may
be weak or absent — altered landmark reliability.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

### Batch 9 Architectural Observations

> **Observation 28 — LUO-CONNECTING FUNCTIONAL SPECTRUM:
> ST40 AS BROADEST-SCOPE LUO POINT.**
>
> ST40 is the 10th Luo-Connecting point documented. The Luo
> system now shows a clear functional spectrum:
>
> | Type | Points | Scope |
> |------|--------|-------|
> | Channel-local | LI6, SI7 | Paired-channel connection |
> | Extraordinary vessel | LU7, SP4, PC6, TE5 | Opens vessel system |
> | Sensory specialty | HT5, GB37 | Distant sensory target |
> | **Systemic** | **ST40** | **Cross-organ phlegm** |
>
> ST40's phlegm function is unique: not organ-specific
> (unlike Lower He-Sea) or sensory-specific (unlike HT5/
> GB37) but SYSTEMIC — affecting respiratory, digestive,
> metabolic, and neurological domains simultaneously. No
> other Luo has such broad therapeutic scope.
>
> The Luo excess pathology (throat obstruction/voice loss)
> adds another non-segmental claim: L4–L5 → throat (CN X).
> This shares the proposed supraspinal mechanism with the
> Lower He-Sea system (L4–L5 → brainstem → vagal), suggesting
> that ST40's Luo pathology and the Lower He-Sea function may
> activate the SAME brainstem relay — different categorical
> designation, overlapping mechanism.
> → Cross-ref: AP-06 SI7, AP-09 PC6, AP-10 TE5, AP-11 GB37

> **Observation 29 — YUAN-SOURCE PULSE DIAGNOSTIC AXIS:
> ST CHANNEL PROVIDES 2 OF 3 CLASSICAL PULSE SITES.**
>
> ST42 completes the classical three-pulse diagnostic system
> (三部脉): 寸口 LU9 (radial, wrist), 人迎 ST9 (carotid,
> neck), 趺阳 ST42 (dorsalis pedis, foot). The ST channel
> provides 2 of 3 pulse sites — consistent with ST's Four
> Seas dominance (3/4 seas, Obs. 11) and its role as "Sea
> of Water and Grain" (primary vitality assessor).
>
> Two of three pulse sites are Yuan-Source points (LU9,
> ST42); the third (ST9) is Window of Heaven. Yuan-Source
> at major arteries supports the literal reading of 原
> (source) as where organ Qi surfaces as palpable pulse.
>
> Yang Yuan ≠ Five Shu confirmed at 10/12 (5 Yin overlap,
> 5 Yang separate). 100% consistency across 10 data points.
> Two channels remain: BL (Yang, predicted separate) and KI
> (Yin, predicted overlap). This remains the most
> consistently confirmed structural rule in the Atlas.
> → Cross-ref: LU9 (AP-01), ST9 entry, AP-11 GB40

> **Observation 30 — EARTH PAIR HOST-GUEST QUADRUPLE:
> 5TH COMPLETE INTERIOR-EXTERIOR YUAN-LUO COUPLING.**
>
> With ST40 (Luo) and ST42 (Yuan), both directions of the
> ST↔SP Host-Guest coupling are fully documented: SP3(Yuan)
> +ST40(Luo) treats SP; ST42(Yuan)+SP4(Luo) treats ST.
> This is the 5th complete Interior-Exterior Yuan-Luo
> quadruple (after LU-LI, HT-SI, PC-TE, LR-GB).
>
> The Earth pair is architecturally the most multi-layered:
> - SP4 (Luo) also carries Eight Confluent (Chong Mai + Yin
>   Wei Mai) — the ONLY Host-Guest pair where the Luo member
>   opens an extraordinary vessel
> - SP3 (Yuan) also carries triple designation (Yuan +
>   Stream/Earth + Horary) — highest designation density at
>   any Yuan point in a paired channel
> - SP4+PC6 = double-Luo Eight Confluent pair (AP-04 Obs. 3)
>   — the ONLY Eight Confluent pair where both members are
>   Luo-Connecting points
>
> The Earth pair's Host-Guest system thus intersects Five Shu,
> Eight Confluent, Chong Mai, and Horary networks — more
> categorical overlap than any other Interior-Exterior
> coupling documented to date.
> → Cross-ref: AP-04 SP3 (line 816), SP4 (line 1153)

## ST43 — Hãm Cốc (陷谷, Xiàn Gǔ)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Sunken Valley" (陷 = sunken/depressed, 谷 = valley) — named
for the palpable depression between the 2nd and 3rd metatarsal
bones on the dorsum of the foot.

ST43 is the **Five Shu Stream/Wood point (输穴/木穴)** of the
Stomach channel. In the Ke cycle, Wood controls Earth (木克土)
— making ST43 the **Ke Controller (克主)**, the element that
RESTRAINS the channel's Earth. Cross-cycle check on Stomach
excess, stronger than Sedation (Sheng-cycle draining).

Nan Jing Ch. 68: "输主体重节痛" — Stream points treat
heaviness of body and joint pain. For Yang channels only:
Stream also treats intermittent diseases (时病) — periodic
fevers, malaria-like alternating states. Yang-exclusive
positional function.
→ Cross-ref: LI3 (AP-02) — same Stream 时病 function

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the dorsum of the
foot, in the depression distal to the junction of the 2nd
and 3rd metatarsal bones.

Anatomical landmarks: proximal to the 2nd metatarso-
phalangeal joint, in the intermetatarsal space. ~1 cun
distal to ST42, ~1 cun proximal to ST44.

Needle depth: 10–15 mm perpendicular or oblique. Shallow
tissue over bone.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Intermediate dorsal cutaneous nerve** (branch of
  superficial peroneal nerve, L5–S1) — the 2nd common
  dorsal digital nerve supplies this intermetatarsal space.
- Deep peroneal nerve: ALREADY BIFURCATED at ST41–ST42
  level. Its medial terminal branch serves the 1st web
  space only. ST43 is predominantly superficial peroneal
  territory — continuing the post-bifurcation transition.
- Spinal segment: L5–S1
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Vascular: 2nd dorsal metatarsal artery (from dorsalis pedis
→ arcuate artery). Small caliber, low risk.

Tissue layers: skin → subcutaneous tissue → dorsal digital
expansion → 2nd dorsal interosseous muscle (between 2nd–3rd
metatarsals). Thin tissue over bone.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: Stream/Wood (输穴/木穴) — 3rd in the Five Shu
sequence. All Yang channels have Stream=Wood.

Five Element role: **Ke Controller (克主)**. Wood controls
Earth in the Ke cycle (木克土).

Stream/Wood role across documented Yang channels:

| Channel (element) | Point | Role | Ke relationship |
|--------------------|-------|------|-----------------|
| ST (Earth) | ST43 | **Ke Controller** | Wood controls Earth |
| LI (Metal) | LI3 | Ke Subject | Metal controls Wood |
| GB (Wood) | GB41 | Horary | Wood = Wood |
| SI (Fire) | SI3 | Tonification | Wood generates Fire |
| TE (Fire) | TE3 | Tonification | Wood generates Fire |

Same element (Wood) at same position (Stream) → 4 different
roles across 5 channels. Algebraic structure confirmed.

ST43 + SP1: the Earth Interior-Exterior pair shares the
SAME Ke Controller element (Wood) but at DIFFERENT Five Shu
positions — ST43 Stream/Wood (3rd) vs SP1 Well/Wood (1st).
Parallels all other documented Interior-Exterior pairs.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST43 Xiangu (陷谷). Functions:
regulates Stomach Qi (理胃气), reduces edema (利水消肿),
activates channel. Indications: facial/eye edema, abdominal
distension, borborygmus, dorsal foot pain, intermittent
fever (时病 — Yang Stream function).

**2. Trigger point:** 2nd dorsal interosseous TrP (Travell &
Simons) — referred pain to 2nd–3rd metatarsal area. Muscle
belly extends between the metatarsal shafts where ST43 is
located; TrP center slightly more distal near MTP joint.
Partial overlap. 🟡

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in 2nd metatarsal stress
fracture, metatarsalgia, dorsal foot edema. Common in
runners. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Kshipra Marma is at the 1st web space
(big toe–2nd toe) — ~1 cm medial. Not direct. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** L5–S1 dermatome. Somatic only, no visceral
correspondence. 🔴

**6. Anatomy Trains:** Superficial Front Line (SFL) — EDB
tendons pass over this area, distal SFL pathway. 🟡

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data at dorsal foot. 🔴

**8. Cupping:** not applicable at dorsal foot. 🔴

**Convergence score: 4/8** (TCM, TrP, Ashi, Anatomy Trains).
Maintains the 4/8 level from ankle through metatarsal.
Edema and intermittent disease — ST43's classical
functions — have no cross-tradition support.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- Nan Jing Ch. 68: Stream = heaviness and joint pain; Yang
  Stream = intermittent disease (时病).
- Jiayi Jing: facial edema, eye edema, abdominal distension,
  borborygmus, alternating fever-chills, foot dorsum pain.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: facial swelling, edema, Stomach heat,
  intestinal rumbling, periodic fevers, foot pain.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Edema (PRIMARY):** facial edema, general edema —
  commonly cited ST point for 利水消肿.
- **Gastrointestinal:** abdominal distension, borborygmus,
  diarrhea.
- **Intermittent fever:** periodic conditions — Yang Stream
  positional function.
- **Local:** dorsal foot pain and swelling, metatarsalgia.

Classical combinations:
- **ST43 + SP9 + SP6** — edema protocol (Yangming water
  regulation + SP9 dampness + SP6 Yin meeting).
- **ST43 + ST44** — Ke Controller + Ke Subject: dual Ke-
  cycle approach for Stomach imbalance.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for all indications. No SR or RCT isolating
ST43. Edema protocols use multi-point approaches without
ST43-specific evidence.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data. Metatarsal dorsum is not a typical
sensitization site — thin tissue without muscular depth.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST43:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- Intermediate dorsal cutaneous nerve (L5–S1) → DRG L5 →
  ascending. Post-bifurcation superficial peroneal territory.

A07 (Segmental):
- LOCAL (foot): L5–S1 matches. CONVERGENCE for foot pain.
- DISTANT (facial edema): L5 → face (CN V/VII) = maximum
  segmental mismatch. Same non-segmental pattern as distal
  ST points claiming facial effects.

Edema mechanism: 利水消肿 requires systemic neuroendocrine
modulation (ADH, RAAS). No point-specific mechanism.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 10–15 mm perpendicular into the intermetatarsal
space. De Qi: localized soreness on dorsum of foot.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

Bấm huyệt (B02): press into the depression between 2nd–3rd
metatarsals.

Cứu (B08): applicable. No moxa prohibition. Indirect moxa
preferred on thin dorsal foot tissue.

### An toàn

LOW risk — dorsal foot, no major vascular or neural
structures. No visceral access.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST44 — Nội Đình (内庭, Nèi Tíng)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Inner Court" (内 = inner, 庭 = court/courtyard) — named for
the space between the toes, like a courtyard between walls.
Between the 2nd and 3rd toes, proximal to the web margin.

ST44 is the **Five Shu Spring/Water point (荥穴/水穴)** and
a **Ma Dan-yang Heavenly Star Point (马丹阳天星穴)**. In the
Ke cycle, Earth controls Water (土克水) — making ST44 the
**Ke Subject (被克)**, the element that the channel's Earth
DOMINATES.

The Spring position carries a universal heat-clearing
function: "荥主身热" (Nan Jing Ch. 68) — Spring points treat
Heat in the body. This positional function CONVERGES with the
elemental quality (Water cools), reinforcing ST44's role as
the primary Yangming heat-clearing point.

Counterpart: **LI2** (Hand Yangming Spring/Water). Same
element, same position, same Yangming family — but LI2 is
Sedation (Metal generates Water), not Ke Subject.
→ Cross-ref: LI2 (AP-02)

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the dorsum of the
foot, proximal to the web margin between the 2nd and 3rd
toes, at the junction of the red and white skin (赤白肉际).

Anatomical landmarks: toe web space between 2nd and 3rd
toes. Red-white skin junction = transition between glabrous
(plantar) and hairy (dorsal) skin — a sensory transition
zone with high receptor density.

Needle depth: 5–10 mm oblique proximally along the
interdigital space.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **2nd common dorsal digital nerve** (from intermediate
  dorsal cutaneous nerve, superficial peroneal, L5–S1) —
  supplies opposing surfaces of the 2nd and 3rd toes.
- Plantar: 2nd common plantar digital nerve (from medial
  plantar nerve, tibial, S1–S2). The dorsal-plantar junction
  at this point concentrates both nerve territories.
- Spinal segment: L5–S1 (dorsal), S1–S2 (plantar)
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

Web space: thin tissue, no muscle belly. Skin, sparse
subcutaneous connective tissue, digital vessels and nerves.

Vascular: dorsal digital arteries (small caliber). Minimal
risk.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: Spring/Water (荥穴/水穴) — 2nd in the Five Shu
sequence. All Yang channels have Spring=Water.

Five Element role: **Ke Subject (被克)**. Earth controls
Water in the Ke cycle (土克水). ST44 embodies the element
that the channel's Earth DOMINATES.

Dual reinforcement: positional (Spring = Heat clearing) +
elemental (Water = cooling) converge, making ST44 the
strongest heat-clearing point on the ST channel.

Spring/Water across documented Yang channels:

| Channel (element) | Point | Role | Relationship |
|--------------------|-------|------|-------------|
| ST (Earth) | ST44 | **Ke Subject** | Earth controls Water |
| LI (Metal) | LI2 | Sedation | Metal generates Water |
| SI (Fire) | SI2 | Ke Controller | Water controls Fire |
| TE (Fire) | TE2 | Ke Controller | Water controls Fire |
| GB (Wood) | GB43 | Tonification | Water generates Wood |

5 documented, 4 different roles. BL66 (Water channel,
predicted Horary) will complete all 5 roles at Spring/Water.

Same-Name divergence: LI2 (Hand Yangming) = Sedation vs
ST44 (Foot Yangming) = Ke Subject. Both clear Yangming Heat
(positional function shared), but roles differ because LI =
Metal organ while ST = Earth organ.

**Ma Dan-yang Heavenly Star Point (马丹阳天星穴):** one of
the 12 essential points selected by Ma Dan-yang (马丹阳,
Song dynasty). 7th Ma Dan-yang Star documented:

| Point | Channel | Key categories |
|-------|---------|---------------|
| LU7 | Lung | Luo, Command, Eight Confluent |
| LI4 | Large Intestine | Yuan-Source, Command |
| LI11 | Large Intestine | He-Sea/Earth |
| ST36 | Stomach | He-Sea, Command, Lower He-Sea |
| GB30 | Gallbladder | — |
| GB34 | Gallbladder | He-Sea, Hui-Meeting Sinews |
| **ST44** | **Stomach** | **Spring/Water, Ke Subject** |

ST channel: 2 Ma Dan-yang Stars (ST36, ST44). Matching LI
(LI4, LI11) and GB (GB30, GB34). The Yangming pair (LI +
ST) contributes 4/12 — most of any same-name or Interior-
Exterior pair.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST44 Neiting (内庭). Functions: clears
Yangming Heat/Fire (清阳明热), harmonizes intestines, calms
spirit, alleviates pain.

Indications by domain:
- **Yangming Heat (PRIMARY):** toothache (upper teeth match
  ST trajectory), facial pain, sore throat, epistaxis —
  Heat signs along Yangming.
- **Gastrointestinal:** epigastric pain, acid reflux,
  diarrhea with foul odor, abdominal distension.
- **Febrile:** tidal fever (潮热) — Yangming afternoon fever
  (日晡潮热, Shang Han Lun Yangming channel pattern).
- **Local:** dorsal foot pain, toe swelling.

Jaundice formula: LR4 + GB34 + LR3 + ST44 — damp-heat
jaundice protocol, 3 channels.
→ Cross-ref: AP-12 LR4, AP-11 GB34

**2. Trigger point:** no muscle at toe web. 🔴

**3. Ashi correspondence:** tender in 2nd–3rd web space
inflammation, metatarsalgia radiating to toes. Limited. 🟡

**4. Marma point:** Kshipra Marma is at the 1st web space —
one web space medial. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** L5–S1. No visceral Heat correspondence. 🔴

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL tendons pass to individual toes
through the web area but no fascial junction at this level.
Minimal engagement. 🔴

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data at toe web. 🔴

**8. Cupping:** not applicable. 🔴

**Convergence score: 2/8** (TCM, Ashi). Drops from 4/8 at
ST43 — loss of muscle belly removes TrP, and AT is no longer
substantive. The heat-clearing function — ST44's defining
role — has NO cross-tradition support.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Nan Jing Ch. 68:** "荥主身热" — Spring treats body Heat.
- **Shang Han Lun:** Yangming channel pattern (日晡潮热,
  tidal afternoon fever, constipation). ST44 for channel
  Heat (经热) vs ST25 for Fu organ Heat (腑热).
- Jiayi Jing: toothache, facial swelling, sore throat,
  fever, abdominal distension, nosebleed, foot pain.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: toothache (upper jaw), epistaxis, acid
  reflux, tidal fever, diarrhea.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Yangming Heat/Fire (PRIMARY):** toothache (upper teeth),
  gum pain, sore throat, epistaxis. FIRST point for acute
  Yangming channel Heat.
- **Gastrointestinal Heat:** epigastric pain, acid reflux,
  halitosis, burning diarrhea.
- **Febrile:** high fever, tidal fever — Spring + Water =
  strongest fever-clearing combination on ST channel.
- **Local:** dorsal foot pain, toe swelling.

Classical combinations:
- **ST44 + LI4** — paired Yangming Heat clearing (face,
  teeth, throat).
- **ST44 + LI2** — Hand + Foot Yangming Spring pair for
  maximum Yangming heat-clearing.
- **ST44 + ST41** — heat regulation pair (Water Ke Subject
  balanced by Fire Tonification).
  → Cross-ref: ST41 entry
- **LR4 + GB34 + LR3 + ST44** — damp-heat jaundice.

Yangming channel vs Fu differentiation:
- **ST44 (Spring/Water):** Yangming CHANNEL Heat (经热) —
  fever, toothache, facial Heat symptoms.
- **ST25 (Front-Mu):** Yangming FU organ Heat (腑热) —
  constipation, abdominal distension.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for ST44-specific indications. No SR or RCT
isolating ST44.

ST44 appears in protocols for dental pain and facial
conditions, always in combination. The Ma Dan-yang Star
designation reflects historical clinical consensus (Song
dynasty) but not evidence of efficacy.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data. Toe web spaces are not typical
sensitization sites — minimal muscular tissue.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST44:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- 2nd common dorsal digital nerve (L5–S1) + plantar digital
  nerve (S1–S2) → dual dorsal-plantar input at the red-
  white skin sensory transition zone.

A07 (Segmental):
- LOCAL (toe): L5–S1 matches. CONVERGENCE.
- DISTANT (face, fever): complete MISMATCH. Toothache
  (CN V2) and thermoregulation (hypothalamus) have no
  segmental connection to L5.

Heat-clearing mechanism: the positional (Spring = Heat) and
elemental (Water = cooling) logic is internally consistent
within TCM but has NO neurophysiological correlate. The heat-
clearing function is the most widely utilized UNTESTED
classical claim across the Five Shu system.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 5–10 mm oblique proximally into the web space.
De Qi: localized soreness between the toes.
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

Bấm huyệt (B02): pinch the web space between 2nd and 3rd
toes.

Cứu (B08): applicable but less common — moxa for cold
patterns contradicts the primary heat-clearing use.

### An toàn

LOW risk — toe web, minimal depth. No major vascular or
neural structures.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.

---

## ST45 — Lệ Đoài (厉兑, Lì Duì)

**Evidence tier: B (Standard Entry)**
"Severe Mouth" / "Stern Exchange" (厉 = severe/stern,
兑 = mouth/exchange — the Dui trigram in the Yijing
represents mouth, lake, joy). Some interpret 厉兑 as
"severity at the orifice" — the terminal exchange point
where the channel exits.

ST45 is the **Five Shu Well/Metal point (井穴/金穴)** —
the most distal point and the channel EXIT. In the Sheng
cycle, Earth generates Metal (土生金) — making ST45 the
**Sedation point (泻穴/子穴)**, the "child" that drains
excess from the Earth parent.

ST45 is classically the primary point for **insomnia and
dream-disturbed sleep** (失眠, 多梦). Ling Shu Ch. 71
(邪客) explicitly prescribes bleeding ST45 for Yangming-
type insomnia: "胃不和则卧不安" — when the Stomach is
disharmonious, one cannot sleep peacefully.

Channel exit: ST45 → SP1. Yangming exits at the lateral
2nd toe; Taiyin begins at the medial big toe — crossing
both toes and nerve territories (superficial peroneal L5
→ deep peroneal L4–L5).

### Vị trí giải phẫu

WHO standard location (WPRO 2008): on the lateral side of
the 2nd toe, 0.1 cun proximal to the corner of the toenail.

Anatomical landmarks: lateral nail fold of the 2nd toe.
Counterpart: LI1 (radial nail corner of the index finger,
Well/Metal of Hand Yangming).

Needle depth: 1–3 mm superficial prick. NOT a deep-needling
point — primary technique is bloodletting.

### Cấu trúc mô học tại điểm

Nerve supply:
- **Proper dorsal digital nerve of the lateral 2nd toe**
  (from superficial peroneal nerve, L5) — terminal branch.
  The MOST DISTAL nerve access on the ST channel.
- The nail bed contains the highest density of free nerve
  endings (A-delta and C fibers) — critical for bloodletting
  mechanism.
- Spinal segment: L5
→ Cross-ref: A01 S1

The ST channel spans CN V1 (orbital, ST1) to L5 (2nd toe,
ST45) — ~20 spinal segments plus 3 cranial nerve divisions.
ST42–ST45 are exclusively in superficial peroneal territory,
post-bifurcation.

Vascular: proper dorsal digital arteries (minute caliber).
Minimal bleeding from bloodletting.

Tissue layers: skin → nail bed/nail fold. Dense capillary
loops, high nerve density, minimal intervening tissue.

### Phân loại huyệt (Point Category Membership)

Five Shu: Well/Metal (井穴/金穴) — 1st in the Five Shu
sequence (most distal). All Yang channels have Well=Metal.

Five Element role: **Sedation point (泻穴/子穴)**. Earth
generates Metal in the Sheng cycle (土生金). Stimulating
the child drains excess from the parent.

Nan Jing Ch. 68: "井主心下满" — Well points treat fullness
below the Heart. Positional function for all Well points.

Well/Metal across all 6 Yang channels — COMPLETE role set:

| Channel (element) | Point | Role | Status |
|--------------------|-------|------|--------|
| LI (Metal) | LI1 | Horary (self) | Documented |
| **ST (Earth)** | **ST45** | **Sedation (child)** | **NEW** |
| SI (Fire) | SI1 | Ke Subject (controlled) | Documented |
| TE (Fire) | TE1 | Ke Subject (controlled) | Documented |
| GB (Wood) | GB44 | Ke Controller (restrains) | Documented |
| BL (Water) | BL67 | Tonification (parent) | Predicted |

ALL 5 possible Five Element roles appear at this single
position — the only position where every role is
demonstrably present. 5/6 channels documented, 5/5 roles
confirmed or predicted. BL67 will complete 6/6.

12 Jing-Well set (十二井穴): ST45 is the 10th Well
documented (after LI1, SI1, TE1, PC9, HT9, LU11, SP1,
LR1, GB44). Remaining: BL67, KI1.

Channel exit: ST45 (lateral 2nd toe, superficial peroneal
L5) → SP1 (medial big toe, deep peroneal L4–L5). The
Yangming→Taiyin transition crosses neural territories and
toes.

Sedation point evidence desert: no Sedation point in the
Atlas has point-specific evidence for the sedation function.
Universal across all 10 documented channels.

### Cross-tradition mapping

**1. TCM acupoint:** ST45 Lidui (厉兑). Functions: clears
Yangming Heat (清热), calms spirit and promotes sleep
(安神助眠), opens orifices (开窍).

Primary indications:
- **Insomnia / dream-disturbed sleep (PRIMARY):** Ling Shu
  Ch. 71 mechanism: Yangming excess → Wei Qi stays in Yang
  → eyes cannot close → insomnia.
- **Mental disorders:** mania (狂), agitation, delirium.
- **Emergency:** febrile delirium, loss of consciousness —
  12 Jing-Well bleeding.
- **Local:** toothache, facial swelling.

**2. Trigger point:** no muscle at nail corner. 🔴

**3. Ashi correspondence:** no common pathology at the
lateral 2nd toenail. 🔴

**4. Marma point:** no Marma at toenail corners. 🔴

**5. Head zone:** L5. No visceral or sleep-related Head
zone. 🔴

**6. Anatomy Trains:** SFL terminal — tendon insertions at
toe tip. Minimal. 🔴

**7. Neurogenic spot:** no data. 🔴

**8. Cupping:** not applicable. 🔴

**Convergence score: 1/8** (TCM only). Consistent with ALL
documented Well points (LI1 2/8 includes sham contamination;
all others 1/8). The insomnia indication — ST45's DEFINING
function — has ZERO cross-tradition support.

### Chỉ định truyền thống

Nguồn cổ:
- **Ling Shu Ch. 71 (邪客):** "胃不和则卧不安" — Stomach
  disharmony → restless sleep. Prescribes bleeding ST45 to
  drain Yangming excess and restore sleep. The most specific
  classical insomnia prescription: diagnosis (Yangming
  excess) → pathophysiology (Wei Qi in Yang) → point (ST45
  Sedation) → technique (bleeding) → outcome (sleep).
- Ling Shu Ch. 2: Well points treat fullness below Heart.
- Jiayi Jing: mania, facial swelling, dream-disturbed
  sleep, toothache, epistaxis.
- Zhenjiu Dacheng: insomnia, excessive dreaming, mania,
  facial paralysis. Bleeding emphasized.

Nguồn hiện đại (Deadman, WHO):
- **Insomnia (PRIMARY):** FIRST point for Yangming-type
  insomnia — excess → restlessness → inability to close
  eyes. Differentiated from other insomnia points:

  | Point | Mechanism | Best for |
  |-------|-----------|----------|
  | **ST45** | **Sedation: drain excess** | **Restless insomnia, agitation** |
  | HT7 | Yuan: anchor spirit | Heart Blood deficiency |
  | SP6 | Three Yin: nourish Yin | Yin deficiency, night sweats |
  | KI1 | Well: descend Fire | Heart-Kidney disharmony |

- **Mental disorders:** mania, agitation, delirium.
- **Emergency:** febrile delirium, consciousness loss —
  12 Jing-Well bleeding protocol.
- **Local:** toothache, facial swelling.

Classical combinations:
- **ST45 bleeding (standalone):** Ling Shu insomnia
  protocol. Bleeding before bedtime.
- **ST45 + HT7** — excess + deficiency insomnia (drain
  Yangming + anchor Heart spirit).
- **ST45 + SP1** — paired-channel Well points. Both treat
  dream-disturbed sleep. Lateral 2nd toe + medial big toe.

Dream-disturbed sleep cluster (6 points, 5 channels):

| Point | Channel | Mechanism |
|-------|---------|-----------|
| **ST45** | **Stomach** | **Yangming excess → restless** |
| GB44 | Gallbladder | GB Fire → disturbed dreams |
| GB43 | Gallbladder | Shaoyang Heat → dreams |
| SP1 | Spleen | SP failing to house Yi |
| HT7 | Heart | Heart Blood empty → unanchored |
| LR2 | Liver | Liver Fire → vivid dreams |

Three of six are Well points (ST45, GB44, SP1) — suggesting
a positional link between the Well category and sleep/dream
regulation.

### Bằng chứng hiện đại

🔴 Unvalidated for ST45 individually.

The Ling Shu insomnia protocol (bleeding ST45) has NOT been
tested in any modern trial. Modern insomnia protocols use
multi-point approaches (GV20 + Yintang + HT7 + SP6 is
standard) — ST45 is not typically included.
→ Cross-ref: AP-05 HT7 insomnia (🟡 Plausible)

12 Jing-Well bleeding for emergency: class-level evidence
from multi-center RCTs for acute stroke. Individual ST45
contribution cannot be isolated.
→ Cross-ref: AP-05 HT9

Evidence hierarchy shows systematic inversion: the most
classically specified insomnia point (ST45, complete causal
chain) is the least tested, while less specified points
(GV20, Yintang) have the most protocol inclusion. Modern
protocols favor accessible, multi-purpose points over
technically demanding bloodletting.

### Dynamic acupoint properties

No sensitization data. Well points at nail corners are not
sensitization sites — too superficial and non-muscular for
connective tissue remodeling.
→ Cross-ref: C04 S4

### Cơ chế giải thích (BSA framework attribution)

**Track A systems activated at ST45:**

A01/A02 (Neural):
- Proper dorsal digital nerve (L5) → DRG L5 → ascending
  via spinothalamic tract. Terminal nerve with MAXIMUM free
  nerve ending density at the nail bed.

A07 (Segmental):
- LOCAL (toe): L5 matches. CONVERGENCE for toe pain.
- DISTANT (insomnia): L5 → sleep centers = **MAXIMUM
  SEGMENTAL MISMATCH** for a primary indication. Sleep-wake
  circuits reside in hypothalamus (VLPO/orexin), brainstem
  reticular formation, basal forebrain. L5 has NO segmental
  connection to any sleep center.

**Insomnia mechanism — two hypotheses:**

1. **RAS modulation:** nail-bed nociceptive input → A-delta/
   C fiber → spinoreticular tract → reticular activating
   system modulation. Same pathway as Well-point emergency
   arousal (12 Jing-Well bleeding). Question: can the SAME
   pathway produce bidirectional effects — arousal (coma)
   AND sedation (insomnia)? Homeostatic rebound conceivable
   but unconfirmed.

2. **Vagal-parasympathetic shift:** L5 → NTS/DMVN → vagal
   tone → parasympathetic promotion. If ST45 activates a
   vagal pathway (PROKR2-independent), the parasympathetic
   shift could counter Yangming sympathetic overdrive. This
   parallels the TCM mechanism: drain Yangming excess =
   reduce sympathetic tone.

Neither hypothesis is tested. The classical mechanism (Wei Qi
theory) provides a more COMPLETE causal chain than any modern
proposal for this specific point-indication pair.

### Kỹ thuật kích thích

Châm (B01): 1–3 mm superficial prick. Primary technique is
**Jing-Well bleeding (放血):**
→ Cross-ref: B01 S4.3

Bleeding protocol:
1. Sterilize the lateral 2nd toenail area
2. Three-edged needle (三棱针) or sterile lancet
3. Superficial prick at the nail corner
4. Squeeze 3–5 drops of blood
5. Apply pressure to stop bleeding

For insomnia: bleed ST45 before bedtime (Ling Shu protocol).
Acupressure is an alternative for patients declining
bloodletting.

Bấm huyệt (B02): firm pressure at the lateral nail corner
of the 2nd toe.

Cứu (B08): moxa at Well points for cold/deficiency patterns.
For insomnia (excess pattern), bleeding is the classical
technique, not moxa.

### An toàn

VERY LOW risk — superficial nail corner. No significant
vascular structures. No visceral access.

Infection control: nail fold can harbor bacteria. Sterile
technique mandatory for bloodletting. Avoid needling through
fungal or inflamed nail beds.

No pregnancy contraindication. No classical moxa prohibition.
